Three clarifications: not imagination, not the intensionality of knowledge, not Mary's own experiences — the missing facts are about **other people**. The accurate (1)′–(3)′ display replacing Churchland's Leibniz's-Law version; the Lewis–Nemirow ability analysis answered by the other-minds-skepticism case; no parity of reasons against dualism; no claim of proof. | source-summary | philosophy-of-mind, epistemology, metaphysics
- [[Sources/What Is It Like to Be a Bat - Thomas Nagel (1974)]] - Thomas Nagel, "What Is It Like to Be a Bat?" (*Philosophical Review*, 1974), ingested as the 2024 OUP reprint with a new preface and the 2023 lecture "Further Thoughts: The Psychophysical Nexus." Consciousness iff there is something it is like for the organism; functional analyses are compatible with its absence; the point of view is a type; physicalism is unintelligible, not refuted; objective phenomenology as next method; 2023: conceptual dualism + hoped-for monism (not property dualism). Closes the one-sided Dennett/Nagel contradiction. | source-summary | philosophy-of-mind, phenomenology, metaphysics
- [[Sources/Epiphenomenal Qualia - Frank Jackson (1982)]] - Frank Jackson, *Philosophical Quarterly* 32 (1982): 127–136. Qualia freak; knowledge argument (Fred's extra colour, then Mary) as the polemically strongest case that Physicalism is false; modal/zombie argument weaker because disputed; distinguished from Nagel (a missing *property*, not a missing point of view); epiphenomenal qualia a "perfectly possible" view (common cause, polar-bear coats, *Times*/*Telegraph*, sea-slugists). Origin of the Mary case the vault had only from Dennett and Chalmers. | source-summary | philosophy-of-mind, epistemology, metaphysics
- [[Sources/Consciousness Explained - Daniel C. Dennett (1991)]] - Daniel C. Dennett (1991): comprehensive naturalist theory of consciousness. Multiple Drafts Model replaces Cartesian Theater (no neural finish-line; parallel distributed editorial revision; Orwellian/Stalinesque distinction collapses at millisecond scales; color-phi phenomenon). Heterophenomenology as neutral method (extract texts → intentional stance → theorist's fiction; Feenomanologist analogy). Qualia deflated: colors are dispositional/ecological; Mary thought experiment relies on imagining incomplete information; inverted qualia presuppose the Theater; epiphenomenal qualia incoherent (parity argument). Self = Center of Narrative Gravity (abstraction; MPD = two narrative centers, no more mysterious than one). Joycean machine = serial virtual machine on parallel brain hardware; culturally installed via memes (Dawkins); Baldwin Effect explains how learned consciousness features become genetically entrenched. Systems Reply to Chinese Room. Chs. 1–14 + appendices. See [[Thinkers/Daniel Dennett]] + 6 concepts + [[Contradictions/Dennett vs Nagel - Multiple Drafts vs Subjective Experience]].
- [[Sources/Aristotle - Modal Logic (Organon, Patterson)]] - Richard Patterson’s analysis of Aristotle’s modal syllogistic in the *Prior Analytics* (Organon). Modal propositions via modal copula (weak vs strong necessity) grounded in essentialism (essence/accident); critique of de dicto/de re; link to scientific demonstration. Key for logic + metaphysics.
- [[Sources/Aristotle - Physics]] — *Physics* (Hardie & Gaye). Eight books: nature as internal principle of motion; four causes (canonical); chance/spontaneity; motion as incomplete actuality; potential infinite; place as boundary; void denied; time as number of motion; continuum vs Zeno; eternal unmoved first mover (kinematic, not yet *noēsis noēseōs*). Physical home of the Metaphysics cluster. | source-summary | philosophy-of-nature, metaphysics
- [[Sources/Aristotle - Metaphysics]] - The full fourteen-book treatise (W. D. Ross). Being qua being and the *pros hen* unity of being; the four causes; substance (*ousia*) as form/essence; hylomorphism; potentiality/actuality and the priority of actuality; principle of non-contradiction; the unmoved mover (thought thinking itself); sustained critique of Plato's Forms and number-metaphysics. The vault's metaphysical keystone. | source-summary | metaphysics, theology
- [[Sources/Aristotle - Nicomachean Ethics (Book I)]] - Aristotle on the highest good (*eudaimonia*) as activity of the soul in accordance with virtue (*arête*) over a complete life (*teleiotēs*). Function (*ergon*) argument; political science as architectonic; critiques of pleasure/honor/wealth lives; methodological notes on precision and audience (requires good upbringing).
- [[Sources/Aristotle - On the Soul]] - *De Anima* (J.A. Smith trans.), the full three-book treatise. Soul as first actuality of a natural body (hylomorphism applied to living things), rejecting Platonic self-moving soul and atomist/harmony theories; theory of perception as reception of sensible form without matter (proper vs. common sensibles, the medium); imagination and common sense; the active/passive intellect distinction ("alone immortal and eternal"); desire as the actual mover of animal motion. Text incomplete (breaks off mid-sentence). | source-summary | metaphysics, philosophy-of-mind, biology
- [[Sources/On the Universe (Pseudo-Aristotle)]] - **Spurious** *De Mundo* (E. S. Forster trans.), traced to Poseidonius, not genuine Aristotle. Five-element cosmology (earth/water/air/fire/ether); meteorology; unity-from-contraries; God as remote sustaining cause via graduated causal chain (king/satrap, puppeteer, chorus-leader analogies), identified with Fate/Necessity/Destiny. Conflicts with Aristotle's genuine Unmoved Mover. | source-summary | cosmology, theology
- [[Sources/Aristotle - On Sleep and Waking]] - *De Somno et Vigilia* (Beare trans., 453b). Sleep as periodic privation of waking, seated in the primary/common sense-organ (heart); physiological cause is upward evaporation of digested nutriment and its cooling reflux; final cause is conservation. | source-summary | philosophy-of-mind, physiology
- [[Sources/Aristotle - On Dreams]] - *De Insomniis* (Beare trans., 458a). Dreaming belongs to *phantasia*: a residual sensory "movement" persisting after stimulation ceases, amplified by sleep and mistaken for present perception ("like Koriskos" but not Koriskos). | source-summary | philosophy-of-mind, physiology
- [[Sources/Aristotle - On Memory and Recollection]] - *De Memoria et Reminiscentia* (Beare trans., 449b). Memory as seal-impression/picture contemplated as a likeness of the absent past; recollection as effortful, associative search (similarity/contrast/contiguity), exclusive to deliberative animals. Founds classical associationism. | source-summary | philosophy-of-mind, epistemology
- [[Sources/Aristotle - On Divination in Sleep]] - *De Divinatione per Somnum* (Beare trans., 462b). Naturalistic verdict on prophetic dreams: cause/token/coincidence taxonomy; most are coincidence; denies divine sending; rejects Democritus's images/emanations theory. | source-summary | philosophy-of-mind, philosophy-of-religion
- [[Sources/Aristotle - Nicomachean Ethics (Complete, W.D. Ross)]] - Full ten-book treatise (Ross trans.), superseding the vault's prior Book-I-only summary. Eudaimonia as activity in accordance with virtue; doctrine of the mean; voluntary action and choice; particular virtues (courage, temperance, liberality, magnanimity); justice (distributive/corrective/equity); practical wisdom; akrasia; friendship; contemplation (*theōria*) as the highest happiness. Direct prologue to the *Politics*. | source-summary | ethics, political-philosophy
- [[Sources/Aristotle - Nicomachean Ethics (Book I)]] - **Superseded** by the complete-treatise source above; retained for provenance (built from secondary structured notes, Book I only). | source-summary | ethics
- [[Sources/Aristotle (Pseudo) - Economics]] - *Oikonomikos* (Armstrong trans., 1343a), likely by a student of Aristotle or Theophrastus. Book I: household management (husband/wife, master/slave, property) as prior to statecraft, ranked modes of wealth-getting. Book II: catalogue of historical state revenue-raising stratagems (Dionysius of Syracuse, Mausolus, etc.). Book III (Latin only): virtues of husband and wife. | source-summary | political-philosophy, ethics, economics
- [[Sources/Aristotle - Politics]] - The full eight-book treatise (Jowett trans.). Man as *zōon politikon*; the polis as natural and prior to the individual; natural slavery and household management (*oikonomia* vs *chrēmatistikē*, usury condemned); critique of Plato's *Republic*/*Laws* (excess unity vs. essential plurality); sixfold classification of constitutions (true: kingship/aristocracy/polity; deviant: tyranny/oligarchy/democracy); distributive justice and rule of law; polity/middle-class stability thesis; causes of *stasis*; the best state as sized for self-sufficiency and leisure; education and music's ethical power. Unfinished (breaks off mid-sentence). Completes the Ethics→Politics arc. | source-summary | politics, ethics
- [[Sources/Aristotle - Rhetoric (Book I)]] - Rhetoric as counterpart to dialectic and systematic art. Artistic proofs (*logos*, *ethos*, *pathos*); enthymeme (rhetorical syllogism from probabilities/signs) as substance of persuasion; three branches of oratory (deliberative, forensic, epideictic); critique of emotional-manipulation manuals; integrity of judgment.
- [[Sources/Thucydides - History of the Peloponnesian War (Book III, Mytilene revolt)]] - Narrative of Lesbos/Mytilene revolt from Athens: invasion, preparations, preemptive fleet, negotiations, siege, wider operations, Mytilenaean appeal at Olympia. Themes of empire vs equal alliance, power asymmetry, strategy/diplomacy, honor, and the rhetoric of justified revolt.
- [[Sources/Classical Philosophy - Stoics, Additional Plato Dialogues, and Notes 2026]] - Collection of structured notes on additional Plato dialogues (Five Dialogues covering Euthyphro/Apology/Crito/Meno/Phaedo; Gorgias, Protagoras, Laws, Theaetetus, Parmenides, Clitophon, Ion/Hippias/Laches group, Philebus, etc.) and Stoic texts (Epictetus Enchiridion/Fragments/Golden Sayings; Seneca Letters; Marcus Aurelius biography). Themes: virtue, the good (pleasure vs intellect), teachability/recollection, dichotomy of control, examined life, law/education, rhetoric vs philosophy, power. Expands classical domain with practical Stoicism and more Platonic depth.
- [[Sources/Letters and Sayings of Epicurus - Epicurus]] - 2005 Barnes & Noble compilation (trans./intro./notes by Odysseus Makridis) of Epicurus' core texts (Letter to Herodotus on atomist physics/empiricist epistemology/cosmology with swerve for free will; Letter to Pythocles on celestial phenomena/method; Letter to Menoeceus and Principal Doctrines on ethics — ataraxia/tranquility as goal via prudent pleasure, "death is nothing," "live unnoticed"/lathe biosas, friendship; fragments on philosophy, soul, gods, society as contract). Expands Hellenistic thought with materialist empiricism and refined hedonism.
- [[Sources/On the Nature of Things - Lucretius (Books II-III)]] - **Complete** *De Rerum Natura*, all 6 books (J.S.W. scholarly translation with preface, life, notes). Book I: ex nihilo nihil fit, conservation, void, body+void only, Iphigenia anti-religion, refutation of pre-Socratics; II: swerve, secondary qualities, infinite worlds; III: mortalism (14 args), symmetry argument; IV: simulacra theory of perception, defense of senses, dreams, love critique; V: world not eternal/not for man, cosmogony, origin of civilization and language, origin of religion from fear, proto-natural selection; VI: naturalistic meteorology, proto-germ theory of disease, theory of magnetism, Plague of Athens finale. (Note: page filename retains Books II-III for continuity; frontmatter title updated.)
- [[Sources/The Enneads - Plotinus]] - Structural/page-number reference for Plotinus' *Enneads* (c. 204–270 CE, Porphyry ed., 6 Enneads × 9 treatises). Minimal numeric content in file; signals the foundational Neoplatonic text: the One (transcendent Good/source), emanation (proodos: One → Intellect/Nous → Soul → matter), return of the soul (epistrophe/henosis via purification, virtue, contemplation). Synthesis of prior classical (Plato + Aristotle + Stoic elements) into hierarchical mysticism; major influence on later thought. Expands classical domain with late-antique metaphysics of unity and ascent.
- [[Sources/The Great Chain of Being - Arthur O. Lovejoy (1936)]] - Arthur O. Lovejoy (1936): the definitive history of the "Great Chain of Being" as fusion of plenitude (all possibles realized), continuity (no gaps), and gradation (*scala naturae*); from Plato/Aristotle → Neoplatonism (emanation) → medieval (Augustine/Aquinas) → Leibniz/Spinoza/18c optimism (Pope) → temporalizing (Schelling, pre-Darwin) and "negative outcome" (hypothesis of complete rational intelligibility of the cosmos is a failure; world contingent). Initiates History of Ideas leg; root presupposition for classical/medieval cluster and Phil of Science reactions. See new [[Thinkers/Arthur O. Lovejoy]] + 4 concepts.
- [[Sources/Outlines of Pyrrhonism - Sextus Empiricus]] - Benson Mates trans. (Oxford 1996) of Sextus Empiricus' *Outlines of Pyrrhonism* (3 books). The "third philosophy": Dogmatists (Aristotle/Epicurus followers, Stoics — claim found truth), Academics (assert cannot be apprehended), Skeptics (continue to search). Equipollence (isosthenia) of opposed claims/appearances → epoché (suspension) → ataraxia (tranquility "by chance," Apelles anecdote). 10 Modes (Aenesidemus: animals incl. dog "dialectic," humans, senses, circumstances, relativity etc.), 5 Modes (Agrippa: disagreement/regress/relativity/hypothesis/circularity), slogans ("Nothing more," "to every argument an equal..."), critiques (God/cause as inconceivable or impious; material sources total disagreement incl. atoms; bodies). "Chronicler" reporting of appearances without dogma on non-evident. Direct counter to vault's Dogmatists; third Hellenistic path to ataraxia; method parallels for wiki (opposition before synthesis, epistemic humility). New entity + concepts. See dedicated source page + log.
- [[Sources/Summa Theologica, Part I (QQ. 75-102) - Thomas Aquinas]] - Dominican Province translation (2nd ed., 1932): complete Treatise on Man — QQ. 75-76 (soul's essence and hylomorphism: soul as incorruptible, subsistent form of the body; anti-Averroist; one soul per person); QQ. 77-83 (five genera of powers; five senses + four interior including estimative/cogitative; active/passive intellect; synderesis; conscience; will; free choice as intellectual appetite); QQ. 84-86 (abstraction from phantasms; three levels: natural/mathematical/metaphysical; intelligible species as means not object of knowing); QQ. 87-89 (self-knowledge by act not essence; separated soul knows by participated species); QQ. 90-93 (soul created directly by God; body from earth; woman from rib; Imago Dei in three degrees — creation/grace/glory; Augustinian Trinitarian analogy); QQ. 94-102 (Adam's knowledge by infused species; original justice as supernatural gift; immortality as supernatural gift; Tree of Life; Paradise as real corporeal place). Seven-pass ingest.
- [[Sources/Summa Theologica, Part I-II - Saint Thomas Aquinas]] - Prima Secundae (I-II, QQ. 1-114) of Aquinas' *Summa Theologica* (Gutenberg/Benziger ed.). Medieval scholastic synthesis of Aristotle + Christian theology. Treatise on the Last End (happiness as beatific vision of God in essence, imperfect in this life); human acts, passions, habits/virtues (acquired + infused); Treatise on Law (eternal, natural law as rational participation in eternal/"light of natural reason" discerning good/evil, human, divine); Treatise on Grace (necessary for supernatural end; "grace does not destroy nature but perfects it"). Quaestio method. Major extension of classical ethics (eudaimonia → beatitude; natural law grounds Aristotle). New entity + concepts. See dedicated source + log.
- [[Sources/The Neoplatonic Writings of Numenius - Guthrie (1917)]] - The extant fragments & testimonia of Numenius of Apamea (fl. c. 150), coll./trans. Kenneth Guthrie (Selene 1987, comm. omitted). Preserved via Eusebius, Calcidius, Porphyry, Proclus, Nemesius, Origen. The named Neopythagorean-Middle Platonist predecessor of Plotinus: three-god triad, matter-as-evil dualism, timeless incorporeal Being, immortal/imprisoned soul, "Plato = Moses in Attic," and a polemic against the skeptical Academy. | source-summary | metaphysics, philosophy-of-religion, history-of-philosophy
- [[Sources/Neoplatonism - R. T. Wallis (1972)]] - R. T. Wallis (Scribner's, 1972). The standard single-author survey of the whole movement: the three aims; sources (Parmenides-as-theology, Aristotle, Stoics, Moderatus, Numenius); Plotinus (Hypostases, emanation, sympathy, undescended soul, return) and the problems he bequeathed (pp. 90–93); Porphyry vs Iamblichus on theurgy; the Athenian School (henads, triadic law, Academy's closure 529); the influence chapter (Pseudo-Dionysius → falsafa → Renaissance → German Idealism). | source-summary | metaphysics, philosophy-of-religion, history-of-philosophy
- [[Sources/The Mystical Theology and The Divine Names - Dionysius the Areopagite]] - C. E. Rolt trans. (SPCK/Macmillan 1920; Dover 2004). Two complementary late-5th-c. Syrian treatises: *Divine Names* (cataphatic itinerary of scriptural titles as emanations of the Super-Essence); *Mystical Theology* (apophatic stripping into Divine Darkness / Unknowing). Preface + influence essay by W. J. Sparrow-Simpson. Primary for [[Thinkers/Pseudo-Dionysius]]. | source-summary | metaphysics, philosophy-of-religion, theology, mysticism
- [[Sources/Confessions - Augustine]] - Outler 1955 (updated Gross 2007) of Augustine's *Confessions* (13 books, c. 397–400). Sustained prayer/confession (sins + praise of God); autobiographical pilgrimage of grace (childhood, Manicheanism, skepticism, Neo-Platonism Book 7, "tolle lege" garden conversion Book 8, baptism, Monica's death); philosophical analysis of memory (Book 10: vast inner chamber), time as distentio animi (Book 11), creation/hermeneutics (12–13). Core: "restless is our heart until it comes to rest in you"; grace transforms divided will; evil as privation (pear theft); interiority. Primary patristic source for Aquinas; explicit Plotinus engagement. New entity + 3 concepts. See dedicated source + log.
- [[Sources/City of God - Augustine]] - Bettenson trans., Penguin Classics (1972). *De Civitate Dei contra Paganos* (c. 413–426 CE), 22 books. Written in response to Alaric's sack of Rome (410 CE). Against pagan religion (Books I–V) and Neoplatonism/Porphyry (Books VI–X); philosophy of history via the two cities (Books XI–XXII). Core: peace as *tranquillitas ordinis*; Christ as the universal way (vs. Porphyry's failed search); supreme good = peace in life everlasting; bodily resurrection; eternal Sabbath. 3 new concepts: [[Concepts/Two Cities (Augustine)]], [[Concepts/Tranquillitas Ordinis - Peace as Order (Augustine)]], [[Concepts/Universal Way of Salvation (Augustine)]]. Major Augustine entity expansion. See dedicated source + log.
- [[Sources/Epictetus - Enchiridion, Fragments, and Golden Sayings]] - RAG-formatted texts: *Enchiridion* (P. E. Matheson, 53 sections), *Fragments* (Crossley), *Golden Sayings* (Crossley, 86 sayings). Core Epictetan discipline: the foundational division between what is in our power (*eph' hēmin*, prohairesis) and what is not; impression test; role/duty; the philosopher as singer of the divine hymn; *"Men are disturbed not by things but by opinions about things."* Primary source for [[Concepts/Dichotomy of Control (Epictetus)]].
- [[Sources/The Works of Epictetus - Discourses, Enchiridion, and Fragments (Carter-Higginson)]] - The complete extant Epictetus (Carter/Higginson trans.); the vault's ingest of the full **Discourses (Four Books)** recorded by Arrian. Supplies the system behind the *Enchiridion*: *prohairesis* as the sole good, the discipline of assent, preconceptions, the three fields of training, kinship with God, the Cynic ideal (III.22), and freedom (IV.1). | source-summary | ethics, epistemology, philosophy-of-religion
- [[Sources/Seneca - Letters from a Stoic]] - Gummere trans. (1917–1925), all 124 *Epistulae Morales*. Time as our only possession (Letter I); friendship; death rehearsal; crowds as moral contagion (Letter VII); master and slave (XLVII); quality vs. length of life (XCIII); Cleanthes' hymn (CVII); true good through reason alone (CXXIV). Governing metaphor: philosophy as medicine for the soul. New concept: [[Concepts/Philosophy as Medicine (Seneca)]].
- [[Sources/The Obstacle Is the Way - Ryan Holiday (2014)]] - Ryan Holiday's modern popularization of Roman Stoicism as an "operating system" for adversity, built on Marcus Aurelius's *Meditations* 5.20 and Hadot's three disciplines (Perception, Action, Will). ~50 historical vignettes rather than textual exegesis. Source for [[Concepts/Amor Fati (Love of Fate)]], [[Concepts/Premeditatio Malorum (Premeditation of Evils)]], [[Concepts/Memento Mori (Stoic Practice)]], [[Concepts/The Inner Citadel (Hadot)]], [[Concepts/The Three Disciplines - Perception, Action, and Will (Hadot)]], [[Concepts/Turning Obstacles Upside Down (Holiday)]]. Reception/application, not primary doctrine. | source-summary | ethics
- [[Sources/Philosophy as a Way of Life - Pierre Hadot (1995)]] - Pierre Hadot, *Philosophy as a Way of Life: Spiritual Exercises from Socrates to Foucault* (Blackwell 1995; Davidson ed., Chase trans.). The missing primary Holiday imported. Ancient *philosophia* as a way of life; spiritual exercises; discourse vs philosophy; creative mistakes; Socrates as figure; Marcus's three *topoi*; critique of Foucault's care of the self. | source-summary | ethics, metaphilosophy, ancient-philosophy
- [[Sources/Marcus Aurelius - Biography (Lord)]] - John Lord's 1883 essay from *Beacon Lights of History* Vol. IV. Philosopher-king thesis: Marcus as unique historical fulfillment of Plato's ideal. *Meditations* as private self-correction (not a treatise). Decisive formation: Rusticus → Epictetus. *"The best of the pagan world."* Commodus as successor; Lord's Christian historical frame.
- [[Sources/Meditations - Marcus Aurelius]] - George Long trans. (1862) from the Stoic Six Pack compilation (full 12 books of the emperor's private journal *Ta eis heauton*, written on the Danubian campaigns). Core disciplines: catalogue of debts and gratitude (Book 1); ruling faculty / daemon as inner citadel; morning reflection on meeting others as kin; "we are made for co-operation"; "do every act as if it were the last"; view from above (earth a point, fame empty); universe as transformation; live with the gods by following the assigned portion and the daemon within; cheerful acceptance of death as natural. Primary source for Marcus' own voice and the emperor's application of Stoicism under power. Complements the bio and completes the Roman Stoic triad. See dedicated source + [[Thinkers/Marcus Aurelius]] + log.
- [[Sources/The Guide for the Perplexed - Maimonides]] - Maimonides' *Moreh Nevukhim* (c. 1190, Friedländer tr.), the supreme work of medieval Jewish rationalism reconciling the Bible with Aristotle. Negative theology + attributes of action; rejection of the Kalam atomism/occasionalism (mirror image of Ghazali); creation undemonstrable but accepted on prophecy + design; naturalized prophecy; evil as privation; providence graded by intellect (Job as parable); rational reasons for the commandments (Sabean anti-idolatry). Supplies the missing Maimonides thinker page + 6 concepts; third corner of the Ghazali–Averroes triangle; conduit to Aquinas. | source-summary | metaphysics, theology, ethics
- [[Sources/The Incoherence of the Philosophers - Al-Ghazali]] - Al-Ghazali's *Tahafut al-Falasifa* (c. 1095), the primary text behind the already-ingested Averroes rebuttal. Refutes 20 doctrines of the Islamic Aristotelians (chiefly Avicenna); 3 condemned as unbelief (world's eternity, God's ignorance of particulars, denial of bodily resurrection). Supplies the missing al-Ghazali thinker page + 4 concepts (criterion of demonstrability, eternal will/temporal creation, God's knowledge of particulars, bodily resurrection/immaterial soul) + the Temporal-Creation-vs-Eternal-World contradiction. Famous Seventeenth Discussion: occasionalism (fire-and-cotton). | source-summary | metaphysics, theology, epistemology
- [[Sources/Incoherence of the Incoherence - Ibn Rushd]] - Averroes (Ibn Rushd)'s *Tahafut al-Tahafut* (The Incoherence of the Incoherence), point-by-point defense of Aristotelian philosophy (eternity of the world, real secondary causation and natures vs. occasionalism, soul/intellect nuances) against Ghazali’s *Tahafut al-Falasifa*. Long editor intro on Greek-to-Arabic transmission, Mu‘tazilites/Ash‘arites, Ghazali vs. *falasifa*. Grounds Aquinas’ anti-Averroist arguments (esp. unicity of intellect, soul as form of *this* body). New entity + concepts on eternity and causation. Critical Islamic link in classical → scholastic chain. See dedicated source page + log.
- [[Sources/Discourse on the Method - Descartes (1637)]] - René Descartes, *Discourse on the Method* (1637; Duke Classics public-domain trans.). Six Parts: critique of school learning; the four precepts of the method; the provisional morality; the metaphysics (hyperbolic doubt → *cogito ergo sum* → God-proofs → mind-body distinction); mechanistic physics/physiology (the blood, the body-machine, the two tests and the *bête-machine*); the mastery-of-nature program and why publish. The earlier, French, autobiographical companion to the *Meditations* — the *cogito*'s first publication. Bridges the early-modern and AI/Cognitive-Science legs (the two tests as ancestor of the Turing Test). New source + 3 concepts. See dedicated source page + log.
- [[Sources/Meditations on First Philosophy - Descartes (Oxford, 2008)]] - René Descartes, *Meditations on First Philosophy* (1641/42; Oxford World's Classics, Michael Moriarty trans. 2008) with selections from the Objections and Replies. Six Meditations (hyperbolic doubt, cogito, causal + ontological proofs of God, truth/error and free will, essence of material things + ontological reinforcement, existence of bodies + real distinction mind/body with union) + objectors (Caterus, Mersenne circle, Hobbes, Arnauld, Gassendi, group, Bourdin) and Descartes's replies. Pivotal early modern rationalist foundational text; hinge from classical cluster (Plato/Aristotle/Augustine/Aquinas/Sextus/Plotinus/Stoics) into modern epistemology, dualism, and certainty. New entity + multiple concepts. See dedicated source page + log.
- [[Sources/Ethics - Spinoza (Cambridge, Kisner ed. 2018)]] - Benedict de Spinoza, *Ethics Proved in Geometrical Order* (Cambridge Texts in the History of Philosophy, Matthew J. Kisner ed., Michael Silverthorne & Kisner trans. 2018). Five parts (God/substance monism, Nature and Origin of the Mind/parallelism, Origin and Nature of the Affects/conatus, Human Servitude, Power of the Intellect/Freedom and blessedness) demonstrated more geometrico. Profound monist naturalist counterpoint and synthesis to Descartes + classical cluster; exceptional model for vault method (geometrical deduction from essences), architecture (parallelism of sources and representations), persistence (conatus), and highest good (intellectual love of the whole). New entity + 6 concepts. See dedicated source page + log.
- [[Sources/An Essay Concerning Human Understanding - Locke]] - John Locke, *An Essay Concerning Human Understanding* (1689/1690; 2nd ed. text from Delphi Classics *Complete Works of John Locke*, 2017) + other major works in the volume (Toleration letters, Two Treatises of Government, Thoughts on Education, Reasonableness of Christianity + vindications, Conduct of the Understanding). Full 4 Books of the Essay: Book I (anti-innatism, tabula rasa/empty cabinet); Book II (two fountains sensation+reflection; simple ideas; primary real/resemblant vs secondary powers; complex ideas/modes/substances/relations; identity by existence/organization/consciousness; association by custom as error source); Book III (words as voluntary signs of ideas in speaker's mind; general terms from sensible/abstract; arbitrary signification); Book IV (knowledge as perception of agreement/disagreement of ideas in 4 sorts with intuitive/demonstrative/sensitive degrees; self/God/other existence; faith/reason, enthusiasm critique). Summaries of other works: toleration (inward persuasion, civil interests only); government (consent, property by labor, revolution); education (habits, virtue first); Christianity (simple Messiah faith); conduct of understanding (practical improvement). New/expanded entity + 11+ concepts. See dedicated source page + [[Thinkers/Locke]] + log.
- [[Sources/An Enquiry concerning Human Understanding - Hume (Oxford, Millican ed.)]] - David Hume, *An Enquiry concerning Human Understanding* (1748; Oxford World's Classics, Peter Millican ed. 2007) + appendices (1740 *Abstract*, immortality essay, *Dialogues* excerpts, letters, "My Own Life"). Twelve sections: impressions/ideas + Copy Principle (II); association (III); Hume's Fork + the problem of induction (IV); the "sceptical solution" — custom and belief as feeling (V); probability (VI); necessary connexion + the two definitions of cause (VII); compatibilism / liberty and necessity (VIII); animal reason (IX); Of Miracles (X); critique of the design argument / providence (XI); academical or mitigated scepticism (XII, "commit it to the flames"). The empiricist capstone completing the Early Modern triad (Descartes → Spinoza → Hume); deepest vault source on the limits of reason, induction, causation, testimony, and free will. Roused Kant from his "dogmatic slumber." New entity + 8 concepts. See dedicated source page + log.
- [[Sources/Metaphysics of Morals - Immanuel Kant]] - Immanuel Kant, *Groundwork for the Metaphysics of Morals* (1785; Yale "Rethinking the Western Tradition" ed. with Editor's Preface/Note by Allen W. Wood + essays by Schneewind/Baron/Kagan/Wood). Kant's foundational text seeking the supreme principle of morality in pure a priori reason (note: volume centers on 1785 *Groundwork*, preparatory to 1797 *Metaphysics of Morals*). Full: Editor's apparatus + Preface (pure a priori metaphysics of morals, analytic/synthetic method, 3 sections); First (good will/"jewel", duty from respect, 3 props, CI universal law + false promise, common reason has it); Second (popular critique; hyp vs cat; CI system: universal law + Humanity as end + Kingdom of Ends; autonomy supreme vs heteronomy of all prior); Third (freedom key to autonomy, two standpoints/sensible vs intelligible/"as if", incomprehensibility of pure practical reason). + summaries of 4 essays (Schneewind historical/autonomy revolution; Baron duty defense; Kagan for consequentialists; Wood 3 features + community value). New entity + 9 concepts total. See dedicated source page + [[Thinkers/Kant]] + log. **Companion edition:** [[Sources/Groundwork of the Metaphysics of Morals - Immanuel Kant (Gregor, Cambridge 1998)]].
- [[Sources/Groundwork of the Metaphysics of Morals - Immanuel Kant (Gregor, Cambridge 1998)]] - Immanuel Kant, *Groundwork of the Metaphysics of Morals* (1785; Mary Gregor trans./ed., Christine M. Korsgaard introduction; Cambridge Texts in the History of Philosophy, 1998). Standard English + Academy 4:387–463. Second vault edition of the same book as the Wood/Yale file (not the 1797 *MdS*). Korsgaard road map: one continuous argument; §§I–II analytic, §III deduction; reluctant-duty myth rejected; dignity = legislative membership. New: Dignity of Rational Nature; Perfect and Imperfect Duties. See [[Thinkers/Kant]] + log.
- [[Sources/Critique of Pure Reason - Immanuel Kant (Guyer Wood, Cambridge)]] - Immanuel Kant, *Critique of Pure Reason* (A 1781 / B 1787; Paul Guyer and Allen W. Wood trans./ed., Cambridge Edition, 1998). How are synthetic a priori judgments possible? Copernican turn; space/time as forms of intuition; twelve categories; deduction via unity of apperception; schematism and Analogies; phenomena/noumena; Dialectic (Paralogisms, Antinomies, Ideal); regulative ideas; Canon (three questions; moral faith). First primary theoretical Kant. See [[Thinkers/Kant]] + log.
- [[Sources/On Truth and Lies in a Nonmoral Sense - Nietzsche (Breazeale, Collection 2013)]] - Friedrich Nietzsche, *On Truth and Lies in a Nonmoral Sense* (written 1873, unpublished; Breazeale, from the 2013 22-work collection). Truth as a movable host of worn-out metaphors; concept = equation of unequals; metaphor-drive in myth and art; rational vs intuitive man. Not Kaufmann’s “mobile army.” Collection stays in `raw/`. | source-summary | epistemology, aesthetics
- [[Sources/The Birth of Tragedy - Nietzsche (Kaufmann, Collection 2013)]] - Friedrich Nietzsche, *The Birth of Tragedy* (1872; Kaufmann, from the 2013 22-work collection). First book. Apollinian–Dionysian duality; tragedy from the chorus/music; aesthetic justification of existence; Socrates as theoretical man; Wagner as hoped German rebirth. **Not** the *Ecce Homo* chapter of the same name. No 1886 self-criticism preface in this extract. Collection stays in `raw/`. | source-summary | aesthetics, metaphysics
- [[Sources/Human, All Too Human - Nietzsche (Zimmern, Collection excerpt)]] - Friedrich Nietzsche, *Human, All Too Human: A Book for Free Spirits* (1878; 1886 Preface; Helen Zimmern / Levy 1909). Collection excerpt = Preface + Vol. I chs. I–III only (§§1–144). Chemistry of feelings; no eternal facts; two-fold origin of good and bad; saint as misread type. 1886 Preface is a second voice. | source-summary | ethics, epistemology, philosophy-of-religion
- [[Sources/The Gay Science - Nietzsche (Kaufmann 1974)]] - Friedrich Nietzsche, *The Gay Science* (1882; 2nd ed. 1887; Kaufmann Vintage 1974). Last free-spirit book and the primary for death of God (§§108, 125, 343), *amor fati* (§276), eternal recurrence as thought-experiment (§341), intellectual conscience (§2), the great health (§382). Title = *fröhliche Wissenschaft* / *gaya scienza*, not joyful wisdom. Book V is 1887, after *Zarathustra* and BGE. | source-summary | ethics, epistemology, metaphysics
- [[Sources/Thus Spoke Zarathustra - Nietzsche (Common 1909)]] - Friedrich Nietzsche, *Also sprach Zarathustra* (1883–85; Thomas Common 1909, extracted from the 2013 22-work collection). Down-going; Superman (*Übermensch*) vs last man; three metamorphoses; Will to Power named as Life's self-surpassing (before BGE); redemption of the "It was"; eternal return as abysmal thought (dwarf / Moment / animals / bite); higher men who are not the Superman. Common's "Superman" is a translation. *Amor fati* is not in this book. | source-summary | ethics, metaphysics, aesthetics
- [[Sources/Beyond Good and Evil - Nietzsche (AmazonClassics Edition)]] - Friedrich Nietzsche, *Beyond Good and Evil: Prelude to a Philosophy of the Future* (1886; Helen Zimmern; AmazonClassics + same text in the 2013 collection). **Complete 2026-08-12** (June 4 was Preface + Ch. I §§1–9 only). Will to power defined (§13 discharge; §259 organic); free spirits as heralds not legislators (§44); soul as social structure (§§12, 19); master/slave first stated as typology (§260); pathos of distance and order of rank. *Genealogy* amplifies §260. No aftersong in either Zimmern file. | source-summary | ethics, epistemology, metaphysics
- [[Sources/On the Genealogy of Morals - Nietzsche (Johnston)]] - Friedrich Nietzsche, *On the Genealogy of Morals* (1887; Ian Johnston, extracted from the 2013 22-work collection). Second English primary. Collection title page unnamed; wording is Johnston ("knowledgeable people," "PROLOGUE," gray, English *resentment*). Fills the 2026-06-04 Essay III hole: science as newest form of the ascetic ideal, not its enemy. New: [[Concepts/Genealogy (Nietzsche)]]. Collection stays in `raw/`. | source-summary | ethics, methodology
- [[Sources/The Genealogy of Morals - Nietzsche (1887)]] - Friedrich Nietzsche, *On the Genealogy of Morals* (1887; Internet Archive edition). The analytic "polemic" that amplifies and completes the revaluation project sketched in BGE (esp. Aph. 260). Preface (personal origins of the problem of the value of morality, response to Rée's "English" utilitarian psychology, call for critique of moral *values* and "grey" authentic history, "rumination" as reading art). First Essay: master morality (good/bad from pathos of distance and aristocratic self-affirmation) vs. slave morality (good/evil from ressentiment of the weak/priestly; the Jewish/Christian "revolt of the slaves in morals," "evil enemy" construction, Rome vs. Judaea with Judaea victorious). Second Essay: sovereign individual (the autonomous "super-moral" being competent to promise, with proud conscience as the memory of the will); bad conscience as internalization when instincts of freedom are caged by society/peace (the "illness" that is also "pregnancy" of soul/idealism/beauty); "ought" from "owe" (creditor-ower); punishment's many meanings (not primarily to create remorse). Third Essay: "What is the meaning of ascetic ideals?" (nothing or too much for artists; flair for intellect in philosophers; best engine of power for priests; "horror vacui" — man needs a goal and will sooner will nothingness than not will). New source + 5 concepts (Ressentiment, Master Morality and Slave Morality, Bad Conscience, Ascetic Ideal, Sovereign Individual). See dedicated source page + [[Thinkers/Nietzsche]] + log. The genealogical method and full moral-historical account now primary-sourced for the vault.
- [[Sources/The Case of Wagner - Nietzsche (1888)]] - Friedrich Nietzsche, *The Case of Wagner: A Musician’s Problem* (1888; Ludovici/Levy line from the 2013 22-work collection). Short polemic: decadence as the master problem of which good and evil is a subdivision; Nietzsche a decadent who recognised it; Wagner = modernity concentrated / Cagliostro; *Parsifal* as masterpiece of seduction; actor vs musician; three requisitions against theatrocracy. Not the *Ecce Homo* chapter of the same name. Collection stays in `raw/`. | source-summary | aesthetics, ethics
- [[Sources/Nietzsche Contra Wagner - Nietzsche (Ludovici, Collection 2013)]] - Friedrich Nietzsche, *Nietzsche contra Wagner* (assembled Christmas 1888; Ludovici/Levy). Dossier of earlier pages, not a second pamphlet. Antipodes; aesthetic = applied physiology; 1876 Bayreuth farewell; *amor fati* as core (1887 Preface selected). Levy 1878 appendix is not the book. | source-summary | aesthetics, ethics
- [[Sources/Twilight of the Idols - Nietzsche (Kaufmann)]] - Friedrich Nietzsche, *Götzen-Dämmerung* (written 1888; pub. 1889; Kaufmann *Portable Nietzsche* text from the 2013 22-work collection). Recreation from the Revaluation that is also a declaration of war; eternal idols sounded with a hammer as a tuning fork. Problem of Socrates; six-stage True World fable (stage 6 abolishes the apparent world too); morality as anti-nature / castratism; four great errors; last disciple of Dionysus / teacher of the eternal recurrence. Maxim 8 is *destroy* + school of war, not Marcus 5.20. | source-summary | ethics, metaphysics, epistemology
- [[Sources/The Will to Power - Nietzsche (Kaufmann, Collection 2013)]] - Friedrich Nietzsche, *The Will to Power* (notebooks 1883–1888; Kaufmann/Hollingdale numbering from the 2013 22-work collection). **Not a published book.** Excerpt: Preface + Book I European Nihilism (notes 1–134) + Book III.I Will to Power as Knowledge (466–617). Books II & IV and n.1067 missing. Highest values devaluate themselves; no facts, only interpretations. | source-summary | ethics, metaphysics, epistemology
- [[Sources/The Antichrist - Nietzsche (Mencken, Collection 2013)]] - Friedrich Nietzsche, *Der Antichrist* (written 1888; pub. 1895; H. L. Mencken 1920, from the 2013 22-work collection). Book I of the Revaluation. §2 table of values; pity as technic of nihilism; Redeemer as physiological type vs Paul the dysangelist; condemnation dated from today. Mencken: *transvaluation*, *botched*. No *Gesetz wider das Christenthum* in this file. Collection stays in `raw/`. | source-summary | ethics, philosophy-of-religion

*(All non-empty substantial .docx/PDFs ingested as dropped in the 2026-06-04 batch. 7 0B Gemini/NotebookLM/Google placeholders re-confirmed empty (titles only; see updated placeholder page and daily AI tips for freshest Gemini details). CPCU and MasterClass represent major new domains: professional insurance education + broad craft/leadership/personal mastery library. MasterClass handled via one collection synthesis page + targeted entity/concept updates for manageability and compounding value. Additional classical sources (Aristotle Logic/Ethics/Rhetoric + Thucydides history + this Stoics/Plato notes collection + Epicurus + Plotinus Enneads reference + this Pyrrhonism full text + this Aquinas Summa I-II + this Confessions - Augustine + this 2026-06-04 Incoherence of the Incoherence - Ibn Rushd) ingested as dedicated summaries + entity/concept expansion (now including patristics: restless heart/rest in God, memory as interior path, time as distentio, grace transforming the will, evil as privation; primary source grounding Aquinas medieval synthesis; Ibn Rushd as the key Islamic Aristotelian defense grounding Aquinas’ anti-Averroist arguments on soul/intellect and the occasionalism vs. natural causation debate presupposed by natural law). 2026-06-04 Descartes *Meditations* (Oxford 2008) added as dedicated source summary + entity + 6+ concepts (hyperbolic doubt, cogito, clear & distinct, res cogitans/extensa + union, ontological + causal God proofs); extensive cross-entity/concept updates across the classical cluster (skepticism/certainty contrasts, God proofs, dualism vs. hylomorphism, interiority, eudaimonia/ataraxia, method). 2026-06-04 Spinoza *Ethics* (Cambridge Kisner ed.) added as dedicated source summary + entity + 6 concepts (Deus sive Natura monism, attributes/parallelism, conatus, affects active vs passive, geometrical method, freedom/blessedness/intellectual love of God); major cross-updates and contrasts to Descartes (dualism → monism/parallelism), Aristotle, Aquinas, Augustine, Plotinus, Stoics, Sextus, and related concepts. Profound monist naturalist counterpoint and synthesis to the entire prior cluster; exceptional model for vault method (geometrical deduction), architecture (parallelism of sources and representations), persistence (conatus), and highest good (understanding the whole). See log for full batch + follow-up details.)*

- [[Sources/The Federalist Papers - Publius (1787-1788)]] - Alexander Hamilton, James Madison, and John Jay writing as "Publius," *The Federalist Papers* (1787–1788; 85 essays originally in NY newspapers, collected 1788). The canonical contemporary defense and exposition of the U.S. Constitution. Nos. 1–14: utility of Union vs. dangers of disunion/separate confederacies. No. 10 (Madison): factions — definition, causes (human nature, property, opinions), control via republic (representation refines views; large extent + variety of interests makes majority faction less likely or harder to concert). Nos. 15–22: insufficiency of the Articles of Confederation ("imbecility"). Nos. 23+: necessity of energetic government with powers adequate to objects. No. 51 (Madison): separation of powers and checks ("ambition must be made to counteract ambition"); double security of the compound republic (power divided between federal/state governments, then each subdivided among departments); multiplicity of interests/sects protects minorities. Nos. 52–66: legislative (House biennial, Senate stability + state equality). Nos. 67–77: executive (energy + responsibility via elections, fixed salary, impeachment, veto). No. 78 (Hamilton): judiciary as weakest branch (judgment, not will or force); good-behavior tenure and judicial review essential for limited Constitution (courts declare acts contrary to "manifest tenor" void); bulwark against legislative encroachments and majority "ill humors." Nos. 84–85: Constitution itself as bill of rights via structure; responses to objections. New entity + 4 concepts. See dedicated source page + [[Thinkers/Publius (Hamilton, Madison, and Jay)]] + log. Foundational primary source for constitutional republican design, institutional checks, and compound federalism — directly applicable to vault/AI governance and checked agentic systems.

- [[Sources/The Varieties of Religious Experience - William James (1902)]] - William James, *The Varieties of Religious Experience: A Study in Human Nature* (Gifford Lectures on Natural Religion, Edinburgh 1901–02; Longmans 1902/1905). Personal religion from documents; fruits not roots; healthy-minded vs sick soul; conversion via the subliminal door; four marks of mysticism; piecemeal supernaturalism. Opens the religion-as-philosophy cluster from a thinker already present. | source-summary | philosophy-of-religion, psychology, pragmatism
- [[Sources/Pragmatism - William James (1907, Standard Ebooks)]] - William James (1842–1910), *Pragmatism: A New Name for Some Old Ways of Thinking* (1907; eight Lowell/Columbia lectures, Standard Ebooks ed.). The classic statement of American pragmatism, generalizing Peirce's principle into (1) a **method** — settle disputes by tracing practical consequences ("no difference anywhere that doesn't make a difference elsewhere"; theories as "instruments, not answers… in which we can rest"; the squirrel anecdote; Ostwald) — and (2) a **genetic theory of truth** — "truth *happens* to an idea… is *made* true by events"/verification; "the 'true' is only the expedient in the way of our thinking"; truth lives "on a credit system" and is *in rebus* like health/wealth/strength. Lectures: I tender-minded vs. tough-minded temperaments + the modern dilemma (facts vs. religion); II the pragmatic method + Peirce + first truth-statement ("corridor in a hotel"); III metaphysics pragmatically (substance/Eucharist; materialism vs. theism as retrospectively idle but prospectively a difference of *promise*; "design is a blank cartridge"; free will as "a doctrine of relief"); IV the one and the many (oneness/manyness "absolutely coordinate" → pluralism; Vivekananda's Vedanta monism as mystical; "Absolute" → "Ultimate," oneness at the *end*); V common sense as inherited *denkmittel* (three stages: common sense/science/critical philosophy, none "absolutely more true"); VI the truth theory in full (agreement = fruitful *leading*, not copying; verification; rebuts the "whatever is pleasant" slander); VII Schiller's **humanism** ("the world is malleable… man engenders truths upon it"; "we carve the statue"; "the *that* is its own, the *what* depends on the *which*… on us"; constellations/27/audience; rationalism's "edition de luxe, eternally complete" vs. pragmatism's "one edition, unfinished, growing"); VIII **meliorism** (salvation as a *grounded possibility* between optimism and pessimism; "possible chicken means actual egg"; the world's-author wager; real losses accepted) + pluralistic theism (God as "*primus inter pares*"). Forerunners James claims: Socrates, Aristotle, Locke, Berkeley, Hume. New entity + 6 concepts; the vault's first American pragmatist and its third path on truth (alongside classical adequation and rationalist correspondence). See dedicated source page + [[Thinkers/William James]] + log.

- [[Sources/A History of Western Philosophy - Bertrand Russell (1945)]] - Bertrand Russell (1872–1970), *A History of Western Philosophy, and Its Connection with Political and Social Circumstances* (1945; the book behind his 1950 Nobel). An **opinionated single-author survey** of the whole tradition (Thales → logical analysis), ingested as a *meta-layer/verdict-layer* over nearly every vault thinker — **not** a primary text. Three Books (Ancient; Catholic; Modern), ~80 chapters. Russell's program: philosophy is "both an effect and a cause" of social/political circumstances (the **social-circumstances method**); philosophy is the **"No Man's Land" between theology and science** (reasoned speculation where definite knowledge is unavailable; its gift is "to live without certainty, yet without being paralyzed"); the master political dialectic is **social cohesion vs. individual liberty** (disciplinarians [dogma, "nobility," irrationalist, anti-science] vs. libertarians [scientific, utilitarian, rationalist], with **liberalism** as the escape from "endless oscillation"); his own positive school is the **philosophy of logical analysis** (piecemeal, scientific, "successive approximations — improvement not rejection"; **Occam's razor**; the **"cosmic impiety"** warning against making truth depend on us). Famous **verdicts**: Spinoza "the noblest and most lovable of the great philosophers… ethically supreme"; Aquinas "little of the true philosophic spirit… special pleading"; Plato "hardly ever intellectually honest"; Aristotle's authority "a serious obstacle to progress"; Hume "developed empiricism to its logical conclusion and… made it incredible… a dead end"; Kant "a pre-Humian type of rationalism" who never answered Hume; "I dislike Nietzsche…"; and the realist critique ("cosmic impiety") of James's pragmatic theory of truth (while praising James's radical empiricism). Also surveys thinkers not yet in the vault (Presocratics, Machiavelli, Hobbes, Leibniz, Rousseau, Hegel, Schopenhauer, Marx, Bergson, Dewey, the Utilitarians). **Caution**: partisan (atheist/liberal/logical-empiricist, 1945) and dated in places — read verdicts as *Russell's*. Companion not-yet-ingested raw file: *The Problems of Philosophy* (1912). New entity + 3 concepts + cross-updates to Spinoza/Aquinas/Plato/Nietzsche/Hume/William James/Pragmatic Theory of Truth. See dedicated source page + [[Thinkers/Bertrand Russell]] + log.

- [[Sources/The Problems of Philosophy - Bertrand Russell (1912)]] - Bertrand Russell, *The Problems of Philosophy* (1912; Home University Library; acks Moore + Keynes). Russell's classic short **primary** epistemology — the companion to his 1945 survey (same [[Thinkers/Bertrand Russell]] entity). 15 chapters: appearance/reality and **sense-data vs. physical objects** (the table; structural realism — we know matter's *relations* not its intrinsic nature; "seeing the sun of eight minutes ago"; idealism refuted by the act/object distinction against Berkeley's *esse est percipi*); **knowledge by acquaintance vs. by description** (acquaintance = direct, inference-free awareness of sense-data, memory, introspection, probably the Self, and universals; description = "the so-and-so," knowing things we are *not* acquainted with; "every proposition we can understand must be composed wholly of constituents with which we are acquainted"; description "enables us to pass beyond the limits of our private experience"); **induction** (restates [[Concepts/Problem of Induction (Hume)|Hume]] with the **chicken**; the inductive principle unprovable by experience, accepted as a priori); **a priori knowledge** (rationalists right on logic, empiricists right that "nothing can be known to exist except by experience"; math a priori); realism about **universals** (qualified Platonism; relations *subsist*); the **correspondence theory of truth** ("minds do not *create* truth… what makes a belief true is a *fact*"; coherence rejected as *definition*, kept as *test*; the Othello/Desdemona multiple-relation theory); and **the value of philosophy** ("studied not for definite answers but for the questions"; enlargement of the Self via the not-Self; the free intellect; explicit rejection of "Man is the measure… truth is man-made"). Notably the 1912 anti-pragmatism prefigures the 1945 "cosmic impiety." 4 new concepts; cross-updates to the Russell entity, [[Concepts/Problem of Induction (Hume)]], [[Concepts/Method of Hyperbolic Doubt (Descartes)]], [[Concepts/Pragmatic Theory of Truth (James)]]. See dedicated source page + [[Thinkers/Bertrand Russell]] + log.

- [[Sources/The Art of War - Sun Tzu (Giles trans., 1910)]] - **Sun Tzu** (Sun Wu, fl. c. 544–496 BCE), *The Art of War* (Lionel Giles trans., 1910, with classical Chinese commentary; etext by Bob Sutton). "The oldest military treatise in the world" and the vault's **first work of strategy/statecraft + first non-Western classic**. A *primary but practical* manual — 13 aphoristic chapters. Core doctrine: war is grave and decided by prior **calculation** of the five factors (Moral Law/*tao*, Heaven, Earth, Commander, Method) and seven considerations; **"supreme excellence consists in breaking the enemy's resistance without fighting"** (take whole/intact; attack his *plans*; siege worst); **"if you know the enemy and know yourself, you need not fear a hundred battles"**; **"all warfare is based on deception"** (control the enemy's perception); direct/indirect, **momentum (*shih*)**, and water-like **formlessness** ("avoid what is strong, strike what is weak"; "no constant tactics"; wind/forest/fire/mountain); the five dangerous faults of a general; reading the signs of the enemy; the nine kinds of ground; restraint ("no ruler should put troops into the field merely to gratify his own spleen"); and **foreknowledge** from human intelligence (the five classes of spies; "cannot be obtained inductively from experience… only from other men" — a twist convergent with Hume/Russell). Concubine anecdote (delegated command, discipline). New entity + 6 concepts; cross-updates to Thucydides (entity + Power/Empire/Revolt — the Western descriptive-realist counterpart) and Kasparov (chess as Sun Tzu's laboratory). Strong transfer to career/competition, negotiation, AI/agentic planning, game theory; ethics/just-war tension with Augustine/Kant flagged. **Caution**: a Victorian paraphrase-heavy etext; read for transferable strategic logic, not endorsement of means. See dedicated source page + [[Thinkers/Sun Tzu]] + log.

**German Idealism (Hegel Ingest, 2026-06-11)**:
- [[Sources/Phenomenology of Spirit - Hegel (1807)]] - G.W.F. Hegel, *The Phenomenology of Spirit* (Baillie trans.); developmental biography of Spirit (*Geist*) through successive stages of cognitive and historical alienation, self-contradiction, and resolution, culminating in Absolute Knowing.
- [[Sources/The Logic of Hegel - Hegel (1812)]] - G.W.F. Hegel, *The Logic of Hegel* (Wallace trans.); also known as the *Lesser Logic*, detailing the systematic development of pure conceptual thought and the identity of logic and metaphysics.
- [[Sources/Philosophy of History - Hegel (1837)]] - G.W.F. Hegel, *Lectures on the Philosophy of History* (Sibree trans.); teleological view of world history as the progressive unfolding of Spirit's consciousness of freedom realized in the constitutional state.
- [[Sources/Philosophy of Mind - Hegel (1894)]] - G.W.F. Hegel, *Hegel's Philosophy of Mind* (Wallace trans.); the culmination of the Encyclopaedia system, tracing the development of Spirit from individual psychology (Subjective) through social institutions (Objective) to absolute self-knowledge in art, religion, and philosophy.

**Modern, Political, and Rationalist Ingest (2026-06-11)**:
- [[Sources/Leviathan - Thomas Hobbes (1651)]] - Thomas Hobbes, *Leviathan* (1651). Establishes modern social contract theory, arguing that individuals in a brutal State of Nature contract to surrender their rights to an absolute sovereign to secure peace and physical survival.
- [[Sources/Lore and Science in Ancient Pythagoreanism - Walter Burkert (1972)]] - Walter Burkert (1972; Ger. *Weisheit und Wissenschaft* 1962). The definitive source-critical study that dismantles "Pythagoras the mathematician-scientist": reading the earliest evidence strictly, Pythagoras is a religious charismatic (metempsychosis + ritual *acusmata*), while the number-cosmology belongs to [[Thinkers/Philolaus]] and Plato's Academy. Opens [[Contradictions/Pythagoras the Scientist vs the Religious Charismatic]]; creates [[Thinkers/Philolaus]] + [[Schools/Pythagoreanism]] + 3 concepts. | source-summary | history-of-philosophy, history-of-science
- [[Sources/A History of Pythagoreanism - Carl A. Huffman (ed.) (2014)]] - Carl A. Huffman, ed. (Cambridge, 2014). The authoritative 21-chapter synthesis adjudicating the Burkert↔Zhmud debate by *disaggregating* "Pythagoreanism" into strands (society, *bios*, religion, mathematics, harmonics) and tracing its millennium-long reception (Plato → Academy → Peripatetics → Rome → the three Lives → Middle Ages → Renaissance). Creates [[Thinkers/Archytas]], [[Schools/Neopythagoreanism]] + 8 concept pages; deepens the whole Pythagorean cluster. | source-summary | history-of-philosophy, history-of-science, religion
- [[Sources/Pythagoras and the Early Pythagoreans - Leonid Zhmud (2012)]] - Leonid Zhmud (2012; Russ. 1994). The major modern rebuttal of Burkert: a source-critical rehabilitation of Pythagoras and the early Pythagoreans as genuine pioneers of mathematics, harmonics, planetary astronomy, and medicine — *both* religious teacher *and* scientist. Privileges the 4th-c. Peripatetics (Eudemus, Aristoxenus); denies any early "all is number" doctrine; reframes the school as a political *hetairia* not a sect. Creates [[Thinkers/Hippasus]], [[Thinkers/Alcmaeon of Croton]], [[Concepts/Pythagorean Medicine and the Life Sciences]]; reopens [[Contradictions/Pythagoras the Scientist vs the Religious Charismatic]]. | source-summary | history-of-philosophy, history-of-science
- [[Sources/On Liberty - John Stuart Mill (1859)]] - John Stuart Mill, *On Liberty* (1859). The foundational text of political liberalism, defending individual autonomy, free speech, and personal diversity against state coercion and the tyranny of the majority using the Harm Principle.
- [[Sources/Utilitarianism - John Stuart Mill (1861)]] - John Stuart Mill, *Utilitarianism* (1861). The classic defense and formulation of utilitarian ethics, arguing that the Greatest Happiness Principle is the foundation of morality, introducing qualitative distinctions of pleasure, and reconciling utility with justice.
- [[Sources/The Communist Manifesto - Marx and Engels (1848)]] - Karl Marx and Friedrich Engels, *The Communist Manifesto* (1848). The seminal text of modern communism, outlining a materialist theory of history driven by class struggle and culminating in a proletarian revolution.
- [[Sources/Monadology - Leibniz (1714)]] - Gottfried Wilhelm Leibniz, *The Monadology* (1714). A condensed synthesis of rationalist metaphysics, presenting a universe composed of infinite immaterial, windowless simple substances (monads) pre-synchronized by God's Pre-Established Harmony.

**Immaterialism, Social Contract, Logical Atomism, Voluntarism, and Ontological Argument Ingest (2026-06-11)**:
- [[Sources/Three Dialogues between Hylas and Philonous - George Berkeley (1713)]] - George Berkeley, *Three Dialogues between Hylas and Philonous* (1713). A defense of immaterialism, arguing that physical objects are collections of sensible ideas that exist only in perceiving minds, sustained by God.
- [[Sources/The Social Contract - Jean-Jacques Rousseau (1762)]] - Jean-Jacques Rousseau, *The Social Contract* (1762). Outlines political legitimacy through popular sovereignty, direct democracy, and the collective General Will.
- [[Sources/Tractatus Logico-Philosophicus - Ludwig Wittgenstein (1922)]] - Ludwig Wittgenstein, *Tractatus Logico-Philosophicus* (1922). Presents the Picture Theory of Language, defining the logical limits of what can be said, while leaving ethics and aesthetics as unsayable.
- [[Sources/Philosophical Investigations - Ludwig Wittgenstein (1953)]] - Ludwig Wittgenstein, *Philosophical Investigations* (1953, posthumous). The masterwork of the "later Wittgenstein," refuting his own *Tractatus*: meaning is **use** within rule-governed **language-games**; concepts hang together by **family resemblance**, not essence; following a rule is a **practice** (the §201 paradox); a **private language** is incoherent (beetle in a box); justification ends in **forms of life**; philosophy is **therapy** ("the fly-bottle"). New: 9 concepts + 1 contradiction ([[Contradictions/Early vs Later Wittgenstein]]). | source-summary | philosophy-of-language, philosophy-of-mind, epistemology
- [[Sources/Wittgenstein’s Philosophical Investigations - A Critical Guide - Ahmed (2010)]] - Arif Ahmed (ed.), *Wittgenstein's Philosophical Investigations: A Critical Guide* (Cambridge, 2010). A scholarly collection of critical essays by contemporary philosophers (McDowell, Rorty, Horwich, etc.) analyzing core themes (metre bar, rules, contextualism, private language, inference, intentions). 2 new thinkers + 6 concepts. See log.
- [[Sources/The World as Will and Idea - Arthur Schopenhauer (1819)]] - Arthur Schopenhauer, *The World as Will and Idea* (1819). Establishes a voluntaristic metaphysics identifying the Kantian thing-in-itself as blind, insatiable Will, leading to inevitable suffering. PSR now specified from the 1847 *Fourfold Root*, not as one lump.
- [[Sources/The World as Will and Representation Volume 2 - Schopenhauer (Payne, Dover 2012)]] - Arthur Schopenhauer, *WWR* Volume 2 (1844; Payne 1958 / Dover 1966). Supplements, not a second system. Primacy of the will (not quite naked); need for metaphysics; genius; history not a science; sexual love; worst world (Ch. XLVI). | source-summary | metaphysics, epistemology, ethics, aesthetics
- [[Sources/On the Fourfold Root of the Principle of Sufficient Reason - Schopenhauer (Cambridge 2012)]] - Arthur Schopenhauer, *On the Fourfold Root* (1813 / 1847 second edition; Cartwright/Erdmann/Janaway, Cambridge 2012). Propaedeutic to *WWR*. Four roots: becoming, knowing, being, acting. Intellectual nature of intuition. Vision now a separate source; *On Will in Nature* still in the same EPUB. | source-summary | epistemology, metaphysics
- [[Sources/On Vision and Colours - Schopenhauer (Cambridge 2012)]] - Arthur Schopenhauer, *On Vision and Colours* (1816 / 1854 second edition; Cartwright/Erdmann/Janaway, Cambridge 2012). Colour = qualitatively divided activity of the retina. Goethe's data; this theory. Ch. I rehearses FR §21. Kantian primer. Not Stahl/Runge 2010. | source-summary | epistemology, philosophy-of-mind, philosophy-of-science
- [[Sources/Parerga and Paralipomena Volume 1 - Schopenhauer (Cambridge 2014)]] - Arthur Schopenhauer, *Parerga and Paralipomena* Volume 1 (1851; Roehr/Janaway Cambridge 2014). The *parerga*: popular companion that made him famous. Ideal/real solved (will = the real); university philosophy as hire; fate as fantasy; Aphorisms as eudaemonological **accommodation**. Volume 2 now a separate source. | source-summary | metaphysics, ethics, epistemology, metaphilosophy
- [[Sources/Parerga and Paralipomena Volume 2 - Schopenhauer (Payne 2000)]] - Arthur Schopenhauer, *Parerga and Paralipomena* Volume 2 (1851; Payne, Oxford 1974/2000). The *paralipomena*: 31 short essays. Compassion as moral incentive; suicide ≠ denial; religion as allegory; thinking for oneself; “On Women” as rant. | source-summary | metaphysics, ethics, aesthetics, metaphilosophy, philosophy-of-religion
- [[Sources/Proslogion - Anselm of Canterbury (1078)]] - Anselm of Canterbury, *Proslogion* (1078). A prayerful rational meditation containing the original a priori ontological proof of God's existence.

**Philosophy of Science (Karl Popper, 2026-06-04)**:
- [[Sources/The Logic of Scientific Discovery - Karl Popper (Routledge Classics)]] - Karl Popper, *The Logic of Scientific Discovery* (*Logik der Forschung*, 1934; English 1959; Routledge Classics 2002). The technical foundation of critical rationalism: the logic of science is the logic of *testing*, not of discovery or induction; **falsifiability** (not verifiability) demarcates empirical science via the logical asymmetry of universal and singular statements; the deductive (hypothetico-deductive) method; **corroboration** ("proved its mettle") vs. probability/verification; **basic statements** accepted by decision (intersubjective testability, no incorrigible "given"); methodological rules whose supreme rule is to protect no statement from falsification; solves Hume's induction problem by dropping "conclusive decidability." New entity + 4 concepts. (*Catalog line added 2026-06-04 to fix prior index drift.*)
- [[Sources/Conjectures and Refutations - Karl Popper]] - Karl Popper, *Conjectures and Refutations: The Growth of Scientific Knowledge* (1963; Routledge Classics 2002; dedicated to Hayek). The accessible, wider-ranging companion to *LSD*. Knowledge grows by **bold conjectures controlled by attempted refutations**; **no authoritative sources** of knowledge (neither observation nor reason; both classical schools share the false "manifest truth" doctrine, whose shadow is the conspiracy theory of ignorance); the famous demarcation lecture (**Einstein's risky eclipse prediction vs. the all-explaining Marx/Freud/Adler**; the seven conclusions; "conventionalist twist"); **three views of knowledge** (against essentialism + instrumentalism, for conjecturalism/realism); **verisimilitude** (truth-content − falsity-content; Tarski; the opposite of probability); the **three requirements for growth**; the **Presocratic critical tradition** ("all science is cosmology"; Anaximander); a sympathetic re-reading of Kant; the Carnap critique; and Popper's **political philosophy** (anti-**historicism**, prophecy vs. conditional prediction, anti-dialectic, open society, piecemeal engineering). 4 new concepts + updates to the 3 *LSD* concepts. See dedicated source page + [[Thinkers/Karl Popper]] + log.
- [[Sources/The Structure of Scientific Revolutions - Kuhn (Chicago, 4th ed.)]] - Thomas Kuhn, *The Structure of Scientific Revolutions* (1962; Chicago 4th ed. 2012, with Ian Hacking's introductory essay + Postscript—1969). The most influential 20th-c. work in history/philosophy of science and the **major counterweight to Popper**. Science as a cycle: **normal science** (puzzle-solving under a [[Concepts/Paradigm (Kuhn)|paradigm]]) → accumulating [[Concepts/Anomaly and Crisis (Kuhn)|anomaly → crisis]] → [[Concepts/Scientific Revolutions and Paradigm Shifts (Kuhn)|revolution / paradigm shift]] (a gestalt-switch/conversion, not compelled by proof) → new normal science; rival paradigms are [[Concepts/Incommensurability (Kuhn)|incommensurable]]; progress is "evolution *from*, not *toward*" a fixed truth. Postscript splits *paradigm* into disciplinary matrix vs. exemplar and ties it to the scientific community. Directly disputes Popperian falsification ("I doubt that [falsifying experiences] exist"; reject one paradigm only by accepting another). New entity + 5 concepts + the [[Contradictions/Popper vs Kuhn - Falsification vs Paradigms]] page. The vault's first history/sociology-of-science source. See dedicated source page + [[Thinkers/Thomas Kuhn]] + log.
- [[Sources/The Origins of Modern Science 1300-1800 - Herbert Butterfield (1949)]] - Herbert Butterfield, *The Origins of Modern Science 1300-1800* (1949; based on 1948 Cambridge lectures). The classic mid-20th-c. framing of the "Scientific Revolution" (16th–17th c., roots earlier) as the pivotal event that "overturned the authority in science not only of the middle ages but of the ancient world", changed "habitual mental operations", and originated the modern world and mentality — outshining Renaissance and Reformation. Emphasises transformation of "thinking cap," recovery of older systems being overthrown, "misfires" and hurdles, and science's later "directing role"; civilisational leadership shifts north. Historiographical background for the Phil of Science cluster (Kuhn as major revision with normal science, conversions, incommensurability). New source + thinker + concept; cross-updates to Kuhn etc. See [[Thinkers/Herbert Butterfield]] + [[Concepts/Scientific Revolution (Butterfield)]] + log.
- [[Sources/Two Dogmas of Empiricism - W.V.O. Quine (1951)]] - W.V.O. Quine, "Two Dogmas of Empiricism" (1951 *Philosophical Review*; 1961 revisions in *From a Logical Point of View*). Foundational critique of the two dogmas of logical empiricism: the analytic/synthetic distinction (truth by meaning vs. fact) and reductionism (each meaningful statement reducible to experience constructs or having unique confirming sensory events). Yields **confirmation holism** (web of belief; unit of significance = whole of science), physical objects etc. as pragmatic "cultural posits" (epistemologically on a par with gods), and a shift to thorough **pragmatism** + naturalized epistemology. Directly attacks Hume's Fork background and Carnap's verificationism/state-descriptions; sets up post-positivist issues for Popper and Kuhn. New entity + 4 concepts. See dedicated source page + [[Thinkers/W.V.O. Quine]] + log.
- [[Sources/Empiricism and the Philosophy of Mind - Wilfrid Sellars (1956)]] - Wilfrid Sellars, *Empiricism and the Philosophy of Mind* (1956 London lectures / *Minnesota Studies* vol. 1; Harvard 1997 ed. with Richard Rorty's Introduction and Robert Brandom's Study Guide). The essay that kills **the Given** — the demand that some episodes be both epistemically independent of all other knowledge and able to justify it. Sixteen parts: the **inconsistent triad** in sense-datum theory (sensing particulars vs. knowing facts; the sense datum as "a mongrel"); sense-datum talk as *code* vs. *theory*; the **logic of 'looks'** (the necktie shop; "looks" ascribes a propositional claim but withholds endorsement; *looking* green presupposes *being* green; the break with logical atomism); **psychological nominalism** ("all awareness … is a linguistic affair"; Locke/Berkeley/Hume shared an unargued innate awareness of determinate repeatables); 'means' as not a relation; the foundations argument and the **space of reasons** ("we are placing it in the logical space of reasons, of justifying and being able to justify what one says," §36) with the elephant-and-tortoise image and "a **self-correcting enterprise** which can put *any* claim in jeopardy, though not *all* at once" (§38); ***scientia mensura*** (§41 — "science is the measure of all things," and instrumentalism rests on the same Given as foundationalism); and the **Myth of Jones** (§§48–63 — thoughts and impressions introduced as *theoretical* posits of a public framework which then gains a reporting role, so anti-foundationalism entails neither behaviorism nor eliminativism). The vault's bridge from philosophy of science to philosophy of mind, and the source behind the Myth of the Given that [[Thinkers/John McDowell]] had named since 2026-06-29. 3 new thinkers + 6 concepts + 1 argument. Scanned PDF, OCR'd from page images. Manifest/scientific image is the **1962** essay, not this. | source-summary | epistemology, philosophy-of-mind, philosophy-of-language, philosophy-of-science
- [[Sources/Visions of Politics Volume 1 - Regarding Method - Quentin Skinner (2002)]] - Quentin Skinner, *Visions of Politics, Vol. 1: Regarding Method* (Cambridge, 2002), 10 chapters, pp. 1–187; vol. 1 of 3, ch. 4 being the revised 1969 manifesto. The **Cambridge School** charter. Programme: "to grasp their concepts... and, so far as possible, to see things their way" — recover not what a text says but what its author was **doing** in writing it (Austin's **illocutionary force**). Ch. 2 demolishes G. R. Elton's "cult of the fact" (the Chatsworth thought-experiment; "true fact" as pleonasm). Ch. 3: "the concept of truth is irrelevant to the enterprise of explaining beliefs" — ask instead whether it was rational to hold true by then-prevailing norms. Ch. 4: the **mythologies** — of *doctrines* (scattered remarks promoted to doctrines; Lovejoy-style reification, ideas that "get up to do battle on their own behalf"; blame for absence — "a pack of tricks we play on the dead"), of *coherence* (Strauss's esotericism), of *prolepsis* ("the episode has to await the future to learn its meaning"), plus two forms of *parochialism* (spurious influence; misdescribed sense). Chs. 5–6: motives (outside the work) vs intentions *in* writing (a feature of it); meaning₁/₂/₃; Skinner is **not** an intentionalist about textual meaning and accepts Ricoeur's surplus meaning. Ch. 8: the **innovating ideologist** must legitimise, so "all revolutionaries are obliged to march backwards into battle" and principles are *enabling conditions* of action even when insincere. Ch. 9: words are not concepts; polysemy is the trace of ideological failure. Ch. 10 (*Retrospect*): **paradiastole** and a set of frank self-corrections. The vault's third and most contested History-of-Ideas method, against Lovejoy and Berlin. 2 new thinkers + 6 concepts. | source-summary | history-of-ideas, philosophy-of-language, methodology, political-philosophy
- [[Sources/The Meaning of Meaning - Ogden and Richards (1923)]] - C. K. Ogden and I. A. Richards, *The Meaning of Meaning* (1923; 8th ed. 1946; 1956 Harcourt reprint). Science of symbolism: the word–thing relation is never direct (Symbol → Thought/Reference → Referent); word-magic is the history of filling in the triangle's base; reference is causal-contextual; truth is context-completion; language has symbolic and emotive functions (Ayer's ancestor); six canons + a technique of definition; sixteen senses of "meaning." Supplements: Malinowski (context of situation, phatic communion) and Crookshank (medical word-magic). Appendix D is the vault's first Peirce dossier. Not Putnam's "The Meaning of 'Meaning'." | source-summary | philosophy-of-language, epistemology, aesthetics
- [[Sources/Language, Truth and Logic - A.J. Ayer (1936)]] - A.J. Ayer, *Language, Truth and Logic* (1936; Penguin 2001). The founding English-language manifesto of logical positivism: verification principle (meaningful = analytic or empirically verifiable; else literally senseless), elimination of metaphysics, all a priori = analytic = tautological (defeats Kant's synthetic a priori), emotive theory of ethics (moral statements express feeling, neither true nor false), philosophy as analysis / logic of science. The canonical statement of what Popper, Kuhn, and Quine are reacting against. New entity ([[Thinkers/A.J. Ayer]]) + 4 concepts. Emotive half now has its 1923 ancestor in [[Sources/The Meaning of Meaning - Ogden and Richards (1923)]]. See source page + log.
- [[Sources/Against Method - Paul Feyerabend]] - Paul Feyerabend, *Against Method: Outline of an Anarchistic Theory of Knowledge* (1975; 3rd ed. 1993, incorporating *Science in a Free Society*; dedicated to Lakatos). The **radical pole** of the philosophy-of-science leg: there is **no fixed method** — every rule (including [[Concepts/Falsifiability as Criterion of Demarcation (Popper)|Popperian falsificationism]]) has been productively broken, so "[[Concepts/Epistemological Anarchism (Feyerabend)|anything goes]]." Built on the [[Concepts/Natural Interpretations and the Galileo Case (Feyerabend)|Galileo case]] (theory-laden "natural interpretations" replaced by rhetoric, an untrustworthy telescope, and [[Concepts/Counterinduction (Feyerabend)|counter-induction]]); argues for [[Concepts/Proliferation and Tenacity (Feyerabend)|proliferation + tenacity]] (Millian pluralism); concludes politically that science is one tradition among many and calls for the [[Concepts/Separation of Science and State (Feyerabend)|separation of science and state]]. New entity + 5 concepts; broadens the [[Contradictions/Popper vs Kuhn - Falsification vs Paradigms]] page. See dedicated source page + [[Thinkers/Paul Feyerabend]] + log.
- [[Sources/Representing and Intervening - Ian Hacking (1983)]] - Ian Hacking, *Representing and Intervening: Introductory Topics in the Philosophy of Natural Science* (1983). The experimental turn complementing the vault's rationality/representation cluster. Distinguishes entity realism from theory realism; "[[Concepts/If You Can Spray Them Then They Are Real (Hacking)|if you can spray them, then they are real]]" (use of electrons/positrons as tools in the Stanford niobium-ball experiment convinced Hacking). Experiment has a life of its own, often creates stable phenomena rather than recording pre-existing ones, and observation is a skill (pre-theoretical cases + convergence across independent instruments). Engages Kuhn (incommensurability as irrealist pressure on representation; Hacking wrote the intro to the ingested Chicago 4th ed.), Feyerabend, Lakatos (as surrogate for truth), and positivism. New entity + 5 concepts; extends the central [[Contradictions/Popper vs Kuhn - Falsification vs Paradigms]] page. See dedicated source page + [[Thinkers/Ian Hacking]] + log.
- [[Sources/Heidegger - A Very Short Introduction - Michael Inwood (1997)]] - Michael Inwood, *Heidegger: A Very Short Introduction* (OUP, 1997/2000). Compressed survey of Heidegger's entire philosophy centered on *Being and Time* (1927): the question of Being and the ontological difference; Dasein as being-in-the-world; ready-to-hand vs. present-at-hand; truth as unconcealment (*aletheia*); care (*Sorge*) as Dasein's being; being-toward-death, anxiety, conscience, resoluteness; ecstatic temporality (four levels of time); historicality and the Turn (*Kehre*); art and truth (*The Origin of the Work of Art*). Biography, Nazism, and legacy. The vault's first dedicated Heidegger source. New thinker + 10 concepts + 1 school (Phenomenology). See [[Thinkers/Martin Heidegger]] + log.
- [[Sources/Being and Time - Martin Heidegger (Stambaugh rev. Schmidt)]] - Martin Heidegger, *Being and Time* (1927; Stambaugh trans., rev. Dennis J. Schmidt). The vault's first primary Heidegger text, full ingest §§1–83 + the later marginalia (*Hüttenexemplar*). The question of the meaning of being; existential analytic of Dasein (existentials vs. categories, handiness, world as significance, das Man, disclosedness, anxiety, care §41); Division Two: being-toward-death, conscience/guilt/resoluteness, temporality as meaning of care (§65 — ecstatic, finite), historicity, world-time vs. vulgar now-time (Aristotle, Hegel); breaks off unfinished at §83. 7 new concepts + 10 existing pages deepened with primary quotations. | source-summary | ontology, phenomenology, philosophy-of-time

**Public Choice & Political Economy (Mancur Olson, Buchanan & Tullock, 2026-06-29 Ingest)**:
- [[Sources/The Rise and Decline of Nations - Mancur Olson (1982)]] - Mancur Olson, *The Rise and Decline of Nations: Economic Growth, Stagflation, and Social Rigidities* (1982). Public choice analysis extending the logic of collective action to the rise, decline, and macroeconomic performance of nations. Denounces special-interest cartels and lobbies (distributional coalitions) as sources of institutional sclerosis that slow growth, complicate regulations, and generate stagflation. Establishes the encompassing organization logic and the role of jurisdictional integration. New thinker + 6 concepts. See log.
- [[Sources/The Calculus of Consent - Buchanan and Tullock (1962)]] - James M. Buchanan and Gordon Tullock, *The Calculus of Consent: Logical Foundations of Constitutional Democracy* (1962). The foundational text of public choice and constitutional political economy. Applies utility-maximizing individual models to the choice of constitutional rules (expected costs model of external vs. decision costs) and legislative processes (vote-trading, logrolling, pressure groups). 2 new thinkers + 5 concepts. See log.
- [[Sources/The Myth of the Rational Voter (Caplan)]] - Bryan Caplan, *The Myth of the Rational Voter: Why Democracies Choose Bad Policies* (2007).
