Argues that democratic failure stems not from voter ignorance but from *rational irrationality* — voters hold systematically biased economic beliefs (anti-market, anti-foreign, make-work, pessimistic) because false political beliefs are individually costless. Empirically grounded in the 1996 SAEE. Revises Classical Public Choice (Downs/Tullock) and defeats Wittman's defense of democracy. 1 new thinker + 6 new concepts. See log.
- [[Sources/Ecce Homo - Nietzsche (Collection 2013)]] — Nietzsche's last completed book (written 1888, published 1908), from the 2013 22-work collection. Self-review of the whole corpus: the Yes-half/No-half periodization, the *amor fati* "formula for greatness" (Clever §10), eternal recurrence dated to August 1881 at Silvaplana, the immoralist's two denials, "Dionysus against the Crucified." **Preface and "Why I am So Wise" are missing from this edition.** | source-summary | ethics, metaphysics, philosophy-of-life, autobiography
- [[Sources/The Dawn of Day - Nietzsche (Kennedy, Collection 2013)]] — Friedrich Nietzsche, *The Dawn of Day* (1881; 1886 Preface; J. M. Kennedy). Missing free-spirit middle. Faith in morals undermined; morality of custom (§9); feeling of power (not WzP); pity as multiplier; §575 aeronauts. Not Hollingdale *Daybreak*. | source-summary | ethics, moral-psychology, epistemology

### Thinkers

Philosophers, scientists, authors, and strategists (`type: thinker`, in `Thinkers/`). *(Off-leg entities formerly listed here — AI tools, Alliant, CPCU, and the personal profile — were retyped during the schema migration and now appear under Concepts and Notes & Other.)*

- [[Thinkers/Saul Kripke]] — Saul A. Kripke (1940–2022). *Naming and Necessity* (1970/1980): rigid designation; a priori ≠ necessary; historical *picture* of naming; necessity of origin; natural-kind essences; identity thesis and pain. Not the WRPL Kripke. | thinker | philosophy-of-language, metaphysics, epistemology, philosophy-of-mind
- [[Thinkers/Hilary Putnam]] — Hilary Putnam (1926–2016). Reception-first page from Pritchard and Ranalli 2016: causal constraints on reference; the BIV argument is semantic, not a knowledge claim; 1981 and 1975 primaries still outstanding. Contrast class for active externalism; failed refutation in Chalmers's Simulation Riposte. N&N Lecture III ally on kinds (reported). | thinker | epistemology, philosophy-of-language, metaphysics
- [[Thinkers/Isaiah Berlin]] — Isaiah Berlin (1909–1997). *Crooked Timber* is now the primary (value pluralism, anti-utopia, Counter-Enlightenment, Romantic will, bent-twig nationalism). 2013 Baum/Nichols remains reception of the 1958 lecture, which is still outstanding. | thinker | political-philosophy, history-of-ideas
- [[Thinkers/Giambattista Vico]] — Berlin’s reading, not a *Scienza nuova* primary: father of culture and cultural pluralism; *fantasia*; *verum/factum*; not a relativist. | thinker | history-of-ideas
- [[Thinkers/Johann Gottfried Herder]] — Berlin’s reading: incommensurable cultures; centre of gravity; belonging vs inflamed nationalism; not a relativist. | thinker | history-of-ideas
- [[Thinkers/Joseph de Maistre]] — Berlin’s reading: executioner, earth as altar, *la secte*; proto-fascist in temperament, not Mussolini; ultramontane not Romantic. | thinker | history-of-ideas, political-philosophy
- [[Thinkers/Robert Nozick]] — Robert Nozick (1938–2002), Harvard. *Anarchy, State, and Utopia* (1974): rights as side constraints; invisible-hand minimal state; entitlement theory; experience machine; utopia as framework. First libertarian primary; Rawls is the named opponent (not yet a vault primary). | thinker | political-philosophy, ethics
- [[Thinkers/Antonio Damasio]] — Portuguese-American neurologist. *Descartes' Error* (1994): emotion is in the loop of reason (somatic markers); feeling is body-landscape perception; the *cogito* slogan's afterlife is the named error. Lesion ancestor of Clark/Hohwy/Seth on the body. | thinker | philosophy-of-mind, cognitive-science, neuroscience, affective-science
- [[Thinkers/Stanislas Dehaene]] — French cognitive neuroscientist (Collège de France / NeuroSpin). *Consciousness and the Brain* (2014): consciousness is conscious access implemented as global neuronal workspace; leftover phenomenal residue is a dualist slope. Experimentalist ally of Dennett/Clark; opponent of Chalmers. | thinker | philosophy-of-mind, cognitive-science, neuroscience, ai
- [[Thinkers/Edmund Husserl]] — Edmund Husserl (1859–1938), founder of phenomenology. Now has a primary: *Cartesian Meditations* (Cairns). Neo-Cartesianism; transcendental ego as pole/habitualities/monad; §41 idealism; pairing. Smith 2013 remains the system map. | thinker | phenomenology, philosophy-of-mind, ontology, epistemology
- [[Thinkers/Hubert Dreyfus]] — Hubert Lederer Dreyfus (1929–2017), Berkeley. *What Computers Still Can't Do*: human intelligence is embodied skilled coping, not facts-plus-rules; GOFAI is the last metaphysics of the Platonic–Leibnizian tradition. Not a proof that AI is impossible. Principal phenomenological opponent of Turing/Minsky; ally-and-fork with Searle; predecessor of Clark. | thinker | philosophy-of-mind, phenomenology, ai
- [[Thinkers/Francisco J. Varela]] — Francisco J. Varela (1946–2001). First author of *The Embodied Mind*: enaction, operational closure, natural drift. Later neurophenomenology is reported, not primary. | thinker | philosophy-of-mind, cognitive-science, phenomenology
- [[Thinkers/Evan Thompson]] — Co-author; 2016 introduction recants three 1991 strategies (Husserl failed; Buddhist philosophy from meditation; mindfulness as introspection). | thinker | philosophy-of-mind, phenomenology
- [[Thinkers/Eleanor Rosch]] — Co-author; categories/color in the 1991 text; 2016 introduction splits enaction into samsaric phase 1 and groundless phase 2. | thinker | cognitive-science, philosophy-of-mind
- [[Thinkers/Jakob Hohwy]] — Australian philosopher of mind (Monash). *The Predictive Mind* (2013): PEM as the brain's only job; seclusion behind a veil of sensory input. Systematic counterpart to Clark; declines the hard problem. | thinker | philosophy-of-mind, cognitive-science, neuroscience
- [[Thinkers/Karl Friston]] — Computational neuroscientist Hohwy treats as the technical source of PEM / free energy / active inference. Reported, not a primary ingest. | thinker | neuroscience, cognitive-science, philosophy-of-mind
- [[Thinkers/Anil Seth]] — British neuroscientist of consciousness, Sussex; *Being You* (2021). **Real problem** (explain/predict/control properties) + **beast machine** (conscious *because* living). Same PEM family as Clark/Hohwy; life before intelligence. | thinker | philosophy-of-mind, cognitive-science, neuroscience
- [[Thinkers/Andy Clark]] — British philosopher of mind and cognitive science (b. 1957), Sussex; architect of the **extended mind** (with Chalmers, 1998) and of **action-oriented predictive processing**. Primaries: 1998 paper; *Surfing Uncertainty* (2016); *The Experience Machine* (2023). Hohwy 2013 is the seclusion opponent; 2016 is Clark's reply. Seth biologizes the same mechanism. | thinker | philosophy-of-mind, cognitive-science, ai, neuroscience
- [[Thinkers/David Chalmers]] — Australian philosopher of mind (b. 1966): the **hard problem of consciousness**, the **extended mind** (with Clark), and *Reality+*'s thesis that virtual reality is genuine reality — answering Descartes inside the Cartesian problematic via simulation realism. | thinker | philosophy-of-mind, metaphysics, epistemology, ai
- [[Thinkers/Frank Jackson]] — Frank Jackson, Australian philosopher (Monash). Author of the knowledge argument (Fred, then Mary) and of the claim that leftover qualia may be physically idle ("Epiphenomenal Qualia," 1982); defended the argument against Churchland in 1986. Separates his case from Nagel (a missing *property*, not a missing point of view) and ranks the modal/zombie argument as weaker. Fourth voice on the Dennett/Nagel contradiction. Later recanted (no vault source). | thinker | philosophy-of-mind, epistemology, metaphysics
- [[Thinkers/Thomas Nagel]] — Thomas Nagel (b. 1937), NYU. "What Is It Like to Be a Bat?" (1974): consciousness iff there is something it is like for the organism; physicalism presently unintelligible, not false; 2023 lecture hopes for dual-aspect monism and refuses Chalmers's property dualism. The missing pole of [[Contradictions/Dennett vs Nagel - Multiple Drafts vs Subjective Experience]]. Not [[Thinkers/Ernest Nagel]]. | thinker | philosophy-of-mind, phenomenology, metaphysics

**Pre-Socratic Philosophy (Burnet 1920, 2026-06-11 Ingest)**:
- [[Thinkers/Thales]] - Milesian philosopher and scientist (c. 624–546 BCE), recognized as the first Western philosopher; held water as the *arche* of all things.
- [[Thinkers/Anaximander]] - Milesian philosopher (c. 610–546 BCE), pupil of Thales; proposed the *Apeiron* (the Boundless) as the first principle of cosmology.
- [[Thinkers/Anaximenes]] - Milesian philosopher (c. 586–526 BCE), associate of Anaximander; held *Air* to be the primary substance through condensation and rarefaction.
- [[Thinkers/Pythagoras]] - Greek philosopher and religious reformer (c. 570–495 BCE); held that number is the essence of all reality and taught metempsychosis. **Now contested**: Burkert (1972) argues the mathematician-scientist portrait is a later retrojection and the historical Pythagoras was a religious charismatic; Zhmud (2012) rebuts, defending him as both teacher and scientist — see [[Contradictions/Pythagoras the Scientist vs the Religious Charismatic]].
- [[Thinkers/Philolaus]] - Pythagorean of Croton (c. 470–385 BCE); earliest Pythagorean with surviving (partly genuine) fragments and, on Burkert's reconstruction, the true source of the "number cosmology" (limiters & unlimiteds bound by harmony) and the central-fire system credited to Pythagoras. Zhmud (2012) corrects: "to have number" = knowability not number-ontology, and the harmony of the spheres should be detached from his system. | thinker | metaphysics, cosmology, mathematics
- [[Thinkers/Archytas]] - Pythagorean mathematician, harmonic theorist, and statesman of Tarentum (c. 435–360 BC); "the first and only Pythagorean credibly associated with significant technical achievements in mathematics" (the Delian problem, the sibling sciences/quadrivium, the three means). Friend of Plato and the main channel of Pythagorean mathematics into the Academy; the last great figure of the early school. | thinker | mathematics, harmonics, politics
- [[Thinkers/Hippasus]] - Early Pythagorean of Metapontum (early–mid 5th c. BC); the figure to whom genuine mathematics is securely attached — the discovery of incommensurability, the bronze-disc acoustic experiment, the first proportions, the dodecahedron-in-sphere. The pivot of the Burkert–Zhmud dispute; his betrayal-legend springs from the *arrhētos* ("irrational"/"secret") pun. | thinker | mathematics, harmonics
- [[Thinkers/Alcmaeon of Croton]] - Crotonian physician-philosopher (late 6th–early 5th c. BC); Zhmud's case for the empirical heart of early Pythagoreanism — health as *isonomia*, the brain-centred theory of cognition, knowledge by inference (*tekmairesthai*), embryology, and soul-immortality (without transmigration). | thinker | medicine, epistemology, philosophy-of-mind
- [[Thinkers/Heraclitus]] - Greek philosopher (c. 535–475 BCE) from Ephesus; taught universal flux (*panta rhei*, fire), the unity of opposites governed by the *Logos*, and strife as creative force.
- [[Thinkers/Parmenides]] - Greek philosopher (c. 515–450 BCE) from Elea; founder of Eleatic monism, argued that Being is one, indivisible, and unchanging, introducing deductive logic to metaphysics.
- [[Thinkers/Zeno of Elea]] - Eleatic philosopher (c. 490–430 BCE), pupil of Parmenides; invented dialectic and proposed famous paradoxes of motion to defend Eleatic monism.
- [[Thinkers/Empedocles]] - Greek philosopher, poet, and statesman (c. 490–430 BCE) from Sicily; proposed the four elements (earth, air, fire, water) acted upon by Love and Strife.
- [[Thinkers/Anaxagoras]] - Greek philosopher (c. 500–428 BCE); brought philosophy to Athens, argued that a cosmic mind (*Nous*) orders infinite, qualitatively distinct seeds of matter.
- [[Thinkers/Democritus]] - Greek philosopher (c. 460–370 BCE) from Abdera; co-founder of ancient atomism (atoms and void in mechanical motion), presenting a materialist cosmology and cheerful ethics.

**Classical Philosophy**:
- [[Thinkers/Plato]] - Athenian philosopher (c. 427–347 BCE), founder of the Academy; author of dialogues spanning ethics, politics, and metaphysics, including *Timaeus*, *Critias*, *Republic*, *Symposium*, *Phaedrus*, *Sophist*, and *Statesman*. Updated with late dialogues and speeches on love and statesmanship.
- [[Thinkers/Socrates]] - Figure, not reconstructed biography (Hadot): *philo-sopher* not sage; irony and dialogue as spiritual exercises; *atopos*; Eros-identity via the *Symposium*; Kierkegaard and Nietzsche as modern readers. Prototype of philosophy as a way of life. | thinker | ethics, metaphilosophy


**Classical Greek Philosophy & History (Aristotle + Thucydides, 2026-06-04)**:
- [[Thinkers/Aristotle]] - Athenian philosopher (384–322 BCE), student of Plato, founder of the Lyceum. Works on modal logic/Organon (essentialism, modal copula, scientific demonstration), Nicomachean Ethics (eudaimonia as activity in accordance with virtue), Rhetoric (enthymeme, ethos/logos/pathos, branches of oratory), Metaphysics, and Politics (zōon politikon, natural slavery, classification of constitutions, polity/middle-class thesis, stasis, best state and education). Primary classical counterpart to Plato.
- [[Thinkers/Maimonides]] - Moses ben Maimon / Rambam (1138–1204), foremost philosopher of medieval Judaism; physician and jurist in Córdoba→Cairo. *Guide for the Perplexed* reconciles Torah and Aristotle: negative theology, rejection of Kalam occasionalism, creation-on-authority, naturalized prophecy, graded providence, rational law. Third corner of the Ghazali↔Averroes debate; major influence on Aquinas and (by inversion) Spinoza. | thinker | metaphysics, theology, ethics
- [[Thinkers/Al-Ghazali]] - Abu Hamid al-Ghazali (1058–1111), the most influential religious intellectual of Sunni Islam; Ash'arite theologian, jurist, and Sufi. *Tahafut al-Falasifa* refutes the Avicennan Aristotelians and condemns 3 doctrines as unbelief; Ash'arite occasionalism, the eternal-will cosmology, and the demonstrability criterion. The interlocutor whom [[Thinkers/Ibn Rushd]] answers. | thinker | metaphysics, theology, epistemology
- [[Thinkers/Ibn Rushd]] - Last great Islamic philosopher (1126–1198, Cordoba); “The Commentator” on Aristotle. *Tahafut al-Tahafut* defends eternity of the world and real natural causation against Ghazali’s occasionalist theology; nuanced on soul, resurrection, and the necessity of religious law for the masses. Primary source grounding Aquinas’ corrections on unicity of intellect and hylomorphism. New entity + concepts. Bridges Greek Aristotle → Latin scholasticism.
- [[Thinkers/Thucydides]] - Athenian historian/general (c. 460–400 BCE), author of the *History of the Peloponnesian War*. Empirical analysis of empire, subject revolt (Mytilene), power asymmetry in alliances, strategy, diplomacy, and the rhetoric of justification (honor, necessity, justice). “A possession for all time.”

**Stoicism (Roman, 2026-06-04 addition)**:
- [[Thinkers/Epictetus]] - Stoic teacher (c. AD 50–135, former slave). *Enchiridion*, Fragments, Golden Sayings: dichotomy of control (*prohairesis* – what is in our power vs externals), proper use of impressions, acceptance of role, equanimity, philosophy as daily discipline and service to divine order.
- [[Thinkers/Seneca]] - Roman Stoic (c. 4 BC–AD 65). *Letters to Lucilius*: practical ethics from daily occasions on time, death, friendship, virtue vs fortune, self-sufficiency, philosophy as medicine, endurance, critique of spectacle/wealth.
- [[Thinkers/Marcus Aurelius]] - Emperor and Stoic (AD 121–180). *Meditations* (full 12 books, primary journal during campaigns, George Long trans. from Stoic Six Pack); closest historical philosopher-king (per Plato ideal). Education under Stoics (Rusticus → Epictetus), governance with justice, examined life amid power and war. Now primary-sourced in his own words (daemon / ruling faculty, cosmic perspective, every act as if the last, co-operation, cheerful mortality).
- [[Thinkers/Ryan Holiday]] - American author and popularizer of Stoicism (b. 1987). *The Obstacle Is the Way* (2014) reframes Roman Stoic ethics as a practical program (Perception, Action, Will) via Hadot; the vault's bridge from the classical Stoic triad into 21st-century practical philosophy/self-help. Transmitter, not scholar; reads virtue somewhat instrumentally.

**Hellenistic Philosophy (Epicurus, 2026-06-04 addition)**:
- [[Thinkers/Epicurus]] - Greek philosopher (341–271 BCE), founder of Epicureanism. Revived atomism (infinite atoms/void, multiple worlds, swerve for free will and collisions); empiricist epistemology (senses as criterion, verificationism); ethics of refined hedonism (ataraxia/tranquility as goal via prudent pleasure, "death is nothing," "live unnoticed"/lathe biosas, friendship; gods exist but uninvolved). Garden school as anti-elitist community. Materialist counter to Plato/Aristotle/Stoics; precursor to modern science/contractarianism.

**Roman Epicureanism (Lucretius, 2026-06-03 addition)**:
- [[Thinkers/Lucretius]] - Roman poet-philosopher (c. 99–55 BCE), author of *De Rerum Natura*. Primary elaborator of Epicurean atomism: secondary qualities doctrine (atoms have no color/smell — all emergent), four-component mind (heat/wind/air/unnamed 4th), 14 mortalism arguments (Book III), symmetry argument ("pre-birth = post-death nothingness"), mythological hells as earthly psychology. The Epicurean–Enlightenment transmission chain runs through him; rediscovered 1417 by Poggio Bracciolini. Direct philosophical counterpoint to Plato (Forms), Aristotle (final causes), and Plotinus (immortal soul/return).

**Neoplatonism (Plotinus, 2026-06-04 addition)**:
- [[Thinkers/Plotinus]] - Greek philosopher (c. 204–270 CE), founder of Neoplatonism. *Enneads* (Porphyry ed.): the One (transcendent ineffable source/Good) emanates hierarchically (One → Intellect/Nous with Forms → Soul → matter) without loss; soul's natural return (epistrophe) via purification, virtue ("likeness to God"), and contemplation to henosis (union). Synthesis of Plato (Forms, soul), Aristotle (Intellect), Stoicism (virtue, likeness) into mystical metaphysics of unity and ascent. Major influence on Christian, Islamic, and Renaissance thought. "Plato redivivus." Wallis (1972) adds: identification of metaphysical realities with states of consciousness; the undescended soul; cosmic sympathy's limits; the problems bequeathed to the later school.
- [[Thinkers/Numenius of Apamea]] - Neopythagorean-Middle Platonist (fl. c. 150 AD, Apamea, Syria), the most important named forerunner of Plotinus. Triad of gods (Father/uncreating Intellect–Creator/Demiurge–Creature/cosmos); matter as a positive evil Indefinite Dyad coeval with God (rejected by Plotinus); Being as timeless/incorporeal; soul immaterial, immortal, one with its source, imprisoned in body; radical syncretism ("Plato = Moses speaking Attic"); "all is in all." Read in Plotinus's school (Amelius defended Plotinus vs the plagiarism charge); shaped Porphyry and Iamblichus. | thinker | metaphysics, philosophy-of-religion
- [[Thinkers/Porphyry]] - Plotinus's pupil, biographer, and editor (c. 232–c. 305): arranged the *Enneads*, wrote the *Isagoge*, "telescoped" the Hypostases, criticized theurgy rationally (*Letter to Anebo*), chief anti-Christian polemicist and conduit of Neoplatonism to the Latin West (Augustine's lens). | thinker | metaphysics, logic
- [[Thinkers/Iamblichus]] - Syrian Neoplatonist (d. c. 326) who fixed later Neoplatonism's framework: Ineffable beyond the One, triadic stratification (Law of Mean Terms, Unparticipated/Participated/Participant), fully descended soul, theurgy and divine grace over contemplation (*De Mysteriis*), the *skopos* rule of exegesis. | thinker | metaphysics, philosophy-of-religion
- [[Thinkers/Proclus]] - Head of the Athenian Academy (412–85), systematizer of late Neoplatonism: *Elements of Theology* (geometric deduction of the scale of being), the henads, monē–proodos–epistrophē, evil as parhypostasis, providence "by the gods' very existence," two vehicles of the soul; source of the *Liber de Causis* and Pseudo-Dionysius. | thinker | metaphysics, philosophy-of-religion
- [[Thinkers/Pseudo-Dionysius]] - Unknown Syrian ecclesiastic, late 5th c., writing as Paul's Areopagite convert (Proclus-dependent). Super-Essential Godhead beyond being and every name; *Divine Names* as cataphatic itinerary; *Mystical Theology* as Darkness of Unknowing; Good overflows by existing (DN IV); matter not evil; God before Time and Eternity. Chief mould of Christian mystical theology (Maximus, Erigena, Aquinas). | thinker | metaphysics, philosophy-of-religion, theology, mysticism
- [[Thinkers/Arthur O. Lovejoy]] - American philosopher/historian of ideas (1873–1962). *The Great Chain of Being* (1936): definitive tracing of the Chain (plenitude + continuity + gradation) as the pervasive presupposition from Plato/Aristotle/Neoplatonism through medieval/Enlightenment to temporalizing and "negative outcome" (rationalist hypothesis of complete intelligibility fails; world contingent). Initiates History of Ideas leg; method of unit-ideas. Cross to classical (the Chain as historical vehicle of emanation/gradation) and Phil of Science (background cosmology critiqued by Popper/Kuhn et al.). See new source + 4 concepts.

**Hellenistic Skepticism (Pyrrhonism, 2026-06-03 addition)**:
- [[Thinkers/Sextus Empiricus]] - Greek philosopher/physician (fl. late 2nd–early 3rd cent. CE), principal surviving source for Pyrrhonian Skepticism (*Outlines of Pyrrhonism*); method: equipollence (isosthenia) leading to epoché (suspension of judgment) and ataraxia (tranquility).

**Medieval Scholasticism (Anselm & Aquinas, 2026-06-11 Ingest)**:
- [[Thinkers/Anselm of Canterbury]] - Scholastic philosopher and theologian (1033–1109), Archbishop of Canterbury; father of Scholasticism and developer of the original ontological argument.
- [[Thinkers/Thomas Aquinas]] - Dominican theologian/philosopher (1225–1274, "Angelical Doctor" or "Angelic Doctor"). *Summa Theologica* I-II (Prima Secundae): last end/happiness as beatific vision of God (elevating Aristotle's eudaimonia); natural law (rational participation in eternal law); grace perfects nature; virtues (theological + cardinal); quaestio method. Major synthesis of Aristotle + Christian theology, natural law, and grace. New concepts: Beatific Vision/Last End, Natural Law. Extends classical ethics into medieval scholasticism.

**Patristics / Augustine (2026-06-04 addition)**:
- [[Thinkers/Augustine]] - Bishop of Hippo (354–430 CE, "Doctor Gratiae"). *Confessions* (13 books): pilgrimage of grace, "restless heart until it rests in you," divided will, garden conversion ("tolle lege"), memory as inner path to God/self-knowledge, time as distentio animi, evil as privation, Neo-Platonism as bridge (Book 7) to Christian resolution. Primary patristic source for Aquinas (Trinitarian mind analogy, grace/nature, beatific rest); explicit engagement with Plotinus. New concepts: Restless Heart, Memory-Interiority, Time-Eternity. Enormous influence on Western thought and the vault's classical-to-medieval thread.

**Early Modern / Rationalism and Empiricism**:
- [[Thinkers/Thomas Hobbes]] - English political theorist and materialist philosopher (1588–1679). Author of *Leviathan* (1651); developed a mechanistic view of human nature, a bleak concept of the State of Nature, and a contractarian defense of absolute sovereignty.
- [[Thinkers/Descartes]] - René Descartes (1596–1650). *Meditations on First Philosophy* (with Objections and Replies): hyperbolic doubt, cogito as first certainty, causal + ontological proofs of God as non-deceiver, clear and distinct criterion, real distinction of *res cogitans* (thinking substance) and *res extensa* (extended substance) with intimate union in the human composite. Foundational for modern epistemology, rationalism, mind-body dualism, and mechanistic science. Major hinge/rupture and synthesis point for the vault's classical cluster (engages Plato, Aristotle, Augustine, Aquinas, Sextus skepticism, Stoics, Plotinus). New concepts on method, cogito, dualism, God proofs, etc. See dedicated entity + source summary.
- [[Thinkers/Spinoza]] - Benedict de Spinoza (Baruch Espinoza, 1632–1677). *Ethics*: substance monism (*Deus sive Natura*), attribute parallelism (thought and extension as parallel expressions of the one substance), conatus as actual essence and striving to persevere, affects (active from adequate ideas vs. passive passions), geometrical method applied to metaphysics and ethics, freedom as self-determination through adequate ideas, blessedness as the intellectual love of God (*amor Dei intellectualis*) arising from intuitive knowledge. Radical naturalist monist counterpoint and synthesis to Descartes and the classical cluster. Exceptional methodological model for the vault. New concepts on monism, parallelism, conatus, affects, method, freedom/blessedness. See dedicated entity + source summary.
- [[Thinkers/Gottfried Wilhelm Leibniz]] - German rationalist philosopher and mathematician (1646–1716). Developed the monadology (immaterial substance pluralism), Pre-Established Harmony, the Principle of Sufficient Reason, and optimistic theodicy.
- [[Thinkers/Locke]] - John Locke (1632–1704). *An Essay Concerning Human Understanding* (2nd ed. text): the mind at birth as empty cabinet / white paper / tabula rasa (no innate ideas or principles); all ideas and knowledge acquired via sensation and reflection (historical plain method). Book I: full refutation of innate speculative principles (no universal assent; children/idiots lack them; use-of-reason evasion fails) and innate practical/moral principles (conscience shaped by custom; no universal rules; actions > professions; cultural/historical counterexamples; moral rules require proof). Direct predecessor to Hume; primary empiricist counter to Descartes innatism. New entity + 2 concepts (this chunk). See dedicated entity + source summary.
- [[Thinkers/George Berkeley]] - Irish empiricist philosopher and Bishop of Cloyne (1685–1753); developed subjective idealism ("immaterialism") and the maxim *esse est percipi*.
- [[Thinkers/Hume]] - David Hume (1711–76). Scottish Enlightenment; culmination of British empiricism. *Enquiry concerning Human Understanding*: the Copy Principle (ideas copy impressions; the meaning-test "from what impression?"), Hume's Fork (relations of ideas vs. matters of fact), the problem of induction (no rational ground for past→future inference), causation as constant conjunction + the mind's customary transition (two definitions of cause), custom/belief as a lively feeling (the "sceptical solution"; reasoning as "a species of instinct"), compatibilism (liberty and necessity reconciled and both required for morality), the argument against miracles ("proportion belief to the evidence"), the proportionality critique of the design argument, and mitigated (Academical) scepticism ("commit it to the flames"). The empiricist hinge that completes the Early Modern triad and supplies the vault's strongest account of reason's limits; roused Kant from his "dogmatic slumber." Third path on scepticism alongside Sextus (Pyrrhonian) and Descartes (hyperbolic). New entity + 8 concepts. See dedicated entity + source summary.
- [[Thinkers/Kant]] - Immanuel Kant (1724–1804). *Critique of Pure Reason* (A 1781 / B 1787, Guyer/Wood) now primary: Copernican turn; synthetic a priori; transcendental idealism; categories + deduction; Analogies (substance, causality, community); phenomena/noumena; Paralogisms, Antinomies, Ideal; Canon (know / do / hope). *Groundwork* (1785; Wood/Yale + Gregor/Korsgaard): good will; CI system; autonomy; dignity; two standpoints. Responds to Hume; synthesis of rationalism/empiricism. See thinker + three Kant source summaries.

**German Idealism & Post-Kantian Voluntarism (2026-06-11 Ingest)**:
- [[Thinkers/Søren Kierkegaard]] — Søren Aabye Kierkegaard (1813–1855). First primary: *Fear and Trembling* (1843, Hannay). Johannes *de silentio*: faith as double movement; single individual higher than the universal; Abraham or murder. Pseudonymous aesthetic work; *Concept of Anxiety* and *Postscript* still outstanding. | thinker | ethics, philosophy-of-religion, existentialism
- [[Thinkers/Hegel]] - Georg Wilhelm Friedrich Hegel (1770–1831); developed Absolute Idealism, a comprehensive system demonstrating that reality is fundamentally rational, historical, and unified in the dynamic development of *Geist* (Spirit) through the dialectical method. First dedicated ethical opponent now in: [[Thinkers/Søren Kierkegaard]] / [[Contradictions/Kierkegaard vs Hegel - Faith vs Sittlichkeit]]. *Philosophy of Right* still outstanding.
- [[Thinkers/Arthur Schopenhauer]] - German post-Kantian philosopher (1788–1860); noumenal reality is a blind, striving "Will." Primaries: *Fourfold Root* 1847; *On Vision and Colours* 1854; *WWR* I 1819; *WWR* II 1844 Payne (primacy; not-quite-naked; worst world); *Parerga* I–II 1851. He does not call this “pessimism.”

**18th & 19th-Century Political & Ethical Philosophy (2026-06-11 Ingest)**:
- [[Thinkers/Jean-Jacques Rousseau]] - Genevan political philosopher of the Enlightenment (1712–1778); formulated the democratic social contract based on popular sovereignty and the General Will.
- [[Thinkers/John Stuart Mill]] - English utilitarian ethicist and liberal political theorist (1806–1873). Refined utilitarianism with qualitative pleasures and formulated the Harm Principle to limit state/societal interference with individual liberty.
- [[Thinkers/Karl Marx]] - German philosopher, economist, and revolutionary socialist (1818–1883). Co-author of *The Communist Manifesto* (1848); developed historical materialism (economic base determining ideological superstructure) and the theory of class struggle.

**Nietzsche (1844-1900)**:
- [[Thinkers/Nietzsche]] - Friedrich Nietzsche (1844–1900). Primaries: ***The Birth of Tragedy* (1872; Kaufmann)**; ***On Truth and Lies in a Nonmoral Sense* (1873, unpublished; Breazeale)**; *Human, All Too Human* (1878 / 1886 Preface; Zimmern excerpt); *The Gay Science* (1882/87); *Thus Spoke Zarathustra* (1883–85, Common 1909); *Beyond Good and Evil* (1886; Zimmern) complete; *Genealogy* (1887); *The Case of Wagner* (1888); ***Nietzsche Contra Wagner* (1888/89 compilation)**; *Twilight of the Idols* (1888; Kaufmann); ***The Antichrist* (1888; Mencken)** — Book I of the Revaluation. Notebooks: *The Will to Power* (Kaufmann excerpt — Book I + III.I only; not a published book). See dedicated source pages + log.

**American Founding (Publius, 2026-06-04)**:
- [[Thinkers/Publius (Hamilton, Madison, and Jay)]] - Pseudonymous collective author (Alexander Hamilton, James Madison, John Jay) of *The Federalist Papers* (1787–88). Compound-republic design: factions controlled by representation + extent/variety of interests (No. 10); "ambition must be made to counteract ambition" + double security (No. 51); judiciary as weakest branch, judicial review for a limited Constitution (No. 78). Model of disciplined collective synthesis voice; directly applicable to checked/agentic governance.

**American Pragmatism (James, 2026-06-04)**:
- [[Thinkers/William James]] - American philosopher/psychologist (1842–1910). Two primaries: *Varieties* (1902) — personal religion, fruits not roots, healthy-minded/sick soul, conversion/subliminal, four marks, piecemeal supernaturalism — and *Pragmatism* (1907) — method, genetic theory of truth, tender/tough, pluralism, humanism, meliorism. Do not flatten 1902 into 1907. *Principles of Psychology* still outstanding.

**20th-Century Analytic Philosophy (Russell, 2026-06-04)**:
- [[Thinkers/Wilfrid Sellars]] - Wilfrid Sellars (1912–1989), American philosopher; *Empiricism and the Philosophy of Mind* (1956). Killed **the Given** (the demand that episodes be both epistemically independent and justifying); **psychological nominalism** ("all awareness … is a linguistic affair"); the **space of reasons** (§36); *scientia mensura* (§41); the **Myth of Jones** (inner episodes as theoretical posits that later gain a reporting role). Rorty: the third seminal post-positivist work with Quine's "Two Dogmas" and Wittgenstein's *Investigations*; the attempt "to usher analytic philosophy out of its Humean and into its Kantian phase." Direct ancestor of McDowell and Brandom. New entity + 6 concepts + 1 argument. | thinker | epistemology, philosophy-of-mind, philosophy-of-language, philosophy-of-science
- [[Thinkers/Quentin Skinner]] - Quentin Skinner (b. 1940), Regius Professor of Modern History, Cambridge; leading theorist of the **Cambridge School**. Texts are acts: recover what an author was *doing* in writing, not timeless doctrine. The mythologies of doctrines/coherence/prolepsis; motives vs intentions; bracketing truth in belief-explanation; the innovating ideologist and legitimation; *paradiastole*. "There are no perennial problems in philosophy. There are only individual answers to individual questions." Principal antagonist of [[Thinkers/Arthur O. Lovejoy]]; ally-with-a-difference of [[Thinkers/Michel Foucault]] and [[Thinkers/Herbert Butterfield]]. Unusually candid self-corrections in ch. 10. | thinker | history-of-ideas, philosophy-of-language, methodology, political-philosophy
- [[Thinkers/J.L. Austin]] - J. L. Austin (1911–1960), Oxford; *How to Do Things with Words* (1962). Separated the **meaning** of an utterance from its **force**: illocutionary (what we do *in* saying) vs perlocutionary (what we bring about *by* saying), plus **uptake**. Reception-only page built from Skinner's use of him — the primary text is not in the vault. Skinner corrects him twice: illocutionary acts are individuated by *intentions*, not conventions, and there are no unintentional illocutionary acts. Upstream of [[Thinkers/John Searle]]. | thinker | philosophy-of-language
- [[Thinkers/Robert Brandom]] - Robert Brandom (b. 1950), Pittsburgh; author of the Study Guide to the Harvard EPM and of **inferentialism** (conceptual content as premise-and-conclusion role). The **two-ply account of observation**: a reliable differential responsive disposition (which "photocells and parrots could satisfy") *plus* the normative dimension of endorsement; the mu-meson case (what fixes what is seen is the inferential role of the concept noninferentially applied). Built here from the Study Guide only — *Making It Explicit* is not in the vault. | thinker | philosophy-of-language, epistemology, philosophy-of-mind
- [[Thinkers/Gilbert Ryle]] - Gilbert Ryle (1900–1976), Oxford; *The Concept of Mind* (1949), "the ghost in the machine," achievement words. Enters the vault as Sellars's foil: the **Rylean language** is the starting point of the Myth of Jones, and Sellars's whole point is that one can accept Wittgenstein against self-authenticating episodes without accepting Ryle against inner episodes. Reception-only page — *The Concept of Mind* is not itself ingested. | thinker | philosophy-of-mind, philosophy-of-language
- [[Thinkers/Bertrand Russell]] - British philosopher, logician, mathematician (1872–1970); co-founder of analytic philosophy, co-author of *Principia Mathematica*, Nobel Literature 1950. Author of the survey *A History of Western Philosophy* (1945). Empiricist heir (Locke/Hume) turned logical analyst: philosophy as the "No Man's Land" between theology and science; social cohesion vs. individual liberty + liberalism; logical analysis as piecemeal scientific method; the "cosmic impiety" critique of pragmatism's theory of truth. Opinionated verdicts on nearly every vault thinker (Spinoza highest, Aquinas/Plato/Nietzsche lowest). **Two ingested works**: the survey *History* (1945) and the primary epistemology *The Problems of Philosophy* (1912 — sense-data realism, acquaintance/description, correspondence theory of truth, the value of philosophy). Entity + 3 *History* concepts + 4 *Problems* concepts.
- [[Thinkers/Ludwig Wittgenstein]] - Austrian-British philosopher (1889–1951); key figure in 20th-century analytic philosophy and philosophy of language. Author of *Tractatus Logico-Philosophicus* (1922), which presented the Picture Theory of Language, showing that language mirrors the logical structure of facts, and defined the limits of what can be meaningfully said. In *Philosophical Investigations* (1953) the "later Wittgenstein" repudiated that system in favour of meaning-as-use, language-games, family resemblance, rule-following, the private-language argument, and forms of life — see [[Contradictions/Early vs Later Wittgenstein]].
- [[Thinkers/A.J. Ayer]] - British philosopher (1910–1989); principal English-language voice of logical positivism; *Language, Truth and Logic* (1936). Verification principle, emotive ethics, all a priori as tautological, philosophy as logic of science. Vienna Circle synthesizer; Hume's most direct 20th-c. heir; the target Popper/Quine explicitly react against. Emotive ethics now has its 1923 ancestor in Ogden–Richards.
- [[Thinkers/C. K. Ogden]] - Cambridge linguist and International Library editor; with Richards, *The Meaning of Meaning* (1923). Triangle of reference; word-magic; later Basic English, Bentham's fictions. Also sits behind the vault's *Tractatus* as series editor/translator.
- [[Thinkers/I. A. Richards]] - Literary critic and semanticist; with Ogden, *The Meaning of Meaning*. Owns the emotive/literary half (Beauty as test-bed; *Practical Criticism* named, not ingested).
- [[Thinkers/Bronislaw Malinowski]] - Trobriand field anthropologist; Supplement I coins context of situation and phatic communion; language as a mode of action.
- [[Thinkers/C. S. Peirce]] - First dedicated page is an Appendix D *dossier* (sign–object–interpretant; icon/index/symbol; type/token), not a Peirce primary. James's Peirce remains the 1878 maxim.

**Philosophy of Science / Critical Rationalism (Popper, 2026-06-04)**:
- [[Thinkers/Karl Popper]] - Karl Raimund Popper (1902–1994), Austrian-British philosopher; founder of **critical rationalism**. Two primary works ingested: *The Logic of Scientific Discovery* (1934/59) and *Conjectures and Refutations* (1963). Science grows by **bold conjectures + severe attempted refutations**; **falsifiability** demarcates the empirical (not verifiability); accepts Hume's induction problem but bypasses it methodologically; **corroboration** (not probability/verification); **no authoritative sources** of knowledge; **scientific realism/conjecturalism** (vs. essentialism + instrumentalism); **verisimilitude** (truthlikeness) as the aim; the Presocratic critical tradition; and a **political philosophy** against historicism and for the open society / piecemeal engineering. The vault's anchor for the Philosophy of Science / Epistemology leg; ally of Russell (correspondence truth), opponent of James (pragmatic truth), generous critic-heir of Kant. (*Catalog line added 2026-06-04 to fix prior index drift; entity created during the LSD ingest.*)
- [[Thinkers/Thomas Kuhn]] - Thomas S. Kuhn (1922–1996), American historian/philosopher of science (PhD physics, Harvard; Berkeley/Princeton/MIT). *The Structure of Scientific Revolutions* (1962). The **major foil to Popper**: science alternates between paradigm-bound [[Concepts/Normal Science and Puzzle-Solving (Kuhn)|normal science]] and rare [[Concepts/Scientific Revolutions and Paradigm Shifts (Kuhn)|revolutions]]; [[Concepts/Paradigm (Kuhn)|paradigms]] (disciplinary matrix + exemplar) constitute scientific communities; rivals are [[Concepts/Incommensurability (Kuhn)|incommensurable]]; progress is goal-less "evolution-from." Denies bare falsification; makes normal-science dogmatism functional. The vault's first historian/sociologist of science; post-positivist. See [[Contradictions/Popper vs Kuhn - Falsification vs Paradigms]].
- [[Thinkers/Herbert Butterfield]] - Herbert Butterfield (1900–1979), British historian (Master of Peterhouse, Professor of Modern History, Cambridge). *The Origins of Modern Science 1300-1800* (1949): the classic framing of the "Scientific Revolution" as the pivotal transformation (change in "habitual mental operations," overthrow of ancient/medieval authority) that originated the modern world and mentality and outshone Renaissance/Reformation. Emphasises recovery of older systems, misfires/hurdles, and science's later directing role; civilisational centre shifts north. Major historiographical background for the Phil of Science leg (Kuhn as revision). New entity + source + concept. See dedicated source page + log.
- [[Thinkers/W.V.O. Quine]] - Willard Van Orman Quine (1908–2000), American philosopher and logician. "Two Dogmas of Empiricism" (1951/1961). Foundational critique of the analytic/synthetic distinction and reductionism (the dogmas of logical empiricism); confirmation holism (web of belief); physical objects etc. as pragmatic posits on a par with gods; ontological questions continuous with science; shift to naturalized epistemology and thorough pragmatism. Directly engages Hume's Fork, Carnap/verificationism, Russell on meaning; productive tensions with Popper (holism vs. localized falsification) and affinities with Kuhn (meaning change, underdetermination). Other pole on modality vs [[Thinkers/Saul Kripke]] — see [[Contradictions/Kripke vs Quine - De Re Necessity vs Holism]]. The vault's primary post-positivist/holist analytic philosopher. See dedicated source + 4 concepts + log.
- [[Thinkers/Paul Feyerabend]] - Paul Feyerabend (1924–1994), Austrian-born philosopher of science; the **epistemological anarchist** and great provocateur. *Against Method* (1975). Popper's apostate student, Kuhn's Berkeley colleague, Lakatos's friend/antagonist. Theses: no fixed method ("[[Concepts/Epistemological Anarchism (Feyerabend)|anything goes]]"); [[Concepts/Counterinduction (Feyerabend)|counterinduction]]; theory-laden [[Concepts/Natural Interpretations and the Galileo Case (Feyerabend)|natural interpretations]] (the Galileo case); [[Concepts/Proliferation and Tenacity (Feyerabend)|proliferation + tenacity]] (Millian pluralism); science as one tradition, to be [[Concepts/Separation of Science and State (Feyerabend)|separated from the state]]. The radical limit of the rationalism↔anarchism spectrum; sharpest opponent of Popper's verisimilitude. See [[Contradictions/Popper vs Kuhn - Falsification vs Paradigms]].
- [[Thinkers/Ian Hacking]] - Ian Hacking (1936–2023), Canadian philosopher of science. *Representing and Intervening* (1983). Distinguishes [[Concepts/Entity Realism vs Theory Realism (Hacking)|entity realism]] (the objects really exist and can be used as tools) from theory realism; the emblematic "[[Concepts/If You Can Spray Them Then They Are Real (Hacking)|if you can spray them, then they are real]]" (electrons/positrons as reliable interveners). Experiment has a life of its own, creates phenomena, observation is a skill. Engages Kuhn (incommensurability as irrealist pressure; Hacking wrote the intro to the ingested *Structure* ed.), Feyerabend, Popper (realism), and Lakatos (surrogate truth). New entity + 5 concepts; extends the central contradiction page with experimental pivot. See [[Sources/Representing and Intervening - Ian Hacking (1983)]] + [[Contradictions/Popper vs Kuhn - Falsification vs Paradigms]].

**Philosophy of Computation & AI (Turing, 2026-06-04)**:
- [[Thinkers/Alan Turing]] - Alan Mathison Turing (1912–1954). "Computing Machinery and Intelligence" (*Mind*, 1950): reformulates "Can machines think?" as the Imitation Game (operational behavioral test); proves digital computers are universal machines (can mimic any discrete-state process given storage + programme); predicts practical success (~10^9 storage by ~2000 for <70% interrogator accuracy after 5 min) and eventual linguistic acceptance of "machines thinking"; rebuts nine objections (Jefferson consciousness the most persistent; Lady Lovelace originality); outlines learning-machine programme (child machines + education, evolution analogy). Primary source initiating the vault's AI & Cognitive Science leg. See dedicated source-summary + 6 concepts.
- [[Thinkers/Claude Shannon]] - Claude Elwood Shannon (1916–2001). "A Mathematical Theory of Communication" (Bell System Technical Journal, 1948): defines information as uncertainty (entropy H = −∑ p log p in bits); models communication with noise; proves noiseless and noisy channel coding theorems (reliable transmission up to capacity C); shows English has ~50% redundancy. The communication foundation complementing Turing (info + computation). See dedicated source-summary + 6 concepts (Entropy, Channel Capacity, Redundancy, Equivocation, the two coding theorems). Grounds modern AI prediction, context, and reliable generation.
- [[Thinkers/John Searle]] - John Rogers Searle (1932– ). *Minds, Brains and Science* (1984 Reith Lectures): biological naturalism (mental states caused by and realized in brain processes); Chinese Room argument refuting strong AI (syntax is not sufficient for semantics/understanding/intentionality). Major primary critique of the computational theory of mind. See dedicated source-summary + 5 concepts (Chinese Room Argument, Strong AI, Syntax and Semantics, Biological Naturalism, Intentionality). Direct engagement with and counter to Turing computationalism and strong AI readings of the Imitation Game.
- [[Thinkers/Douglas Hofstadter]] - Douglas Hofstadter (b. 1945). *Gödel, Escher, Bach* (1979, Pulitzer 1980): strange loops and tangled hierarchies as structural mechanism of consciousness; meaning from isomorphism; Gödel incompleteness as mirror of mind; reductionism/holism dissolved by tangled hierarchy. The vault's primary computationalist voice and the direct counterweight to Searle in the AI leg. See dedicated source-summary + 5 concepts + [[Contradictions/Hofstadter vs Searle - Strange Loops vs Chinese Room]].
- [[Thinkers/Kurt Gödel]] - Kurt Gödel (1906–1978). 1931 incompleteness theorems: any consistent formal system containing arithmetic is (essentially) incomplete and cannot prove its own consistency. Gödel numbering + self-referential G ("G is not provable") as the technical heart. Platonist about mathematical truth. Primary source for the mathematical objection (limits of formal systems/machines) that Turing rebuts and that Hofstadter reinterprets as the signature of self-reference enabling mind. See dedicated source [[Sources/Gödel’s Proof - Ernest Nagel and James R. Newman (1958)]] (the clearest exposition) + cross to Hofstadter and Turing pages.
- [[Thinkers/Ernest Nagel]] - Ernest Nagel (1901–1985). Co-author (with James R. Newman) of *Gödel’s Proof* (1958/2001), the canonical accessible exposition of the incompleteness theorems. Philosopher of science; personal mentor/friend to the young Hofstadter. See dedicated source + new thinker pages for co-author Newman and subject Gödel.
- [[Thinkers/James R. Newman]] - James R. Newman (1907–1966). Mathematician, writer, editor of *The World of Mathematics*; co-author with Nagel of *Gödel’s Proof*. His lucid popularization made the 1931 result part of general culture and directly enabled the vault's engagement with self-reference and the mechanization debate.
- [[Thinkers/Marvin Minsky]] - Marvin Minsky (1927–2016). *The Society of Mind* (1986): mind as society of many simple, mindless "agents" (no central self or program); memory via K-lines (reactivation of prior agent states); knowledge via frames/uniframes; Papert’s Principle on administrative reorganization for growth. MIT AI Lab co-founder; concrete mechanistic account of distributed/emergent mind that converges with Dennett (Multiple Drafts, no Cartesian Theater) and Hofstadter (strange loops/emergence) while nuancing Searle's "strong AI" attributions. New entity + source + 4 concepts.
- [[Thinkers/Nick Bostrom]] - Nick Bostrom (b. 1973), Oxford philosopher and Director of the Future of Humanity Institute. Two vault-primary contributions: the 2003 simulation argument (a trilemma, even split, not a proof we are sims) and *Superintelligence* (2014: orthogonality, instrumental convergence, control problem). Horn (1) of 2003 is the catastrophe 2014 treats as default — the two arguments trade probability. Successor programme: [[Thinkers/Stuart Russell]]. | thinker | ai, existential-risk, metaphysics
- [[Thinkers/Stuart Russell]] — Stuart Russell (b. 1962), UC Berkeley; AIMA co-author. *Human Compatible* (2019): recants the textbook definition of machine intelligence; three principles + assistance games as the control programme Bostrom left open. | thinker | ai, existential-risk, ethics
- [[Thinkers/Melanie Mitchell]] - Melanie Mitchell, computer scientist (Portland State, Santa Fe Institute); PhD with/worked with Douglas Hofstadter. *Artificial Intelligence: A Guide for Thinking Humans* (2019): history (symbolic vs. connectionist, perceptrons, DL); narrow successes via pattern matching (vision, games, translation) but brittleness, lack of common sense/analogy/understanding; hype cycles and skepticism of singularity; personal account of 2014 Google meeting with Hofstadter. Key "reality check" updating GEB vision and cross-cutting the AI cluster. New entity + source + 4 concepts.
- [[Thinkers/Rizwan Virk]] - MIT-trained computer scientist and game entrepreneur (Play Labs @ MIT); *The Simulation Hypothesis* (2019). Reads the simulation hypothesis through game engineering and Eastern mysticism; champions the RPG version against what he takes to be Bostrom's NPC reading — the 2003 paper is officially neutral. Popularizer-synthesist; contested physics readings flagged. | thinker | ai, metaphysics, religion
- [[Thinkers/Ray Kurzweil]] - Ray Kurzweil (b. 1948), inventor/futurist (OCR, text-to-speech; now Google AI). *The Singularity Is Near* (2005): Singularity ~2045 via Law of Accelerating Returns (exponential tech), GNR (Genetics/Nanotech/Robotics/strong AI), mind uploading; nonbio intelligence 1B x all human today; human-machine merger/transcendence of biology. The canonical optimistic/singularitarian text; explicitly engaged by Mitchell (2014 Google meeting with Kurzweil/hype, Hofstadter's "terror"; her corrective) and Bostrom (critiques with orthogonality/risks). Builds on Turing/Minsky; echoes in 2026 capability sources. New entity + source + 4 concepts.
- [[Thinkers/Kevin Kelly]] - Kevin Kelly (b. 1952), Wired co-founder, techno-philosopher (technium thesis). *What Technology Wants* (2010): technium as seventh kingdom of life with evolutionary "wants" (self-perpetuation, exotropy, convergence, increasing choices); cosmic arc and human co-evolution/amplification; choices within wants (Amish model); infinite game. *The Inevitable* (2016): 12 forces as digital expressions of those wants; protopian embrace + manage; holos and Beginning. Now 2 sources + 7+ concepts; cross to Kurzweil (acceleration as exotropic), Bostrom (wants vs risks/orthogonality), Mitchell (co-evolution vs limits/hype), Wiener (control), Lovejoy (dynamic technium vs static Chain). See dedicated sources + log.
- [[Thinkers/Martin Ford]] - Martin Ford, Silicon Valley entrepreneur and author. *Rise of the Robots* (2015): "Is This Time Different?" — general-purpose IT + AI displacing predictable jobs (white-collar included) at scale; post-1973 productivity-wage decoupling, jobless recoveries, consumer demand risk ("Machines Do Not Consume"). Calls for basic income guarantee as new paradigm. Engages Singularity/super-intelligence debates. +1 thinker + source + 3 concepts; cross to Kelly (cognifying produces the unemployment), Wiener (Second IR update), Bostrom (risks), Kurzweil (Ch9), Mitchell (capabilities vs. limits). See dedicated source + log.
- [[Thinkers/Michio Kaku]] - Michio Kaku (b. 1947), theoretical physicist (string field theory co-founder), CUNY, popularizer. *Physics of the Future* (2011): 300+ scientist interviews, physics laws (4 forces/quantum) basis for 2100 predictions (Type I planetary civ per Kardashev ~100 years; Ch1 mind-over-matter BCI/superconductors; Ch2 AI rise with limits (pattern/common sense, ASIMO "insect IQ," Deep Blue brute ≠ intelligence, Moore slowing); medicine/nanotech/energy (stars)/space/wealth/humanity (Type II/III, von Neumann probes, entropy); "A Day in the Life in 2100." Measured optimism (hype vs. reality; resistance). +1 thinker + source + 3 concepts; cross to Kelly (forces as 2100 realizations), Ford (automation complement), Bostrom (risks), Kurzweil (acceleration measured), Mitchell (limits exact), Wiener (cybernetics), Hofstadter/Dennett/Turing (brain vs. digital). See dedicated source + log.
- [[Thinkers/Norbert Wiener]] - Norbert Wiener (1894–1964), MIT mathematician, founder of cybernetics. *The Human Use of Human Beings* (1950, rev. 1954): cybernetics as unified science of control and communication; organism = pattern not matter; humans as learning beings (anti-ant/fascism); alignment problem (Monkey's Paw / Djinnee; "the machine will in no way be obliged to make decisions we should have made"); second IR = machines displacing judgment = slave-labor equivalent; information is not a commodity; know-what before know-how. Shares entropy mathematics with Shannon (H = −∑ p log p) but insists on semantic/social dimension Shannon brackets. See source-summary + 6 concepts + [[Contradictions/Wiener vs Shannon - Information and Meaning]].
- [[Thinkers/Daniel Dennett]] - Daniel C. Dennett (1942–2024), Tufts University Distinguished Professor. *Consciousness Explained* (1991): Multiple Drafts Model (no Cartesian Theater — consciousness is parallel distributed editorial processes with no canonical final draft); heterophenomenology (neutral third-person method; subjects' reports = theorist's fiction); qualia deflated (colors coevolved with vision; Mary already knows; inverted qualia presuppose Theater; epiphenomenal qualia incoherent); self = Center of Narrative Gravity (abstraction, not brain-pearl); Joycean virtual machine (culturally-installed serial VM on parallel brain hardware; memes as software; Baldwin Effect as evolutionary feedback); Systems Reply as canonical rejoinder to Searle's Chinese Room. The vault's primary naturalized functionalist voice on consciousness. *Freedom Evolves* (2003) cited approvingly by Pearl for compatibilist free will. See source-summary + 6 concepts + [[Contradictions/Dennett vs Nagel - Multiple Drafts vs Subjective Experience]].
- [[Thinkers/Judea Pearl]] - Judea Pearl (b. 1936), Israeli-American computer scientist at UCLA; Turing Award 2011. *The Book of Why* (2018): the Causal Revolution; Ladder of Causation (rung 1 association / rung 2 intervention / rung 3 counterfactuals); do-operator and do-calculus (graph surgery; 3 rules; Shpitser completeness); causal diagrams/DAGs; back-door and front-door criteria; structural causal models; mediation (NDE/NIE). "Data are profoundly dumb." Deep learning stuck at rung 1; mini-Turing test (causal reasoning as the AI criterion). Historical: Sewall Wright suppressed; Galton/Pearson purged causation. See source-summary + 6 concepts + [[Contradictions/Pearl vs. Statistics Orthodoxy - Causation Purged and Recovered]].

**Classical China (Analects + Daodejing, 2026-08-12)**:
- [[Thinkers/Confucius]] — Kong Qiu of Lu (551–479 BC). Chin 2014 *Analects*: no theories; humaneness as beginning and end; restrain the self and return to the rites; rectify names first; stay among human beings. First Confucian. Mencius and Xunzi still outstanding. | thinker | ethics, political-philosophy, philosophy-of-language
- [[Thinkers/Laozi]] — Traditional name ("Old Master") for the position of the *Daodejing*; not a securely historical author. Ziporyn 2023: ironic course, unlearned virtuosity, nondoing, unhewn A/B reversal, water/low place. The vault's first Daoist. Confucian counterpart now in; *Zhuangzi* still outstanding. | thinker | metaphysics, ethics, political-philosophy, philosophy-of-language

**Strategy & Statecraft (Sun Tzu, 2026-06-04)**:
- [[Thinkers/Sun Tzu]] - Chinese general and strategist (Sun Wu, fl. c. 544–496 BCE), advisor to King Ho Lu of Wu; reputed author of *The Art of War*. The vault's first strategist and first non-Western classical author. Doctrine of victory through [[Concepts/Calculation and the Five Factors (Sun Tzu)|calculation]], [[Concepts/Winning Without Fighting (Sun Tzu)|bloodless victory]], [[Concepts/Know the Enemy and Know Yourself (Sun Tzu)|self-and-enemy knowledge]], [[Concepts/All Warfare Is Based on Deception (Sun Tzu)|deception]], [[Concepts/Shih - Momentum, Formlessness, and Striking Weakness (Sun Tzu)|momentum/formlessness]], and [[Concepts/Foreknowledge and the Use of Spies (Sun Tzu)|foreknowledge]]. Realist pairing with Thucydides (prescriptive vs. descriptive) and Kasparov (chess); restraint-from-passion converges partly with just-war prudence. Taoist resonance now grounded in [[Thinkers/Laozi]] (sibling images, opposed uses). New entity + 6 concepts.

**Strategy, Craft & Mastery (MasterClass 2026 batch)**:
- [[Thinkers/Garry Kasparov]] - Greatest chess player of his era (youngest world champion at 22); Deep Blue matches and *Deep Thinking* reflections on where machine intelligence ends and human creativity begins; deliberate practice, calculation, and strategic judgment. Bridges AI/agentic work, career strategy/playbooks, and the broader craft mastery themes from the 2026 collection.

**Phenomenology & Existentialism (Heidegger VSI, 2026-06-29)**:
- [[Thinkers/Søren Kierkegaard]] — also listed under German Idealism. First primary *Fear and Trembling*: Abraham, double movement, teleological suspension. Ancestor Sartre and Heidegger already used.
- [[Thinkers/Michel Foucault]] - Michel Foucault (1926–1984), French philosopher and historian of thought; diagnostician of power, knowledge, and the self. Three phases: archaeology, genealogy, ethics of the self. Primaries: *Discipline and Punish* (1975); *History of Sexuality* I (1976) — repressive hypothesis, deployment of sexuality, bio-power. Biography: [[Sources/Michel Foucault - David Macey]]. Late ethics now has its named interlocutor: [[Thinkers/Pierre Hadot]]. | thinker | epistemology, political-philosophy, ethics, history, poststructuralism
- [[Thinkers/Pierre Hadot]] - French historian of ancient philosophy (1922–2010); EPHE then Collège de France. Greco-Roman *philosophia* as a way of life practiced through spiritual exercises; discourse *about* philosophy is not philosophy; creative mistakes in exegesis; critique of Foucault's aesthetics of existence. | thinker | ethics, metaphilosophy, ancient-philosophy
- [[Thinkers/Maurice Merleau-Ponty]] — Maurice Merleau-Ponty (1908–1961). *Phenomenology of Perception* (1945; Landes 2012): one's own body at the center of perception; empiricism and intellectualism share the constancy hypothesis; consciousness is originarily an *I can*; freedom gears into a sedimented situation. French third way between Husserl's constituting ego and Sartre's nothingness. | thinker | phenomenology, philosophy-of-mind, epistemology, ontology
- [[Thinkers/Jean-Paul Sartre]] - Jean-Paul Sartre (1905–1980), French philosopher, novelist, playwright, and political activist. Systematic primary now in: *Being and Nothingness* (1943, Barnes) — pre-reflective cogito, two regions, néantisation, bad faith, the Look, freedom-in-situation, useless passion. Public primary: *Existentialism is a Humanism* (1945/46). Later *Critique*: totalisation, series/group, fraternity-terror; anti-colonial counter-violence with Fanon. Abraham of the lecture now answerable from [[Sources/Fear and Trembling - Kierkegaard (Hannay 1985)]]. | thinker | existentialism, phenomenology, ontology, ethics, political-philosophy
- [[Thinkers/Martin Heidegger]] - Martin Heidegger (1889–1976), German philosopher. *Being and Time* (1927): the question of Being and the ontological difference; Dasein as being-in-the-world (anti-Cartesian); ready-to-hand vs. present-at-hand; truth as unconcealment (*aletheia*); care (*Sorge*); being-toward-death, anxiety, conscience, resoluteness; ecstatic temporality (four levels); historicality. Later philosophy: the Turn (*Kehre*); art as truth-event (*Origin of the Work of Art*); "language speaks, not man." With Wittgenstein, the most consequential 20th-c. philosopher. Contested: Nazism, absence of normative criteria in resoluteness, BT's incompleteness. New thinker + 10 concepts + Phenomenology school. | thinker | ontology, phenomenology, existentialism, aesthetics

**Public Choice & Political Economy (Mancur Olson, Buchanan & Tullock, 2026-06-29 Ingest)**:
- [[Thinkers/Gilles Deleuze]] - French philosopher (1925–1995); creator of concepts that replace Being with difference and identity with transformation. Major works: *Difference and Repetition*, *The Logic of Sense*, *Anti-Oedipus* and *A Thousand Plateaus* (with Guattari), *Cinema 1–2*, *Francis Bacon: The Logic of Sensation*. The vault's first poststructuralist/continental philosopher after existentialism. | thinker | metaphysics, ethics, political-philosophy, aesthetics
- [[Thinkers/Mancur Olson]] - American economist (1932–1998); key developer of public choice theory. Authored *The Logic of Collective Action* (1965) and *The Rise and Decline of Nations* (1982), demonstrating why large groups face free-rider problems and how stable democracies accumulate growth-repressing cartels and coalitions. | thinker | political-philosophy, economics, sociology
- [[Thinkers/James M. Buchanan]] - American economist (1919–2013); co-founder of public choice theory and constitutional political economy. Awarded the 1986 Nobel Prize for his contractual and constitutional analyses of economic and political decision-making. | thinker | political-philosophy, economics, sociology
- [[Thinkers/Gordon Tullock]] - American economist and legal scholar (1922–2014); co-founder of public choice theory. Foundational contributor to law and economics and the theory of rent-seeking and bureaucracy. Coined "rational ignorance." | thinker | political-philosophy, economics, law-and-economics
- [[Thinkers/Bryan Caplan]] - American economist (b. 1971), George Mason University; public choice theorist. *The Myth of the Rational Voter* (2007): voters are rationally irrational — they hold systematically biased economic beliefs because false political beliefs are individually costless. Revises Downs/Tullock (rational ignorance → rational irrationality) and defeats Wittman's defense of democracy. | thinker | political-philosophy, economics, epistemology

**Wittgenstein's Philosophical Investigations - A Critical Guide (Arif Ahmed, 2026-06-29)**:
- [[Thinkers/Richard Rorty]] - American pragmatist and neopragmatist philosopher (1931–2007); known for his critique of the representationalist tradition and his metaphilosophical distinction between therapeutic and pragmatic readings of the later Wittgenstein. | thinker | epistemology, metaphilosophy, pragmatism
- [[Thinkers/John McDowell]] - Contemporary philosopher (1942–present); synthesized analytic philosophy with Kantian/Hegelian idealism, defending the conceptual nature of experience and a practice-based, non-skeptical view of rule-following. | thinker | epistemology, philosophy-of-mind, philosophy-of-language

*(More entities and sub-pages will be added on future ingests or when specific people/artifacts become central. 0B Gemini/Google/NotebookLM placeholders noted in Sources; no new entities from empty files. Kant added as the a priori deontological foundation responding to Hume ("dogmatic slumber") and contrasting Locke empiricism, Aristotle eudaimonia, and Spinoza conatus; Locke added as the foundational British empiricist (tabula rasa, anti-innatism) grounding the Hume material and contrasting the Descartes/Spinoza/classical threads; Plotinus added as Neoplatonism capstone to classical; Augustine added as pivotal patristic bridge with new Confessions source + 3 concepts.)*

### Concepts

- [[Concepts/Mythologies in the History of Ideas (Skinner)]] — The catalogue of errors from reading the canon for "dateless wisdom": mythology of **doctrines** (incidental remarks promoted to doctrines; Lovejoy's unit-ideas hypostasised so that "ideas get up to do battle on their own behalf"; writers blamed for not producing a doctrine they never intended), of **coherence** (overriding authors' own statements; Strauss reading between the lines), of **prolepsis** ("the episode has to await the future to learn its meaning"), plus two forms of **parochialism** (spurious influence; misdescribed sense). Constraint: no agent meant something they "could never be brought to accept as a correct description" — such questions are "void for lack of reference." | concept | history-of-ideas, methodology
- [[Concepts/Illocutionary Force and Historical Meaning (Skinner)]] — Skinner's Austin-derived machinery. Three senses of meaning (what the words mean / what it means to me / what the writer meant); **motives** (antecedent, outside the work) vs **intentions in** writing (a feature of it); intention *to do* vs intention *in* doing; illocutionary vs perlocutionary. Illocutionary intentions = meaning₃. He is **not** an intentionalist about textual meaning, rejects Hirsch, and accepts Ricoeur's surplus meaning. Irony (Defoe) is the test case. Answers Hollis ("the cap fits, but was the author wearing it?") and Derrida ("haunted by the ghost of Descartes"). | concept | philosophy-of-language, history-of-ideas
- [[Concepts/Explaining Beliefs Without Truth (Skinner)]] — "The concept of truth is irrelevant to the enterprise of explaining beliefs." Ask whether it was rational for that agent to hold it true by then-prevailing norms; rationality is "an achievement" needing its own causal explanation. Charity identifies subject-matter without requiring mostly-true beliefs; Bloor's impartiality accepted, symmetry rejected; symbolic and Freudian readings resisted because the only criterion offered is "our own cognitive discomfort." Relativises *holding true*, not truth. | concept | history-of-ideas, epistemology
- [[Concepts/Innovating Ideologist and Legitimation (Skinner)]] — Against Namier and vulgar Marxism: professed principles are not idle even when insincere. An agent doing something questionable must **legitimise** it using *existing* favourable terms — "all revolutionaries are to this extent obliged to march backwards into battle." Principles are therefore "among the enabling conditions" of action and constrain its range. Weber defended against Trevor-Roper (Protestantism legitimised rather than caused capitalism). Ch. 7 supplies the groundwork: illocutionary redescription explains an action's **point**. | concept | history-of-ideas, political-philosophy
- [[Concepts/Rhetorical Redescription and Paradiastole (Skinner)]] — The later Skinner's central technique, from Quintilian and Cicero: redescribe with a "neighbouring" term — prodigality as liberality, recklessness as courage — and in reverse. Found in Machiavelli (*Il Principe* 16–17), Castiglione's *sprezzatura*, and unrecognised in Nietzsche's *Genealogy* I. **Also the chapter of self-corrections**: evaluative terms have no "accepted denotations," all legislation about correct normative usage is "equally ideological," and he never meant to attack Begriffsgeschichte. | concept | history-of-ideas, rhetoric, ethics
- [[Concepts/Cultural Lexicon - Words vs Concepts (Skinner)]] — Against Raymond Williams's *Keywords*: "what we need to know is not what words they use but rather what concepts they possess." Milton had the concept of originality before the word; one may falsely believe one has a concept. Mastering an appraisive term needs three things (criteria of application, range of reference, range of speech acts), so there are three kinds of dispute, not all about meaning. **Polysemy is the trace of ideological failure** — successful arguments yield new social perceptions, not new meanings. | concept | history-of-ideas, philosophy-of-language
- [[Concepts/Myth of the Given (Sellars)]] — The empiricist demand that some episodes be both epistemically independent of all other knowledge and capable of justifying it; nothing can be both. A *framework* error ("sense contents, material objects, universals, propositions, real connections, first principles, even givenness itself"), not a doctrine about one kind of item, and common to "dogmatic rationalism" and "skeptical empiricism" alike. Self-authenticating episodes as "the tortoise on which stands the elephant"; the replacement is "a self-correcting enterprise which can put *any* claim in jeopardy, though not *all* at once." | concept | epistemology, philosophy-of-mind
- [[Concepts/Space of Reasons (Sellars)]] — "In characterizing an episode or a state as that of *knowing*, we are not giving an empirical description of that episode or state; we are placing it in the logical space of reasons, of justifying and being able to justify what one says" (§36). Dissolves the regress objection; generalizes to the claim that epistemic facts cannot be analyzed into non-epistemic ones — "a mistake of a piece with the so-called 'naturalistic fallacy' in ethics." The term McDowell and Brandom build on. | concept | epistemology, philosophy-of-mind
- [[Concepts/Psychological Nominalism (Sellars)]] — "All awareness of *sorts, resemblances, facts* … indeed, all awareness even of particulars — is a linguistic affair" (§29). Locke, Berkeley and Hume disagree about abstract ideas while sharing an unargued assumption of innate awareness of determinate repeatables. Includes the language-learner fallacy and the logic of 'means' ('… means ---' is not a relational statement). | concept | epistemology, philosophy-of-mind, philosophy-of-language
- [[Concepts/The Logic of Looks (Sellars)]] — "*X* looks green to *S*" ascribes a propositional claim to the experience but **withholds endorsement**; "merely looks" rejects it. The necktie shop (John and the electric light); qualitative, existential and merely generic looking on one mechanism; "standard conditions" means "conditions in which things look what they are"; *looking* green presupposes *being* green — hence the break with logical atomism and the "whole battery of concepts" holism. | concept | epistemology, philosophy-of-mind, philosophy-of-language
- [[Concepts/Myth of Jones (Sellars)]] — §§48–63: "anthropological science fiction" in which Jones postulates thoughts (modelled on overt speech) and impressions (modelled on inner replicas), each with a *commentary* restricting the analogy, and then trains a reporting use — "what began as a language with a purely theoretical use has gained a reporting role." Shows anti-foundationalism entails neither behaviorism nor eliminativism; privileged access is real but not absolute, and is *built on* intersubjective status. Impressions are states, not particulars, and are not literally red. | concept | philosophy-of-mind, epistemology, philosophy-of-science
- [[Concepts/Scientia Mensura (Sellars)]] — §41: "in the dimension of describing and explaining the world, science is the measure of all things, of what is that it is, and of what is not that it is not." The "positivistic" demotion of theoretical entities to calculational devices rests on **two mistakes** — the Myth of the Given, and a reification of the methodological theoretical/observational distinction. Science is continuous with common sense. The vault's link between Sellars's epistemology and scientific realism. | concept | philosophy-of-science, metaphysics, epistemology
- [[Concepts/Phenomenal Field and the Return to Phenomena (Merleau-Ponty)]] — Empiricism and intellectualism share the constancy hypothesis; return to the lived world beneath the objective world. Incomplete reduction. | concept | phenomenology, epistemology, philosophy-of-mind
- [[Concepts/One's Own Body and the Body Schema (Merleau-Ponty)]] — *Le corps propre* is that by which there are objects; habitual/actual body; schema is situational, not a body image. | concept | phenomenology, philosophy-of-mind, cognitive-science
- [[Concepts/Motor Intentionality and the Intentional Arc (Merleau-Ponty)]] — Consciousness is originarily an *I can*. Schneider; motor signification; the arc that goes limp. Dreyfus's "maximum grasp" is a reception name for the hold. | concept | phenomenology, philosophy-of-mind, cognitive-science
- [[Concepts/Speech as Gesture (Merleau-Ponty)]] — Speech accomplishes thought; the body is a natural power of expression; speaking vs spoken speech. | concept | phenomenology, philosophy-of-language, philosophy-of-mind
- [[Concepts/Coexistence and the Other (Merleau-Ponty)]] — Others via body-schema correspondence, not analogy. The Look objectifies only as thinking nature. *Intercorporeity* is not this book's word. | concept | phenomenology, philosophy-of-mind, social-philosophy
- [[Concepts/Tacit Cogito (Merleau-Ponty)]] — Cartesian *Cogito* is spoken and second-hand; tacit cogito has a fleeting hold and is a cogito only when expressed. Not Sartre's pre-reflective cogito. | concept | phenomenology, epistemology, philosophy-of-mind
- [[Concepts/Situated Freedom (Merleau-Ponty)]] — Never determinism and never an absolute choice; freedom gears into a sedimented situation; not-refusing is not a choice. | concept | phenomenology, ethics, philosophy-of-action
- [[Concepts/Minimal State and Ultraminimal State (Nozick)]] — Night-watchman state justified from Lockean anarchy via compensation of independents; anything more violates rights. | concept | political-philosophy, ethics
- [[Concepts/Side Constraints (Nozick)]] — Rights constrain action; they are not goals to minimize-in-violation. Kantian form, libertarian content; separateness of persons. | concept | ethics, political-philosophy
- [[Concepts/Experience Machine (Nozick)]] — We want to *do*, to *be*, and to contact a deeper-than-man-made reality. Primary text; Chalmers and Clark are reception. | concept | ethics, value-theory, philosophy-of-mind
- [[Concepts/Invisible-Hand Explanation of the State (Nozick)]] — A dominant protective association, then a state, can arise from Lockean anarchy by a process no one intends. | concept | political-philosophy
- [[Concepts/Entitlement Theory (Nozick)]] — Justice in holdings is historical: acquisition, transfer, rectification. “From each as they choose, to each as they are chosen.” | concept | political-philosophy, ethics
- [[Concepts/Framework for Utopia (Nozick)]] — Utopia is a filter of voluntary communities, equivalent to the minimal state. Design vs filter; communities need not be libertarian. | concept | political-philosophy, ethics

Ideas, methods, technologies, principles, patterns.

- [[Concepts/Ren - Humaneness (Confucius)]] — Chin: *ren* as humaneness, not default "benevolence." Roots in filiality (1.2); without it rites fail (3.3); *keji fuli* is the way (12.1); he will not call himself humane (7.34). | concept | ethics, political-philosophy
- [[Concepts/Li - Ritual Propriety (Confucius)]] — Rites: harmony is prized, rules still bind (1.12); *Odes* stimulate, rites steady, music completes (8.8); Book Ten as embodied deportment. | concept | ethics, political-philosophy
- [[Concepts/Junzi - The Gentleman (Confucius)]] — Usually moral character, sometimes hereditary rank (1.8, 12.19). Not a vessel (2.12); right vs profit (4.16); wind over grass when positional (12.19). | concept | ethics, political-philosophy
- [[Concepts/Xiao - Filiality (Confucius)]] — Root of *ren* (1.2); respect and the face, not feeding (2.7–2.8); fathers and sons cover for each other (13.18); three-year mourning (17.21). | concept | ethics
- [[Concepts/Zhengming - Rectifying Names (Confucius)]] — First task of government (13.3): names → reasonable speech → accomplishment → rites/music → just punishments. Govern is to correct (12.17). | concept | political-philosophy, philosophy-of-language, ethics
- [[Concepts/Shu - Reciprocity (Confucius)]] — One-word lifelong guide (15.24): do not impose what you do not want. Paired with *zhong* as the one thread (4.15). Chin does not call it the Golden Rule. | concept | ethics
- [[Concepts/Dao - The Course (Laozi)]] — Ziporyn: *dao* as "course." Originally a purposive how-to; here nonpurposive process that cannot be taken as *the* right course (ch. 1) and "conforms to how everything is when left to itself" (ch. 25). | concept | metaphysics, ethics, philosophy-of-language
- [[Concepts/De - Virtuosity and Potency (Laozi)]] — Ironic *de*: not skill from practice but unlearned potency. Highest virtuosity is not grasped as virtuosity and does nothing without motives; ritual is the beginning of disorder (ch. 38). | concept | ethics, metaphysics, political-philosophy
- [[Concepts/Wu Wei - Nondoing (Laozi)]] — Not idleness: "doing nothing yet leaving nothing undone" (ch. 37); daily subtraction until nondoing (ch. 48); to do something with it is to ruin it (ch. 64). | concept | ethics, political-philosophy, metaphysics
- [[Concepts/Unhewn Background and the A-B Reversal (Laozi)]] — Ziporyn's through-tone: occupy the ignored B-pole (soft, nameless, empty, unhewn). The unhewn (*pu*) is raw material, stuff of A, and what A returns to. | concept | metaphysics, ethics, philosophy-of-language
- [[Concepts/Water, Softness, and the Low Place (Laozi)]] — Water benefits by never competing (ch. 8); rivers reign by getting below (ch. 66); soft overcomes hard (ch. 78); alive is soft, dead is hard (ch. 76). | concept | ethics, political-philosophy, metaphysics
- [[Concepts/Conscious Access (Dehaene)]] — Tractable core of consciousness: selected information becomes reportable. Vigilance, attention, self, and leftover "phenomenal awareness" are neighboring phenomena. | concept | philosophy-of-mind, cognitive-science, neuroscience
- [[Concepts/Global Neuronal Workspace (Dehaene)]] — Consciousness is brain-wide information sharing: long-distance pyramids broadcast one winning coalition. Baars plus Changeux anatomy; Dennett's "fame in the brain" with a finish line. | concept | philosophy-of-mind, cognitive-science, neuroscience, ai
- [[Concepts/Signatures of Consciousness and Ignition (Dehaene)]] — Late P3, parietal–prefrontal ignition, late gamma, long-distance brain web. Clinic (Owen, local–global P3b, TMS ping); slow workspace in infants and macaques. | concept | philosophy-of-mind, cognitive-science, neuroscience
- [[Concepts/Minimal Contrast Method (Dehaene)]] — Matched seen/unseen trials (masking, rivalry, blink) plus reports as raw data. Laboratory sibling of heterophenomenology. | concept | philosophy-of-mind, cognitive-science, methodology
- [[Concepts/Functions of Conscious Broadcasting (Dehaene)]] — Unconscious processors are parallel statisticians; consciousness samples, holds, routes (biological Turing machine), and in humans shares. Reply to Descartes's two tests. | concept | philosophy-of-mind, cognitive-science, ai
- [[Concepts/Transcendental Idealism (Husserl)]] — Phenomenology executed concretely *is* transcendental idealism (*CM* §41): every imaginable being falls within constituting subjectivity; an outside is "nonsense." Not psychological, not Kantian, not Berkeleyan. Corrects Smith's "meaning, not existence." | concept | phenomenology, ontology, epistemology
