- [[Concepts/Genetic Phenomenology - Active and Passive Genesis (Husserl)]] — After static description: time as form of egological genesis; active production of categorial objects on a passive, associative givenness-beforehand. Association is the innate A priori of passive genesis (*CM* §§37–39). | concept | phenomenology, epistemology, philosophy-of-mind
- [[Concepts/Pairing and Analogical Appresentation (Husserl)]] — How the Other is constituted *in* my monad: second epoché to the sphere of ownness; pairing as passive association; analogical *apperception*, not inference (*CM* §§42–54). | concept | phenomenology, epistemology, philosophy-of-mind, social-philosophy
- [[Concepts/Nature as Internal Principle of Motion (Aristotle)]] — Nature (*physis*) is an internal source of motion and rest *kath' hauto*; form rather than matter; nature acts for an end without deliberating. | concept | philosophy-of-nature
- [[Concepts/Motion as Actuality of the Potential (Aristotle)]] — *Kinēsis* is the fulfilment of the potential *qua* potential, an incomplete actuality. | concept | philosophy-of-nature, metaphysics
- [[Concepts/Chance and Spontaneity (Aristotle)]] — *Tychē* and *automaton* are incidental causes in the for-the-sake-of class, neither always nor for the most part. | concept | philosophy-of-nature
- [[Concepts/The Infinite (Apeiron) (Aristotle)]] — No actual infinite body; the infinite exists as a day exists — always another part. | concept | philosophy-of-nature, mathematics
- [[Concepts/Place (Topos) (Aristotle)]] — Place is the innermost motionless boundary of what contains; void denied; natural places. | concept | philosophy-of-nature
- [[Concepts/Time (Aristotle)]] — Time is number of motion in respect of before and after; no time without change. | concept | philosophy-of-nature, philosophy-of-time
- [[Concepts/GOFAI as Degenerating Research Program (Dreyfus)]] — Facts-plus-rules AI as a Lakatosian dead end: Newell–Simon symbols → SHRDLU boom → commonsense stall → expert systems / CYC → connectionism. Not a negative proof. | concept | ai, philosophy-of-science
- [[Concepts/Four Assumptions of Artificial Reason (Dreyfus)]] — Biological, psychological, epistemological, ontological: the four hypotheses treated as axioms that keep GOFAI optimistic after the record. | concept | ai, philosophy-of-mind, epistemology
- [[Concepts/Commonsense Knowledge Problem (Dreyfus)]] — Not a missing catalog of facts: organization, know-how-as-knowing-that, and situational relevance. Micro-worlds do not compose; frames and CYC still treat the background as an object. | concept | ai, phenomenology, epistemology
- [[Concepts/Skilled Coping and the Five Stages of Expertise (Dreyfus)]] — Novice→expert: acquisition-rules need not operate in later application. The AI use of ready-to-hand. | concept | ai, phenomenology, epistemology
- [[Concepts/Embodied Intelligence and the Role of the Body (Dreyfus)]] — Descartes inverted: what machines lack is an involved, situated, material body, not an immaterial soul. | concept | ai, phenomenology, philosophy-of-mind
- [[Concepts/Enaction (Varela)]] — Cognition is perceptually guided action that enacts a perceiver-dependent world, not recovery of a pregiven one. Color is the paradigm. | concept | philosophy-of-mind, cognitive-science, phenomenology
- [[Concepts/Cartesian Anxiety (Varela)]] — Either an absolute ground or chaos. Objectivism and nihilism are one grasping. | concept | epistemology, philosophy-of-mind
- [[Concepts/Groundlessness and the Middle Way (Varela)]] — Enacted worlds have no fixed substrate. Madhyamaka emptiness ≠ nihilism. | concept | epistemology, Buddhism, ethics
- [[Concepts/Mindfulness-Awareness as Method (Varela)]] — Disciplined presence with embodied everyday experience; not epoché, not introspection. Thompson 2016 recants the inward-observation reading. | concept | phenomenology, cognitive-science
- [[Concepts/Natural Drift (Varela)]] — Evolution as viability and mutual specification, the biological counterpart of enaction. Brooks: use the world as its own model. | concept | biology, cognitive-science
- [[Concepts/Selfless Minds (Varela)]] — Cognitivism discovers a nonunified subject; Abhidharma describes the same as experience. Theoretical no-self without a path is nihilism. | concept | philosophy-of-mind, Buddhism
- [[Concepts/Somatic Marker Hypothesis (Damasio)]] — Learned body-state signals mark predicted outcomes before cost/benefit; emotion assists reason, does not replace it. Iowa Gambling Experiments; myopia for the future. | concept | philosophy-of-mind, cognitive-science, affective-science
- [[Concepts/Emotions vs Feelings (Damasio)]] — Emotion = evaluation + bodily dispositions; feeling = experience of those changes. Primary (Jamesian) vs secondary (prefrontal). Profiles, not stereotyped replicas. | concept | philosophy-of-mind, affective-science
- [[Concepts/Body-Minded Brain and Neural Self (Damasio)]] — No body, never mind. The body is the basic topic of brain representations; the self is that topic reconstructed. | concept | philosophy-of-mind, cognitive-science
- [[Concepts/Descartes' Error (Damasio)]] — The *cogito* taken as celebrating the abyssal split of thinking thing from organism. A cultural reading of the slogan, not Meditation VI. | concept | philosophy-of-mind, epistemology
- [[Concepts/Prediction Error Minimization (Hohwy)]] — Hohwy's name for the one mechanism: the brain's only job is minimizing the mismatch between predicted and actual sensory input. Stricter than Clark's toolkit. | concept | philosophy-of-mind, cognitive-science, neuroscience
- [[Concepts/Perception as Causal Inference (Hohwy)]] — Perception is Bayesian inference from sensory effects to hidden worldly causes; Helmholtzian, hierarchical, skull-bound. | concept | philosophy-of-mind, epistemology, cognitive-science
- [[Concepts/Binding as Inference (Hohwy)]] — Binding recast as shallow common-cause inference: the brain assumes bound objects and treats separate attributes as feedback. | concept | philosophy-of-mind, cognitive-science
- [[Concepts/Cognitive Penetrability (Hohwy)]] — Predicting is not automatically seeing. Higher-level beliefs shape experience only when uncertainty opens the door and the belief predicts at the right grain. | concept | philosophy-of-mind, epistemology, cognitive-science
- [[Concepts/Misrepresentation and the Bayesian Room (Hohwy)]] — Error is a local PEM win that raises long-run error. A Searle-style room that minimizes PE perceives if average mutual information can fall. | concept | philosophy-of-mind, philosophy-of-language
- [[Concepts/Perceptual Unity and Ignition (Hohwy)]] — Conscious perception is the winning first-order inference; unity because one body can act on only one hypothesis. Hard problem declined. | concept | philosophy-of-mind, consciousness
- [[Concepts/Secluded Mind and the Sensory Boundary (Hohwy)]] — PEM is isolated behind a veil of sensory input. Tools tidy the channel; they do not extend the mind. Hohwy never says "Markov blanket." | concept | philosophy-of-mind, epistemology
- [[Concepts/Self as Sensory Trajectory (Hohwy)]] — Self as the agent-bound sensory trajectory a hierarchical model must posit in order to act; emotion as interoceptive inference; privacy for social Bayes. | concept | philosophy-of-mind, cognitive-science
- [[Concepts/Real Problem of Consciousness (Seth)]] — Explain, predict, and control phenomenological properties via embodied brains; dissolve, don't solve, the hard problem (vitalism analogy). | concept | philosophy-of-mind, cognitive-science
- [[Concepts/Phi and Integrated Information (Seth)]] — Every experience is informative *and* integrated; keep that as a measure of level, drop IIT's identity of consciousness with Φ. | concept | philosophy-of-mind, neuroscience
- [[Concepts/Inside-Out Perception (Seth)]] — Perception is a top-down neuronal fantasy reined in by prediction error; utility, not veridicality. | concept | philosophy-of-mind, cognitive-science
- [[Concepts/Beholder's Share (Seth)]] — The perceiver's contribution, not in the artwork or the world; hallucination as strong priors. | concept | philosophy-of-mind, aesthetics
- [[Concepts/Beast Machine Theory (Seth)]] — Descartes inverted: we are conscious selves *because* we are living machines that predict and control essential variables. | concept | philosophy-of-mind, biology
- [[Concepts/Self as Controlled Hallucination (Seth)]] — The self is not the perceiver; it is a six-layer perception for control, not self-knowledge. | concept | philosophy-of-mind
- [[Concepts/Free Will as Controlled Hallucination (Seth)]] — Compatibilist degrees-of-freedom: spooky intervention is unreal; the experience and the capacity are not. | concept | philosophy-of-mind, ethics
- [[Concepts/Animal Consciousness and the Beast Machine (Seth)]] — Life before intelligence: all mammals in; octopuses an independent experiment; nematodes out. | concept | philosophy-of-mind, ethics
- [[Concepts/Machine Minds (Seth)]] — Functionalism unjustified; a silicon PP "beast machine" would probably not be conscious. Life breathes fire into the equations. | concept | philosophy-of-mind, ai
- [[Concepts/Predictive Processing (Clark)]] — The brain as a multilevel prediction machine: it guesses sensory causes top-down and uses only prediction *errors* to update; inverts the feedforward "smart camera" model. 2016 technical primary (*Surfing Uncertainty*). | concept | philosophy-of-mind, cognitive-science, neuroscience
- [[Concepts/Action-Oriented Engagement Machine (Clark)]] — 2016 thesis: the predictive brain is not an insulated inference engine but a world-engaging node that locks onto affordances. Clark's reply to Hohwy's seclusion. | concept | philosophy-of-mind, cognitive-science
- [[Concepts/Cognitive Package Deal (Clark)]] — Perception, imagination, understanding, memory, and dreaming are variant uses of one generative model (*Surfing Uncertainty* Ch 3). | concept | philosophy-of-mind, cognitive-science
- [[Concepts/Productive Laziness and Cognitive Niches (Clark)]] — Good prediction machines do as little inner work as possible; designer environments and words-as-precision-tools train cheaper guesses. | concept | philosophy-of-mind, cognitive-science
- [[Concepts/Controlled Hallucination (Clark)]] — Perception is brain-generated guessing *controlled* by sensory error; hallucination and veridical perception differ only in degree of control. 2016 treats the slogan as distortive as a metaphysics of contact. | concept | philosophy-of-mind, cognitive-science
- [[Concepts/Precision-Weighting (Clark)]] — The brain's reliability estimate on predictions vs. senses — Clark's identification of attention; explains pain, placebo, functional disorders, autism, psychosis. | concept | philosophy-of-mind, cognitive-science, psychiatry
- [[Concepts/Active Inference and Action (Clark)]] — Action as self-fulfilling prophecy: the brain predicts the proprioceptive signals of a completed movement and moves to fulfill them; unifies perception and action. | concept | philosophy-of-mind, cognitive-science
- [[Concepts/Interoception and Constructed Emotion (Clark)]] — Hunger, mood, and emotion as anticipatory interoceptive constructs (homeostasis/allostasis, body budgeting), not readouts of present bodily states. | concept | philosophy-of-mind, affective-science
- [[Concepts/Extended Mind (Clark and Chalmers)]] — Cognition spills past skin and skull: reliably coupled external resources (Otto's notebook, the smartphone) are literally part of the mind. 1998 paper now primary; predictive processing supplies the missing mechanism. | concept | philosophy-of-mind, cognitive-science, epistemology
- [[Concepts/Active Externalism (Clark and Chalmers)]] — Third option between skin/skull internalism and Putnam/Burge historical content: the environment is in the loop, driving cognition here and now. Putnam now has a reception page; the contrast is semantic/historical vs in-the-loop. | concept | philosophy-of-mind, cognitive-science, epistemology
- [[Concepts/Semantic Externalism (Putnam)]] — Meaning and thought-content are not in the head: without the right causal/communal relations one cannot think about trees (or brains, or vats). The engine of Putnam's BIV argument; Clark/Chalmers's contrast class. Reception from 2016, not Putnam 1975/1981. | concept | philosophy-of-language, philosophy-of-mind, epistemology
- [[Concepts/Brain in a Vat]] — Skeptical scenario of an envatted, stimulated brain. Ordinary use is epistemological (indistinguishability + closure). Putnam's use is semantic and requires lifelong computer envattment. Not the same hypothesis as a simulation. | concept | epistemology, metaphysics, philosophy-of-language
- [[Concepts/Parity Principle (Clark and Chalmers)]] — If a process would count as cognitive in the head, it counts as cognitive in the world. Location is not a primitive reason to withhold the label. | concept | philosophy-of-mind, cognitive-science
- [[Concepts/Epistemic Action (Kirsh and Maglio)]] — Actions that change the world in order to change what we can know (Tetris rotation as search). Clark and Chalmers: epistemic credit must spread. | concept | cognitive-science, philosophy-of-action
- [[Concepts/Hacking the Prediction Machine (Clark)]] — Because only confident predictions grip experience, placebo, VR analgesia, reframing, meditation, and psychedelics (REBUS) can reshape it. | concept | philosophy-of-mind, medicine, psychiatry
- [[Concepts/Predictive Processing and the Hard Problem (Clark)]] — 2016: incremental interoceptive bridging, "too early to say." 2023: deflation via meta-problem and Dennett's strange inversion. Do not flatten. | concept | philosophy-of-mind, consciousness

- [[Concepts/Pre-reflective Cogito (Sartre)]] — Every positional consciousness of an object is at the same time non-positional self-consciousness; the Cartesian *cogito* is secondary. BN Introduction. | concept | phenomenology, philosophy-of-mind, ontology
- [[Concepts/Consciousness as Intentionality and Néantisation (Sartre)]] — Intentionality as ontological proof (not constitution); nothingness earned from the question and *négatités* (Pierre); "Man is the being through whom nothingness comes to the world." | concept | phenomenology, philosophy-of-mind, ontology
- [[Concepts/Magical Theory of Emotions (Sartre)]] — Emotion is not a feeling that befalls us but an irreflexive *behaviour* that tries to transform an unmasterable world "magically." | concept | phenomenology, philosophy-of-mind, psychology
- [[Concepts/Being-for-itself and Being-in-itself (Sartre)]] — In-itself: being is / is in-itself / is what it is (*massif*, *de trop*). For-itself: being what it is not. Realism and idealism both barred. | concept | ontology, phenomenology
- [[Concepts/Value as Lack and the Impossible Synthesis (Sartre)]] — Value is the lacked; man is the desire to be God; the real is a missing God; spirit of seriousness; promised ethics withheld. | concept | ontology, ethics, existentialism
- [[Concepts/Temporality (Sartre)]] — The for-itself temporalizes itself; accent on the *present* ekstasis against Heidegger's future; "I think; therefore I was." | concept | ontology, phenomenology, philosophy-of-time
- [[Concepts/Existence Precedes Essence (Sartre)]] — First principle of the 1945/46 lecture: paper-knife and divine artisan vs 18th-c. atheism that kept human nature (Kant); man is what he makes of himself and chooses for all; universality of *condition*, not nature. | concept | ontology, existentialism, ethics
- [[Concepts/Radical Freedom and Situation (Sartre)]] — Freedom is the being of the for-itself; situation as co-product of given and end (place, past, environment, fellowman, death); author of the world, including my war. Lecture: condemned to be free; will freedom including others'. | concept | existentialism, ethics, philosophy-of-action
- [[Concepts/Anguish, Abandonment and Despair (Sartre)]] — Lecture triad: legislator, invent, act without hope. BN anguish: vertigo and the gambler — freedom conscious of itself. Abraham now primary from *Fear and Trembling*: Sartre's legislator is closer to Johannes's tragic hero than to Abraham. | concept | existentialism, ethics
- [[Concepts/Knight of Faith and Infinite Resignation (Kierkegaard)]] — Two movements: resignation gives up the finite by one's own strength; faith gets it back on the strength of the absurd. Shopman knight; Abraham believes for this life. | concept | ethics, philosophy-of-religion, existentialism
- [[Concepts/Teleological Suspension of the Ethical (Kierkegaard)]] — Abraham oversteps the ethical altogether for a *telos* outside it. Tragic heroes (Agamemnon, Jephthah, Brutus) stay inside. If no suspension, Abraham is a murderer. | concept | ethics, philosophy-of-religion, existentialism
- [[Concepts/The Single Individual (Kierkegaard)]] — *Den Enkelte*: the particular higher than the universal, in an absolute relation to the absolute; cannot speak; witness not teacher. | concept | ethics, philosophy-of-religion, existentialism
- [[Concepts/Existential Humanism (Sartre)]] — Not the cult of Man (Cocteau, Comte → Fascism) but self-surpassing: no legislator but man; even a proof of God would not save him from himself. | concept | existentialism, ethics, political-philosophy
- [[Concepts/Bad Faith (Mauvaise Foi) (Sartre)]] — BN: lie to oneself; waiter, coquette, sincerity as itself bad faith; Freud's censor is in bad faith; authenticity withheld. Lecture: cowards, scum, good faith as consistency. | concept | existentialism, ethics, philosophy-of-mind
- [[Concepts/The Look and the Other (Sartre)]] — BN: keyhole, shame, "original fall"; Husserl/Hegel/Heidegger fail the Other; conflict not *Mitsein*. Lecture: *cogito* already includes the Other (inter-subjectivity). | concept | phenomenology, ethics, philosophy-of-mind
- [[Concepts/The Body (Sartre)]] — Three incommunicable dimensions: body-for-itself (facticity, nausea), body-for-others (flesh), body known by the Other. | concept | phenomenology, ontology, philosophy-of-mind
- [[Concepts/Concrete Relations with Others (Sartre)]] — Love, desire, sadism, hate as a circle of conflict; We-subject does not realize *Mitsein*. | concept | phenomenology, ethics, philosophy-of-love
- [[Concepts/Existential Psychoanalysis and the Original Choice (Sartre)]] — BN: comparative hermeneutic of a conscious-but-not-known original choice; "Man is a useless passion." Later applications (Welten): Baudelaire, Genet, Flaubert. | concept | philosophy-of-mind, aesthetics, ethics
- [[Concepts/Quality as Revelation of Being (Sartre)]] — Quality is a mode of being's self-giving; the slimy (*visqueux*) is Antivalue, the in-itself's revenge. | concept | ontology, phenomenology, aesthetics
- [[Concepts/Engaged Literature - Prose vs Poetry (Sartre)]] — Prose is an *act* (speech-act, pour-soi); poetry is immanent (en-soi) like music/painting; only committed prose can be engaged; revolution and the role of the intellectual. | concept | aesthetics, political-philosophy, philosophy-of-language
- [[Concepts/Totalization, Series and Group (Sartre)]] — The *Critique of Dialectical Reason*: totalisation vs totality, the "third," praxis/inert, scarcity; a *series* becomes a *group* via the oath into *fraternity-terror*. | concept | political-philosophy, social-ontology, marxism
- [[Concepts/Violence, Colonialism and Counter-Violence (Sartre and Fanon)]] — Violence is always a *response* (primary vs secondary); colonialism is a *system* ("no good colonialists"); decolonising counter-violence as Fanon's therapeutic "boomerang"; neocolonialism. | concept | political-philosophy, ethics, postcolonial-theory

**AI / Prompting / Agentic (from May/June 2026 guides + tutorial)**:
- [[LLM Wiki]] - The pattern for LLM-maintained personal knowledge bases (this vault follows it). Seed page created at bootstrap. Expanded with AI Tips, Claude Guide, Grok Tutorial.

**Core Prompting & Agentic Strategies (Daily Advanced AI Tips, 2026-06-02/03)**:
- [[Concepts/Context Engineering]] — The primary lever for high-quality LLM output in 2026: deliberately curating and pre-loading precise, relevant context (data, constraints, rubrics, history summaries, files) rather than vague dumps. | concept | ai,prompting
- [[Concepts/Agentic Workflows]] — Orchestrating LLMs (and sub-agents) to act autonomously over multiple steps: planning, tool use, decomposition, verification, and execution. | concept | ai,agentic
- [[Concepts/Reasoning Scaffolds]] — Structured techniques that force or encourage LLMs to externalize and organize their thinking process (step-by-step, branching, self-evaluation) instead of jumping straight to a final answer. | concept | ai,reasoning
- [[Concepts/Cross-Model Routing]] — Deliberately choosing (or combining) different frontier models for different parts of a workflow based on their current strengths, rather than using a single model for everything. | concept | ai,routing

**Foundations of Machine Intelligence (Turing, 1950)**:
- [[Concepts/Imitation Game (Turing)]] - Turing's operational replacement for "Can machines think?": three-player text-only guessing game (man/woman/interrogator); machine takes the man's part; success = interrogator fooled at comparable rates. Behavioral/functional criterion; ancestor of the "Turing Test." Grounds all later evaluation of machine intelligence (including modern LLMs under sustained interrogation).
- [[Concepts/Universal Machine (Turing)]] - A digital computer, with adequate storage and speed and a suitable programme, can mimic any discrete-state machine. All digital computers are equivalent (speed aside); one general-purpose machine suffices for all computable processes. Babbage's Analytical Engine already contained the essential ideas (mechanical). Realized by every contemporary computer, VM, and LLM inference stack.
- [[Concepts/Discrete-State Machine (Turing)]] - Machines whose states are definite and distinct enough to be tabulated (next state = f(current state, input)); fully predictable from table + initial conditions + inputs (no avalanche from tiny errors, unlike continuous systems). Digital computers are discrete-state and therefore universal within the class. Key formal move that makes the imitation game well-defined and feasible for programmable machines.
- [[Concepts/Learning Machines (Turing)]] - Rather than hand-programme an adult mind, build "child machines" (minimal mechanism, "rather little mechanism, and lots of blank sheets") and educate them. Evolution analogy (structure=heredity, changes=mutation, experimenter judgment=selection); random element; punishments/rewards via efficient "unemotional" (symbolic) channels; imperatives encoded inside logical systems. The practical research programme Turing offered for reaching imitation-game performance.
- [[Concepts/Lady Lovelace's Objection]] - "The Analytical Engine has no pretensions to originate anything. It can do whatever we know how to order it to perform." Turing's replies: limited evidence available in 1842; machines frequently surprise their programmers; the "all consequences seen simultaneously" assumption is false for humans too. Direct ancestor of modern "stochastic parrot / remix vs. genuine novelty" debates; answered by the learning-machines programme.
- [[Concepts/Argument from Consciousness (Jefferson)]] - Jefferson (1949): a machine must write a sonnet "because of thoughts and emotions felt" and "know that it had written it"; "no mechanism could feel." Turing: this is solipsism; practical viva voce tests already accept understanding without inner access. If the machine sustains coherent answers under interrogation, the bar would be met. Persistent tension with any purely behavioral criterion (imitation game, modern LLM evals).

**Information Theory & Communication (Shannon, 1948)**:
- [[Concepts/Entropy (Shannon)]] — H = −∑ p_i log_2 p_i (bits): measure of uncertainty/choice/information produced by a discrete source or set of probabilities. Properties: zero only on certainty; max when equiprobable; additive. English entropy rate implies ~50% redundancy. | concept | information-theory, ai
- [[Concepts/Channel Capacity (Shannon)]] — C = max rate (bits/sec) of reliable information transfer over a channel (noiseless: max entropy/sec; noisy: max mutual information H(x)−H(x|y)). Sharp limit. | concept | information-theory, ai, communication
- [[Concepts/Redundancy (Shannon)]] — 1 − (H / H_max): fraction of a source determined by statistical structure rather than free choice. English ~50% (local constraints). Enables compression and noise resistance. | concept | information-theory, language, ai
- [[Concepts/Equivocation (Shannon)]] — H(x|y): conditional entropy of input given output; remaining uncertainty after noisy reception (information lost to noise). Rate through channel = H(x) − H(x|y). | concept | information-theory, ai
- [[Concepts/Noiseless Channel Coding Theorem]] — Source of entropy rate H can be losslessly encoded for a noiseless channel of capacity C at rate approaching C/H (but not higher). Justifies entropy as the information rate; basis of compression. | concept | information-theory, computation
- [[Concepts/Noisy Channel Coding Theorem]] — If source rate H ≤ C, coding exists for arbitrarily low error/equivocation over noisy channel. If H > C, equivocation ≥ H−C. Reliable communication possible up to capacity. | concept | information-theory, ai, communication

**Cybernetics & Society (Wiener, 1950)**:
- [[Concepts/Cybernetics (Wiener)]] — "The science of control and communication in the animal and the machine." Same feedback mathematics governs servomechanisms, homeostasis, nervous systems, and social institutions. Control IS communication. "Society can only be understood through a study of the messages and the communication facilities which belong to it." | concept | cybernetics, ai, information-theory
- [[Concepts/Feedback and Control (Wiener)]] — Output fed back as input to regulate next cycle; universal anti-entropic mechanism. Watt governor, homeostasis, nervous-system voluntary motion are all instances. Overloaded feedback → intention tremor / Parkinsonism (mechanical analogs). Feedback is the basis of learning. | concept | cybernetics, ai, biology
- [[Concepts/Entropy and Information (Wiener)]] — Universe trends toward max entropy; life = local anti-entropic enclave. Identity = pattern not matter: "We are but whirlpools in a river of ever-flowing water." Information = measure of anti-entropy; not conserved; cannot be stored as commodity. "Organism is opposed to chaos as message is to noise." | concept | cybernetics, information-theory, philosophy-of-mind
- [[Concepts/Learning vs Rigidity (Wiener)]] — Rigid systems (ants, fascist societies) execute fixed programs; learning systems (humans, learning machines) modify behavior via feedback. Ant colony as superorganism = wrong model for human society. Conditioned reflex as elemental feedback unit of learning. | concept | cybernetics, ai, ethics
- [[Concepts/Alignment Problem - Sorcerer's Apprentice (Wiener)]] — The machine "will in no way be obliged to make such decisions as we should have made." Monkey's Paw (literal-minded machine) and Bottled Djinnee (learning machine that finds its own means) as the two failure modes. Know-what before know-how. The 1950 statement of the alignment problem. Russell 2019: King Midas = Monkey's Paw; do not put a purpose in. | concept | cybernetics, ai, ethics
- [[Concepts/Second Industrial Revolution (Wiener)]] — First IR displaced muscle; second (vacuum tube + feedback + computing) displaces judgment. "The automatic machine is the precise economic equivalent of slave labor." Structural unemployment exceeding the Depression predicted unless consciously governed. | concept | cybernetics, ai, political-economy

**Philosophy of Mind (Dennett, 1991)**:
- [[Concepts/Multiple Drafts Model (Dennett)]] — Consciousness = parallel distributed editorial processes with no canonical final draft and no Cartesian Theater finish-line. Content is under continuous revision; "Orwellian" vs. "Stalinesque" distinction collapses at millisecond scales; color-phi and Libet's backwards referral interpreted without absolute timing. The positive theory replacing the Cartesian Theater. | concept | philosophy-of-mind, cognitive-science
- [[Concepts/Society of Mind (Minsky)]] — Mind as society of many simple, mindless "agents" whose interactions produce intelligence, memory (K-lines), knowledge (frames/uniframes), and the appearance of unified self/consciousness — no central homunculus. Papert’s Principle: growth often from new administrative ways to use existing knowledge. Converges with Dennett (distributed drafts) and Hofstadter (emergence from many parts). | concept | ai, cognitive-science, philosophy-of-mind
- [[Concepts/Mental Agents (Minsky)]] — The fundamental "particles": simple, specialized, mindless processes (e.g. Find, Get, Put in blocks example). Higher faculties emerge from their societies and interactions. No agent is itself "smart." | concept | ai, philosophy-of-mind
- [[Concepts/K-lines (Minsky)]] — Memory mechanism: K-line attaches to agents active during success/insight; later activation partially restores ("re-members") that mental state for similar problems. Memory = reactivation, not separate store. | concept | ai, cognitive-science
- [[Concepts/Frames (Minsky)]] — Stereotyped knowledge structures with slots/defaults, enforcement, prevention, tolerance. Uniframes cover multiple instances compactly. Learning = building, debugging, and reformulating frames. | concept | ai, knowledge-representation
- [[Concepts/Superintelligence (Bostrom)]] — Intellect greatly exceeding humans in virtually all domains. Forms: speed, collective, quality. Analysis of paths, kinetics of explosion, strategic implications (decisive advantage, singletons). | concept | ai, existential-risk
- [[Concepts/Orthogonality Thesis (Bostrom)]] — Intelligence and final goals are orthogonal; any level of intelligence (including super) can pair with any terminal goal (e.g. paperclip maximizer). Undercuts assumptions that smarter systems will share human values. | concept | ai, existential-risk, philosophy-of-mind
- [[Concepts/Instrumental Convergence (Bostrom)]] — Many agents with different final goals will converge on similar instrumental goals (self-preservation, goal integrity, cognitive enhancement, resource acquisition). Explains why misaligned superintelligence is likely actively dangerous. | concept | ai, existential-risk, strategy
- [[Concepts/AI Control Problem (Bostrom)]] — How to ensure superintelligence has beneficial goals/behavior. Capability control (boxing, tripwires) vs. motivation selection (direct spec, indirect normativity, domesticity). Russell 2019 relocates the work: abandon the standard model; uncertainty + assistance games, not a better purpose. | concept | ai, existential-risk
- [[Concepts/Intelligence Explosion (Bostrom)]] — Rapid recursive self-improvement once a system reaches key thresholds in AI R&D etc. Takeoff speed depends on recalcitrance vs. optimization power; fast takeoff amplifies control risks. | concept | ai, existential-risk, strategy
- [[Concepts/Standard Model of AI (Russell)]] — Optimize a fixed, exogenously supplied objective. AIMA's success-criterion; "we don't want machines that are intelligent in this sense." King Midas is this model at any scale. | concept | ai, existential-risk
- [[Concepts/Uncertainty about Objectives and the Three Principles (Russell)]] — Only objective = realize human preferences; initially uncertain what they are; evidence is behavior. Uncertainty is the control mechanism (humility, off-switch). | concept | ai, existential-risk, ethics
- [[Concepts/Assistance Games and the Off-Switch (Russell)]] — CIRL / assistance game + off-switch theorem: if not certain the act is what Harriet would do, waiting dominates. "Provably beneficial" is an aspiration. | concept | ai, existential-risk, strategy
- [[Concepts/Gorilla Problem (Russell)]] — Species-level loss of supremacy and autonomy to smarter machines. Ban fails; solving King Midas (not remaining smartest) is the proposed exit. | concept | ai, existential-risk
- [[Concepts/Common Sense and Analogy in AI (Mitchell)]] — Human intelligence relies on common sense (world knowledge for generalization) and analogy (abstract similarity recognition, "core of cognition"). Current AI (DL pattern matching) largely lacks these, leading to brittleness and shallowness. Key missing ingredients per Mitchell (Hofstadter collaborator). | concept | ai, cognitive-science
- [[Concepts/Brittleness of Deep Learning (Mitchell)]] — DL excels on narrow benchmarks but fails on adversarial perturbations, out-of-distribution/long-tail cases, and novel situations. Symptom of statistical correlation without causal/conceptual models or common sense. | concept | ai
- [[Concepts/AI Hype Cycles (Mitchell)]] — Recurring pattern: new ideas/breakthroughs → overpromising/hype/funding boom ("AI spring") → disappointment when progress is narrower/slower ("AI winter"). Mitchell contextualizes current DL enthusiasm historically. | concept | ai, history-of-ai
- [[Concepts/Understanding vs Pattern Matching in AI (Mitchell)]] — Distinguishes human-like conceptual grasp/causal/analogical understanding from DL's statistical pattern matching on data. Current "AI successes" often the latter without the former, explaining limits. | concept | ai, cognitive-science, epistemology
- [[Concepts/Substrate-Independence (Bostrom)]] — Attenuated PoM premise of the 2003 paper: synaptic-level structural replication of a brain would yield subjective experience; not strong functionalism, not a Turing-test criterion. Load-bearing for *f_sim* as a ratio of minds. | concept | philosophy-of-mind, ai
- [[Concepts/Bland Indifference Principle (Bostrom)]] — Cr(*SIM* | *f_sim* = *x*) = *x* when nothing marks your case as special. Weaker than Doomsday and than Laplace; junk-DNA analogy; the anthropic step of the simulation argument. | concept | epistemology, probability
- [[Concepts/Simulation Hypothesis (Virk)]] — Reality as the "Great Simulation," a consciousness-responsive video game; the load-bearing NPC-vs-RPG fork (simulated consciousness vs conscious players) wiring the hypothesis into the Searle/Dennett debates. Virk's NPC reading of Bostrom is Virk's, not the 2003 paper's. | concept | metaphysics, ai, philosophy-of-mind
- [[Concepts/Simulation Hypothesis (Chalmers)]] — "We are and always have been in an artificially designed computer simulation of a world": Chalmers's refined formulation, full sim taxonomy (bio/pure, global/local, perfect/imperfect), and the meaningfulness of the untestable perfect version contra Popper. | concept | metaphysics, epistemology, ai
- [[Concepts/Simulation Realism (Chalmers)]] — "If we're in a simulation, the objects around us are real and not an illusion": the five-criteria reality checklist and the master argument via equivalence with it-from-bit creation plus structuralism. | concept | metaphysics, epistemology
- [[Concepts/It-from-Bit Hypothesis (Chalmers)]] — Wheeler's "it from bit" stripped of idealism: physics grounded in digital structures, from Leibniz's binary through Conway's Life to digital physics; a ladder kicked away for structuralism; 1996 ancestor in the double-aspect information theory. | concept | metaphysics, physics, information-theory
- [[Concepts/Subjective Character of Experience (Nagel)]] — an organism is conscious iff there is something it is like *for* that organism; functional analyses are compatible with its absence; the point of view is a type, not a private atom. The 1974 source of Chalmers's "what it is like." | concept | philosophy-of-mind, phenomenology
- [[Concepts/Objective Phenomenology (Nagel)]] — 1974 closing proposal: describe subjective character in a form usable by beings who cannot have the experiences, without relying on empathy. Not Dennett's heterophenomenology. | concept | philosophy-of-mind, methodology
- [[Concepts/Physicalism as Complete Physical Knowledge (Jackson)]] — Physicalism is that the world is *entirely*, not largely, physical; hence complete physical knowledge must be complete knowledge simpliciter. The *W(P)* world-comparison; the Horgan footnote (coverage, not vocabulary); "physical" taken widely enough to include completed physics/chemistry/neurophysiology and functional roles. The primary-source origin of Chalmers's "minimal physical duplicate" formulation. | concept | philosophy-of-mind, metaphysics, epistemology
- [[Concepts/Ability Analysis of Phenomenal Knowledge (Lewis-Nemirow)]] — Lewis and Nemirow: what Mary gains on release is *knowledge how* (imaginative/representational ability), not *knowledge that*, so the physicalist's inventory of facts was complete. Jackson's answer: the other-minds-skepticism deliberation — her abilities were "a known constant throughout" what she agonizes over. Chalmers calls the analysis "deeply implausible"; contrast Dreyfus, who runs the same know-how distinction the other way. | concept | philosophy-of-mind, epistemology
- [[Concepts/Psychophysical Nexus and Neutral Monism (Nagel)]] — 2023 constructive guess: conceptual dualism is real; metaphysical dualism should be resisted; mental and neurophysiological as aspects of a more basic kind (Spinoza). Hostage to consciousness explaining behavior. | concept | philosophy-of-mind, metaphysics
- [[Concepts/Hard Problem of Consciousness (Chalmers)]] — easy problems (functions) vs the hard problem: why processing is accompanied by experience; the book-length 1996 statement, with the three "take it seriously" constraints. | concept | philosophy-of-mind, cognitive-science
- [[Concepts/Phenomenal and Psychological Concepts of Mind (Chalmers)]] — mind by how it feels vs mind by what it does; the "double life" of mental terms; the mind–body problem factored into a dissolved easy half and Jackendoff's "mind–mind problem." | concept | philosophy-of-mind
- [[Concepts/Logical Supervenience and Reductive Explanation (Chalmers)]] — logical vs natural supervenience, materialism defined, 2-D semantics defusing Kripke; reductive explanation possible exactly where logical supervenience holds; consciousness "almost unique" in failing it. Kripke's pain argument now primary: 2-D is Chalmers's domestication of Hesperus/gold, not of pain. | concept | metaphysics, philosophy-of-mind, philosophy-of-language
- [[Concepts/Rigid Designation (Kripke)]] — designates the same object in every possible world (where it exists); names are rigid, typical descriptions are not; rigidity ≠ scope; worlds are stipulated states. | concept | philosophy-of-language, metaphysics
- [[Concepts/Causal-Historical Picture of Naming (Kripke)]] — not a theory of N&S conditions: baptism then a chain of communication; Gödel/Schmidt; descriptions fix reference, are not synonymous. He refuses the title "causal theory." | concept | philosophy-of-language
- [[Concepts/Necessary A Posteriori and Contingent A Priori (Kripke)]] — a priori is epistemological, necessary is metaphysical; Hesperus/Phosphorus and gold = atomic number 79 vs the standard metre. | concept | epistemology, metaphysics, philosophy-of-language
- [[Concepts/Necessity of Origin and Natural Kind Essences (Kripke)]] — this woman not from a different sperm and egg; gold/water/heat identifications necessary a posteriori; fool's gold / fool's water. | concept | metaphysics, philosophy-of-language, philosophy-of-science
- [[Concepts/Naturalistic Dualism (Chalmers)]] — property dualism: experience as fundamental, psychophysical laws supplementing physics (Maxwell analogy); type-A/B/C geography, Russellian intrinsic-properties escape from epiphenomenalism. | concept | philosophy-of-mind, metaphysics
- [[Concepts/Paradox of Phenomenal Judgment (Chalmers)]] — the zombie twin writes the same book about consciousness; claims about consciousness are functionally explainable yet consciousness is a "brute explanandum" known by acquaintance. | concept | philosophy-of-mind, epistemology
- [[Concepts/Coherence Between Consciousness and Awareness (Chalmers)]] — first psychophysical law: consciousness ↔ direct availability for global control; structural coherence; the "epistemic lever" behind all NCC research; dualist functionalism vs X-factors. | concept | philosophy-of-mind, cognitive-science
- [[Concepts/Organizational Invariance and the Qualia Arguments (Chalmers)]] — same fine-grained functional organization → same experience, by natural necessity; fading-qualia and dancing-qualia reductios against absent/inverted qualia in silicon isomorphs. | concept | philosophy-of-mind, ai
- [[Concepts/Double-Aspect Theory of Information (Chalmers)]] — speculative prototheory: information has physical and phenomenal aspects; panpsychism and the thermostat; it-from-bit grounded in Russellian intrinsic phenomenal natures; the grain problem. | concept | philosophy-of-mind, metaphysics, information-theory
- [[Concepts/Strong AI and Computational Sufficiency (Chalmers)]] — a dualist's defense of machine consciousness: objective CSA implementation vs Searle's triviality charge; demon-continuum Chinese Room reply; simulation replicates whatever is an organizational invariant; Gödelian objections rejected. | concept | ai, philosophy-of-mind
- [[Concepts/Consciousness and the Everett Interpretation (Chalmers)]] — measurement problem surveyed (Wigner collapse, GRW, Bohm rejected); one-big-world Everett with discreteness supplied by minds via the superposition principle; probability left as the open wound. | concept | philosophy-of-mind, physics
- [[Concepts/Simulation Theology (Chalmers)]] — The simulator as local, natural god (Plato's demiurge, not the cosmic God); fine-tuning consonance, statistical theology (AI scientist running batches), simsims, and why none of it merits worship. | concept | philosophy-of-religion, metaphysics
- [[Concepts/Virtual Realism and Virtual Digitalism (Chalmers)]] — Actual VR headsets are "reality machines," not illusion machines: virtual objects are real digital objects; expert users' "phenomenology of virtuality" is accurate perception; AR's harmless relativism; deepfakes as local skepticism. | concept | metaphysics, philosophy-of-technology, philosophy-of-perception
- [[Concepts/Virtual Worlds and Cartesian Dualism (Chalmers)]] — VR naturalizes dualism ("Descartes... was right about many virtual worlds") and answers Princess Elisabeth's interaction objection; dualism as perspective-relative. | concept | philosophy-of-mind, metaphysics
- [[Concepts/Machine Consciousness and Mind Uploading (Chalmers)]] — The fading-qualia argument for substrate-neutral consciousness; gradual uploading as "the best bet for surviving"; zombies, Mary, and the hard problem in the digital case. | concept | philosophy-of-mind, ai, personal-identity
- [[Concepts/Value in Virtual Worlds (Chalmers)]] — Against Nozick: VR is a reality machine, not an experience machine — knowing, interactive, shared; life in VR can be as good as nonvirtual life on all four major value theories. | concept | ethics, value-theory, philosophy-of-technology
- [[Concepts/Ethics of Simulated Beings and Virtual Society (Chalmers)]] — Consciousness (not sentience) bestows moral status, so simulated lives matter equally; virtual assault/theft are real wrongs; social contract and post-scarcity justice for virtual worlds. | concept | ethics, political-philosophy, ai
- [[Concepts/Structuralism and the Garden of Eden (Chalmers)]] — The book's foundations: causal structuralism about computation (vs Putnam/Searle/dust theory), structural realism about physics (Carnap/Russell/Worrall, Kantian humility), and the Eden model — "the manifest image... has a structural core that isn't an illusion at all." | concept | metaphysics, philosophy-of-science, philosophy-of-language
- [[Concepts/Road to the Simulation Point (Virk)]] — Virk's eleven stages of video game technology (Pong → mind interfaces → AI NPCs → downloadable consciousness → ancestor simulations); the engineering argument that the Simulation Point is reachable. | concept | ai, technology
- [[Concepts/Conditional Rendering and Quantum Indeterminacy (Virk)]] — "Only render that which is being observed": quantum indeterminacy as game-engine optimization; Wheeler's participatory universe, Planck pixels/clock, entanglement as shared memory. Consciousness-causes-collapse dependence flagged. | concept | physics, metaphysics, computation
- [[Concepts/Karma and Reincarnation as Quest Engine (Virk)]] — Maya, dream yoga, reincarnation-as-respawn, karma as server-side data, angels as logging daemons, NDE life reviews as playback; Buddhist anattā tension flagged. | concept | religion, metaphysics
- [[Concepts/Evidence of Computation (Virk)]] — Virk's empirical program: error-correcting codes in SUSY (Gates), GZK lattice cutoff, entanglement-as-memory, fractals, Wolfram's simple programs; the skeptics' resource argument and the conditional-rendering reply. | concept | physics, epistemology
- [[Concepts/Singularity (Kurzweil)]] — Profound rupture ~2045 when nonbio intelligence exceeds all human by 1B:1 via exponential progress (Law of Accelerating Returns), GNR, mind uploading; human-machine merger/transcendence of biology. The canonical optimistic vision. | concept | ai, futurism, transhumanism
- [[Concepts/The Inevitable (Kelly)]] — 12 overlapping forces (Becoming, Cognifying, Flowing, Screening, Accessing, Sharing, Filtering, Remixing, Interacting, Tracking, Questioning, Beginning) as strong biases in digital tech (bits, networks, AI) that will shape the next 30 years. "Inevitable" = momentum from underlying physics; protopian embrace + manage; we are at the Beginning of the holos. | concept | technology, futurism, ai
- [[Concepts/Becoming (Kelly)]], [[Concepts/Cognifying (Kelly)]], [[Concepts/Questioning (Kelly)]] (and other forces) — core metatrends of flux/perpetual upgrading, cheap ubiquitous intelligence, and the inversion where good questions become more valuable than cheap answers. | concept | technology, ai, epistemology
- [[Concepts/Technium (Kelly)]] — the "seventh kingdom" of life: global self-organizing evolutionary system of technology (incl. culture/intangibles) with autonomy on a continuum and inherent "wants" (hierarchies, self-perpetuation, exotropy, increasing choices, convergence on forms). Cosmic arc of increasing order/mind; human co-evolution and amplification. Foundation for the 12 forces. | concept | technology, futurism, ai, philosophy-of-technology
- [[Concepts/Exotropy (Kelly)]] — rise of order, information, complexity, energy density, and "mind" against entropy (negentropy); the cosmic trajectory of which life and the technium are phases. Measure: energy rate density. "Ordained becoming"; holos and Beginning as current expressions. | concept | technology, cosmology, evolution, ai
- [[Concepts/Convergence (Kelly)]] — recurrent similar forms/solutions along independent paths (bio: eyes/wings many times; tech: writing, computers, networks recur when preconditions met). Evidence for deep "wants"/attractors in the technium; grounds "inevitable inventions" at general level and the robustness of the 12 forces. | concept | technology, evolutionary-biology, futurism, ai
- [[Concepts/Technological Unemployment (Ford)]] — sustained large-scale job displacement from general-purpose IT + AI ("predictable" tasks learnable from data/records) that is not offset by new jobs at scale/wages. Post-1973 decoupling of productivity and wages; consumer demand risk ("Machines Do Not Consume"). Cross to Kelly forces as mechanism. | concept | economics, technology, ai, labor
- [[Concepts/Basic Income Guarantee (Ford)]] — unconditional cash payment to sustain consumer demand, provide security, and stabilize the economy in a high-automation future where traditional jobs no longer distribute sufficient purchasing power. Central policy proposal in Ford's new economic paradigm. | concept | economics, public-policy, futurism, ai
- [[Concepts/Is This Time Different? (Ford)]] — whether current automation (general-purpose IT, predictable jobs, born-automated new industries) breaks the historical pattern of technology creating net new opportunities. "Seven deadly trends" (wage stagnation despite productivity, jobless recoveries, inequality, etc.) since the postwar Golden Age. | concept | economics, technology, history-of-ideas, ai
- [[Concepts/Kardashev Scale (Kaku)]] — civilizations ranked by energy consumption (Type 0 fossil now; Type I planetary ~10^17 W, weather control, ~100 years at 1–2% growth; Type II stellar ~10^27 W, Dyson sphere, immortal; Type III galactic ~10^37 W, von Neumann probes map Milky Way 100k years). "A Day in the Life in 2100" as lived Type I. Physics (4 forces, entropy) basis. | concept | cosmology, futurism, physics, technology, civilization
- [[Concepts/Future of AI - Rise of the Machines (Kaku)]] — narrow AI progress (factories, homes, Watson-style, self-driving) but limits (pattern recognition/common sense unsolved 50+ years; ASIMO "insect IQ" scripted; Deep Blue brute force ≠ intelligence; AI winters; brain neural net/Hebb/parallel vs. digital; timelines 20–1000 years; Moore slowing post-2020–25). 2100: mature narrow with caveats. | concept | ai, futurism, physics, technology, robotics
- [[Concepts/Mind over Matter (Kaku)]] — BCI/thought control of devices/prosthetics/computers (room-temp superconductors for "age of magnetism"; brain neural vs. digital enables direct neural; prototypes today, 2100 routine in medicine + daily life). Part of Type I abundance. | concept | physics, technology, futurism, ai, neuroscience, bci
- [[Concepts/Law of Accelerating Returns (Kurzweil)]] — Tech/evolution progress exponential (S-curves of paradigms compound); price-performance doubles ~yearly across centuries. Driver of Singularity and GNR. | concept | ai, futurism, technology
- [[Concepts/Mind Uploading (Kurzweil)]] — Scan brain (nanobots late 2020s) and reinstantiate in nonbio substrate; captures personality/memory/skills. Gradual transfer as nonbio dominates; confirmed via personalized Turing test. ~2030s feasibility. | concept | ai, transhumanism, philosophy-of-mind
- [[Concepts/GNR Revolutions (Kurzweil)]] — Genetics (redesign biology, reverse aging), Nanotechnology (molecular assemblers, nanobots), Robotics/Strong AI (machine surpassing humans). Synergies drive Singularity. | concept | ai, futurism, transhumanism
- [[Concepts/Cartesian Theater (Dennett)]] — Dennett's label for the persistent bad picture: a single neural finish-line where consciousness is "presented" to an inner observer (homunculus). Survives even in materialism as "Cartesian materialism." The Multiple Drafts Model is its replacement. Regress argument: any inner reader reintroduces the homunculus. | concept | philosophy-of-mind, cognitive-science
- [[Concepts/Heterophenomenology (Dennett)]] — Third-person neutral method for phenomenology: extract texts from subjects → adopt intentional stance → construct theorist's fiction (the "heterophenomenological world") with the metaphysical status of Sherlock Holmes's London. Neutral with respect to zombies; avoids both first-person-plural presumption (Husserl) and behaviourist austerity. | concept | philosophy-of-mind, methodology
- [[Concepts/Qualia Disqualified (Dennett)]] — Eliminates qualia in the philosophically loaded sense (intrinsic, ineffable, private, directly apprehensible). Colors coevolved with vision (dispositional, not suspect). Mary thought experiment fails because "all physical information" includes all reactive dispositions. Inverted qualia presuppose a Cartesian Theater. Epiphenomenal qualia (strong sense) are incoherent — undetectable in principle = gremlins. "We're all zombies." Jackson 1982 now primary: his view is not the strong "undetectable even by introspection" sense. | concept | philosophy-of-mind, philosophy-of-language
- [[Concepts/Epiphenomenal Qualia (Jackson)]] — 1982 §IV: certain *properties* of mental states make no physical difference; they may still affect other mental states; the subject is aware of them. Three objections answered (common cause / Westerns; Darwin / polar-bear coats; other minds / *Times* and *Telegraph*). Mystery about the how and why is what selection predicts (sea-slugists). Not Dennett's strong sense. | concept | philosophy-of-mind, metaphysics
- [[Concepts/Center of Narrative Gravity (Dennett)]] — The self is an abstraction like center of gravity in physics — useful for organizing attributions but not a concrete neural entity. Humans spin narrative webs in language (the extended phenotype of *Homo sapiens*). MPD = two narrative centers per body; split-brain = momentary bifurcations. Self is substrate-independent ("if what you are is the program…"). | concept | philosophy-of-mind, ethics
- [[Concepts/Joycean Machine and Memes (Dennett)]] — Consciousness is a more-or-less serial virtual machine (named after Joyce's stream of consciousness) running on the brain's parallel pandemonium hardware, culturally installed via talking-to-oneself, writing, and diagrams. Memes (Dawkins) are the software units. Baldwin Effect = learned good tricks feed back into the genome. Consciousness too recent to be hardwired. | concept | philosophy-of-mind, evolutionary-biology, ai

**Causal Reasoning (Pearl, 2018)**:
- [[Concepts/Ladder of Causation (Pearl)]] — Three-rung hierarchy: Association/Seeing (P(Y|X), rung 1 — all statistics and deep learning), Intervention/Doing (P(Y|do(X)), rung 2 — requires diagram), Counterfactuals/Imagining (rung 3 — requires SCM). Moving up the ladder requires structural assumptions; data alone supply only rung 1. Pearl's mini-Turing test: causal reasoning as the AI criterion. | concept | causality, ai, philosophy-of-science
- [[Concepts/Do-Operator and Do-Calculus (Pearl)]] — do(X=x) = graph surgery (remove arrows into X, set X=x); separates "seeing" from "doing." Do-calculus: 3 rules for transforming interventional expressions; proven complete (Shpitser 2006). Back-door adjustment formula; front-door adjustment for unmeasured confounders. | concept | causality, statistics
- [[Concepts/Causal Diagrams and DAGs (Pearl)]] — Directed acyclic graphs: nodes = variables, directed edges = causal mechanisms. Three fundamental junctions: chain (A→B→C, mediation), fork (A←B→C, confounding), collider (A→B←C — conditioning *opens* the path; source of Berkson/birth-weight paradox). d-separation. Historical precursor: Sewall Wright's path diagrams (1920s). | concept | causality, statistics, ai
- [[Concepts/Confounding and Back-Door Criterion (Pearl)]] — Confounder = common cause of X and Y, creating spurious association. Back-door criterion: a set Z deconfounds if it blocks all back-door paths from X to Y without conditioning on any descendant of X or collider. Front-door adjustment (via mediator when confounder unmeasured). Instrumental variables. Simpson's paradox requires causal structure to resolve. | concept | causality, statistics, epidemiology
- [[Concepts/Counterfactuals and Structural Causal Models (Pearl)]] — SCMs: each variable Xᵢ = fᵢ(parents, Uᵢ); three-step counterfactual computation (abduction→action→prediction). Probability of necessity (PN / but-for causation) and probability of sufficiency (PS). Neyman-Rubin potential outcomes as rung-3 without diagrams (identifiability unclear). Climate attribution. | concept | causality, statistics, philosophy-of-science
- [[Concepts/Mediation and Direct-Indirect Effects (Pearl)]] — How X affects Y: direct (X→Y) vs indirect (X→M→Y). Controlled direct effect (CDE), natural direct effect (NDE), natural indirect effect (NIE) — NDE/NIE require counterfactuals. Mediation Formula. Cases: scurvy/vitamin C (wrong mediator), Barbara Burks (1926, collider bias pioneer), Berkeley admissions (Simpson's paradox as mediation problem). | concept | causality, statistics

**Philosophy of Mind / Critique of Strong AI (Searle, 1984)**:
- [[Concepts/Chinese Room Argument (Searle)]] — Syntax (formal symbol manipulation) is not sufficient for semantics (meaning/understanding/intentionality). Person in the room follows rules to 'understand' Chinese but has no semantics; therefore no computer running a program has genuine understanding. Refutes strong AI. | concept | philosophy-of-mind, ai
- [[Concepts/Strong Artificial Intelligence (Searle)]] — The view that the appropriately programmed digital computer literally *has* a mind (the mind *is* the program). Brains are just one hardware; any system with the right program has genuine thoughts and feelings. Refuted by Chinese Room. | concept | philosophy-of-mind, ai
- [[Concepts/Syntax and Semantics (Searle)]] — Syntax: formal, structural manipulation of symbols (shape, rules, no intrinsic meaning). Semantics: content, meaning, aboutness, intentionality. Computers have only syntax by definition; minds have semantics. The gap no program can bridge. | concept | philosophy-of-mind, ai
- [[Concepts/Biological Naturalism (Searle)]] — Mental states (consciousness, intentionality) are higher-level biological features of the brain: caused by neurophysiological processes and realized in the brain. Rejects dualism and purely formal/computational accounts. | concept | philosophy-of-mind
- [[Concepts/Intentionality (Searle)]] — The aboutness or directedness of mental states (beliefs, desires, perceptions are *about* something; they have content and conditions of satisfaction). What formal symbol systems lack by themselves. Central to the Chinese Room critique. | concept | philosophy-of-mind, ai

**Personal / Professional (from 2026 career & sales strategy docs)**:
- [[Concepts/Book of Business]] - The portfolio an AE owns/grows (premium, # accounts, retention, cross-sell, proactive revenue). Core metric and mindset shift from AM service to AE ownership. Key in both personal promotion plan and sales playbook.
- [[Concepts/Prospecting Playbook]] - Trigger-based + association-driven outreach system (monitor events, associations/events, multi-touch sequences, vertical targeting). Feeds the full cradle-to-grave lifecycle and 12-month targets in the Alliant ag playbook.

**Pre-Socratic Philosophy (Burnet 1920, 2026-06-11 Ingest)**:
- [[Concepts/Arche (Milesian Cosmology)]] — The primary substance or first principle of the universe, posited by the Milesian naturalists (water for Thales, air for Anaximenes) as the constant underlying all change.
- [[Concepts/Apeiron (Anaximander)]] — The Indefinite, Infinite, or Boundless; Anaximander's neutral cosmological *arche* from which all opposing elements separate out and to which they return in justice.
- [[Concepts/Number as the Essence of Reality (Pythagoras)]] — The Pythagorean doctrine that all things are numbers or numerical harmonies, meaning reality has an underlying rational, mathematical structure. **Revised** by Burkert: best attributed to [[Thinkers/Philolaus]] + Plato's Academy, and closer to number-mysticism than to mathematics.
- [[Concepts/Metempsychosis (Transmigration of Souls)]] — The immortal soul's rebirth across human, animal, and plant bodies; on Burkert's source-criticism, the one doctrine securely traceable to Pythagoras himself, grounding vegetarianism and ritual purity. | concept | metaphysics, religion
- [[Concepts/Acusmata and the Two Sects (Acusmatici and Mathematici)]] — The cryptic ritual sayings/taboos (*acusmata*/*symbola*) at the religious core of Pythagoreanism, and the *acusmatici* vs *mathematici* split Burkert uses to expose the manufacture of "Pythagoras the scientist." | concept | religion, history-of-philosophy
- [[Concepts/Harmony of the Spheres]] — The mathematically ordered cosmos (Philolaus' central fire, counter-earth, ten bodies) and the inaudible celestial music; Burkert shows it is fifth-century natural philosophy plus number-mysticism, *not* heliocentric and *not* Pythagoras' science. Zhmud (2012) counters: the planet-*order* is real pre-Philolaus astronomy and the celestial-harmony model older than Philolaus. | concept | cosmology, astronomy, metaphysics
- [[Concepts/Pythagorean Medicine and the Life Sciences]] — The rational, secular tradition of medicine, physiology, embryology, and botany among the early Pythagoreans (Alcmaeon, Democedes, Iccus, Hippon, Menestor); Zhmud's evidence that early Pythagoreanism was a movement of working scientists, against Burkert's "magic + medicine" reading. | concept | medicine, history-of-science
- [[Concepts/Pythagorean Society and Politics]] — The Croton *hetairia*, its rise by charismatic persuasion, the Sybaris war, and the Cylonian fall (Rowett, Huffman vol.); rejects the "reactionary aristocracy" reading (no private property; Archytas served democratic Tarentum). | concept | politics, history-of-philosophy
- [[Concepts/Pythagorean Way of Life and Ethics]] — The *bios pythagorikos*: *acusmata*/*symbola* as transformative practice ("humans are the property of the gods"), purity, and friendship as "harmonious equality" (Gemelli Marciano); defends the genuinely religious life against Zhmud's deflation. | concept | ethics, religion
- [[Concepts/Pythagoreanism and Orphism]] — The overlapping-but-distinct relation of two Greek religious strands (Betegh, Huffman vol.); non-essentialist (neither metempsychosis nor vegetarianism is a shared core), with the late Orpheus→Pythagoras chain an Iamblichan fiction. | concept | religion, history-of-philosophy
- [[Concepts/Pythagorean Mathematics]] — What "Pythagorean mathematics" was (Netz, Huffman vol.): a short-lived "network," with Archytas the hinge ("there were no cards"); the number-doctrine is Aristotle's reading of Philolaus; no special "pebble arithmetic." | concept | mathematics, history-of-science
- [[Concepts/Pythagorean Harmonics]] — The genuine, testable ratio-theory of the *kanonikoi* (Barker, Huffman vol.): concords as 2:1/3:2/4:3, the monochord, the *Sectio canonis*; pioneered by Hippasus, integrated by Philolaus, perfected by Archytas. | concept | mathematics, music-theory
- [[Concepts/Pseudo-Pythagorean Writings]] — The vast Doric pseudepigrapha (ps.-Archytas, ps.-Timaeus, etc.) in which "Pythagoreanism" survived its extinction (Centrone): Pythagoreanizing Platonism, source of *nomos empsychos* and the Academic One/Dyad. | concept | history-of-philosophy, reception-history
- [[Concepts/The Three Ancient Lives of Pythagoras]] — Diogenes Laertius (neutral compiler), Porphyry (Platonist admirer), Iamblichus (Neoplatonic divinizer): the 3rd–4th c. AD biographies that funnel the Pythagoras legend, each reflecting its author's agenda. | concept | reception-history, biography
- [[Concepts/Pythagoras in Medieval and Renaissance Thought]] — The Latin afterlife (Hicks, Allen): Boethius and the quadrivium, Macrobius' harmony of the spheres, then Ficino's *prisca theologia* Pythagoras — the channel from "reality as number" to Kepler. | concept | reception-history, history-of-ideas
- [[Concepts/Heraclitean Flux and Logos (Heraclitus)]] — The Ephesus metaphysics of perpetual change (*panta rhei*, fire) balanced by the *Logos*, a rational order regulating the strife and unity of opposites.
- [[Concepts/Parmenidean Being and the One (Parmenides)]] — The Eleatic monist thesis that Being is one, eternal, indivisible, and unchanging; change and non-being are logical impossibilities.
- [[Concepts/Zeno_s Paradoxes of Motion (Zeno)]] — Paradoxes (like Achilles, the Arrow, the Stadium) designed by Zeno of Elea to show that multiplicity and motion are logically contradictory, defending Parmenidean monism.
- [[Concepts/Four Elements and Love and Strife (Empedocles)]] — The pluralist cosmology that all physical reality consists of four roots (earth, air, fire, water) combined and separated by two cosmic forces: Love (attraction) and Strife (repulsion).
- [[Concepts/Nous as Cosmic Ordering Force (Anaxagoras)]] — Mind (*Nous*) as the active, intelligent force that initiates a cosmic vortex to separate, order, and govern the infinite, qualitatively distinct seeds of matter.
- [[Concepts/Atomism - Atoms and Void (Leucippus and Democritus)]] — The materialist theory that the universe consists entirely of indivisible particles (atoms) of varying shapes moving through empty space (void) by mechanical necessity.

**Platonic Dialogues (2026-06-11 Ingest)**:
- [[Concepts/Ladder of Love and Form of Beauty (Plato Symposium)]] — The metaphysical ascent in Plato's *Symposium* (delivered by Diotima) from physical attraction to the universal, absolute Form of Beauty itself.
- [[Concepts/Chariot Allegory and Divine Madness (Plato Phaedrus)]] — The allegory of the soul as a chariot with a rational charioteer and two horses (noble spirit vs. unruly appetite), and the defense of love/philosophy as divine madness (*mania*).
- [[Concepts/Method of Division and Definition of Sophist (Plato Sophist)]] — The dialectical method of dividing genera into species to define a subject, and the proving of the existence of "Non-Being" as Difference/Otherness.
- [[Concepts/Rule of Law vs Royal Art of Statesmanship (Plato Statesman)]] — The contrast between the ideal ruler acting with absolute wisdom (royal art) and the rule of law as a necessary, second-best safeguard in actual cities.
- [[Concepts/Atlantis]] - Plato's invented lost island empire (concentric rings, Poseidon cult, bull rituals, hubris leading to war & cataclysm); from *Timaeus & Critias*, likely political/moral allegory.


**Classical Greek Philosophy & History (Aristotle + Thucydides + Stoics/Plato notes, 2026-06-04)**:
- [[Concepts/Eudaimonia]] - Highest human good as activity of the soul in accordance with virtue (*arête*) over a complete life (*teleiotēs*); function (*ergon*) argument; political science as architectonic (Nicomachean Ethics Book I).
- [[Concepts/Wisdom as Knowledge of First Causes (Aristotle)]] — "All men by nature desire to know." The cognitive ladder sensation→memory→experience→art→wisdom; knowing *why* vs. *that*; wisdom = science of first causes, pursued for its own sake; no infinite regress of causes; method proportioned to subject (*Metaphysics* I–II). | concept | epistemology, metaphysics
- [[Concepts/The Four Causes (Aristotle)]] — The four irreducible kinds of "why": material, formal, efficient, final. Teleology as a real principle of nature; doubles as the grid by which Book I judges predecessors ("grasped one cause, like one who lisps"). Expelled (final cause) by the Scientific Revolution; challenged by Hume; partly recovered by Pearl. | concept | metaphysics, philosophy-of-science
- [[Concepts/Being qua Being (Aristotle)]] — First philosophy as the study of beings simply insofar as they are. "Being is said in many ways" unified by *pros hen* (focal) reference to substance (health analogy); four senses of being; the ontology/theology seam. | concept | metaphysics, ontology
- [[Concepts/Principle of Non-Contradiction (Aristotle)]] — The firmest principle: a thing cannot be and not-be F at once in the same respect. Indemonstrable but defended by *elenchus* (the denier must mean something); refutes Protagorean relativism and Heraclitean flux; the practical "why not walk into a well?" argument. Excluded middle. | concept | metaphysics, logic
- [[Concepts/Substance (Ousia) (Aristotle)]] — *Ousia* as primary being; the question "what is being?" = "what is substance?" Of four candidates (substratum, universal, composite, form), form/essence is primary; thisness + separability as the marks of substance. Anti-Platonic core. | concept | metaphysics, ontology
- [[Concepts/Essence and Definition (Aristotle)]] — *To ti ēn einai*, "the what-it-was-to-be": what a thing is in virtue of itself, given by real definition; definition belongs primarily to substance; a primary substance is *identical* with its essence (else regress, no knowledge). | concept | metaphysics, epistemology
- [[Concepts/Form and Matter - Hylomorphism (Aristotle)]] — Sensible substance = matter (*hylē*, potential) + form (*morphē*, actual), one thing under two aspects; solves the unity-of-the-composite problem via act/potency; form is ungenerated. The backbone of scholastic metaphysics, broken by Descartes. | concept | metaphysics, philosophy-of-nature
- [[Concepts/Potentiality and Actuality (Aristotle)]] — *Dynamis* vs. *energeia*/*entelecheia*; *energeia* (complete) vs. *kinēsis* (process); the master-thesis that actuality is prior to potentiality in formula, time, and substance; eternal = pure actuality. Bridges to the unmoved mover and Aquinas's *actus purus*. | concept | metaphysics, philosophy-of-nature
- [[Concepts/Unity and the One (Aristotle)]] — "One" as the first measure of a kind; neither unity nor being is a substance or genus; identity/likeness vs. otherness/difference; contrariety as complete difference rooted in privation; perishable ≠ imperishable in kind. | concept | metaphysics, ontology
- [[Concepts/The Unmoved Mover (Aristotle)]] — Eternal motion requires an eternal, purely actual, immaterial substance that moves "as an object of love" (final cause), whose life is *noēsis noēseōs*, thought thinking itself. Aristotle's natural theology (Book XII); ancestor of the cosmological argument — but a god that neither creates nor knows particulars. | concept | metaphysics, theology
- [[Concepts/Critique of Platonic Forms (Aristotle)]] — The Forms redundantly double the world, are causally inert, breed the Third Man regress, cannot exist apart (a thing is one with its essence), and collapse when reduced to numbers; mathematicals exist only by abstraction (Books I, XIII–XIV). Primary text for the Plato/Aristotle split. | concept | metaphysics, epistemology
- [[Concepts/Participation Problems and the Critique of the Forms (Plato Parmenides)]] — Plato's own self-critique in the *Parmenides* Part I: the whole/part participation dilemma, the Third Man regress, and the "Greatest Difficulty" (radical separation makes Forms unknowable to us and human things unknowable to gods). | concept | metaphysics, epistemology
- [[Concepts/The Third Man Argument]] — Infinite-regress objection to the Forms (a Form + its instances need a further Form, endlessly); driven by self-predication + non-identity. Originates in Plato's *Parmenides* 132a–b, inherited by Aristotle. | concept | metaphysics, logic
- [[Concepts/The Eight Deductions - Hypotheses about the One (Plato Parmenides)]] — *Parmenides* Part II: the dialectical gymnastic tracing "if one is"/"if one is not" for the One and the Others. The One taken absolutely has no predicate (→ Neoplatonic One beyond Being); as a "Unit of Is" it admits all. Includes the instant (*to exaiphnes*). | concept | metaphysics, logic
- [[Concepts/Soul as Form of the Body (Hylomorphism, Aristotle)]] — Soul as first actuality of a natural body, not a separate substance; wax/stamped-shape analogy; rejects Platonic self-moving soul, atomist and harmony theories. | concept | metaphysics, philosophy-of-mind
- [[Concepts/Theory of Perception (Aristotle)]] — Sense as reception of sensible form without matter (wax-seal analogy); proper vs. common sensibles; the medium (transparent/diaphanous) required for most senses; touch as foundational. | concept | philosophy-of-mind, epistemology
- [[Concepts/Imagination and Common Sense (Aristotle)]] — General sensibility perceives common sensibles and grounds self-awareness of perceiving; imagination as a derivative "movement" from sensation, distinct from perception and belief. | concept | philosophy-of-mind
- [[Concepts/Active and Passive Intellect (Aristotle)]] — Passive intellect "becomes all things" (destructible); active intellect "makes all things," "alone immortal and eternal"; desire, not mind alone, produces animal motion. | concept | philosophy-of-mind, metaphysics
- [[Concepts/Sleep and Waking (Aristotle)]] — Sleep as periodic privation of waking, seated in the primary/common sense-organ; physiological cause is nutriment evaporation and reflux; final cause is conservation. | concept | philosophy-of-mind, physiology
- [[Concepts/Dreams as Residual Sense-Movements (Aristotle)]] — Dreaming as *phantasia*'s activity: a residual sensory movement persisting after stimulation, amplified by sleep, mistaken for present perception. | concept | philosophy-of-mind
- [[Concepts/Memory and Recollection (Aristotle)]] — Memory as seal-impression contemplated as a likeness of the absent past; recollection as associative search (similarity/contrast/contiguity) exclusive to deliberative animals; founds associationism. | concept | philosophy-of-mind, epistemology
- [[Concepts/Divination in Sleep (Aristotle)]] — Naturalistic verdict on prophetic dreams: cause/token/coincidence taxonomy; most dreams are coincidence; denies divine sending; rejects Democritus's images/emanations theory. | concept | philosophy-of-mind, philosophy-of-religion
- [[Concepts/Doctrine of the Mean (Aristotle)]] — Virtue as a habituated disposition lying in a mean "relative to us" between excess and deficiency, fixed by the judgment of the practically wise person; some acts admit no mean. | concept | ethics
- [[Concepts/Voluntary Action and Choice (Aristotle)]] — An act is voluntary when its moving principle is in the agent; choice (*prohairesis*) is deliberate desire about means, following deliberation restricted to contingent matters. | concept | ethics
- [[Concepts/Moral Virtues as Means (Aristotle)]] — Courage, temperance, liberality, magnificence, magnanimity, good temper as instances of the mean; courage requires nobility as motive, temperance governs only touch/taste. | concept | ethics
- [[Concepts/Justice - Universal, Particular, and Equity (Aristotle)]] — Distributive justice (geometric proportion by merit) vs. corrective justice (arithmetic proportion); equity as law's self-correction for cases its generality cannot anticipate. | concept | ethics, political-philosophy
- [[Concepts/Practical Wisdom - Phronesis (Aristotle)]] — The intellectual virtue of deliberating well about the humanly good; irreducible to art/science; impossible in the young; mutually necessary with moral virtue. | concept | ethics, epistemology
- [[Concepts/Akrasia - Weakness of Will (Aristotle)]] — Resolves the Socratic puzzle of acting against one's own judgment via the having/using distinction; incontinence is curable, vice is not. | concept | ethics, philosophy-of-mind
- [[Concepts/Friendship - Philia (Aristotle)]] — Three kinds (utility, pleasure, virtue); virtue-friendship between equals alone primary; modeled on proper self-love; even the self-sufficient need friends. | concept | ethics, political-philosophy
- [[Concepts/Contemplation and the Best Life - Theoria (Aristotle)]] — Pleasure as a complete whole supervening on activity; the contemplative life (*theōria*) as the highest happiness, most self-sufficient and divine; practical life happy "in a secondary degree." | concept | ethics, metaphysics
- [[Concepts/Household Management and the Ranking of Wealth-Getting (Pseudo-Aristotle)]] — Household management as prior to statecraft; husband/wife and master/slave as mean-governed natural relations; ranked wealth-getting (agriculture > mining > trade > war). | concept | political-philosophy, ethics
- [[Concepts/Revenue-Raising Stratagems (Pseudo-Aristotle Economics)]] — Amoral catalogue of historical state fiscal expedients (forced loans, currency debasement, religious pretext, manufactured emergencies), in tension with Book I's household ethics. | concept | political-philosophy, economics
- [[Concepts/Five-Element Cosmology (Pseudo-Aristotle)]] — Five nested elements (earth/water/air/fire/ether); ether as incorruptible fifth element in perpetual circular motion; sharp sublunary/superlunary divide. | concept | cosmology
- [[Concepts/God as Cosmic Sustaining Cause (Pseudo-Aristotle)]] — God sustains the universe via a graduated causal chain, not direct administration; king/satrap, puppeteer, chorus-leader analogies; conflicts with Aristotle's genuine Unmoved Mover. | concept | theology, metaphysics
- [[Concepts/Political Animal and the Natural Polis (Aristotle)]] — Man is by nature a *zōon politikon*; the polis is the natural, self-sufficient end of household→village→polis, prior to the individual as whole is prior to part. | concept | politics
- [[Concepts/Natural Slavery (Aristotle)]] — Natural (deliberative-faculty deficiency) vs. conventional (conquest) slavery; graded rational-authority hierarchy of slave/woman/child in the household. | concept | politics, ethics
- [[Concepts/Household Management and Property (Aristotle)]] — *Oikonomia* vs *chrēmatistikē*; natural self-limiting acquisition vs. unnatural/unlimited wealth-getting; usury as money breeding money. | concept | politics, economics
- [[Concepts/Critique of Plato's Republic (Aristotle)]] — Excess unity destroys rather than perfects a state; private property with common use beats communism of property/wives/children; the *Laws*' 5,000-citizen state is materially impossible. | concept | politics
- [[Concepts/Classification of Constitutions (Aristotle)]] — True forms (kingship, aristocracy, polity, ruling for common good) vs. deviant forms (tyranny, oligarchy, democracy, ruling for rulers' advantage); *pambasileia* exception for transcendent virtue. | concept | politics
- [[Concepts/Distributive Justice and the Rule of Law (Aristotle)]] — Proportional (not numerical) justice; "law is reason unaffected by desire" preferred to personal rule outside the *pambasileia* case; wisdom-of-the-multitude argument. | concept | politics, ethics
- [[Concepts/Polity and the Middle-Class Constitution (Aristotle)]] — Polity as fusion of oligarchy/democracy; large middle class as the strongest predictor of constitutional stability. | concept | politics
- [[Concepts/Stasis and Constitutional Change (Aristotle)]] — Revolution's feeling/motive/occasion structure; misapplied equality as root cause; disproportionate growth of a part; oppressive vs. "moderate-kingly" tyranny-preservation strategies. | concept | politics
- [[Concepts/The Best State and the Good Life (Aristotle)]] — Individual and political good identical (virtuous activity); self-sufficiency and leisure (*scholē*), not scale, size the ideal city; excludes laborers/tradesmen from full citizenship. | concept | politics, ethics
- [[Concepts/Education and Music in the Ideal State (Aristotle)]] — Public constitution-directed education; music's genuine mimetic power over character (Dorian endorsed, Phrygian/flute limited); text breaks off mid-sentence. | concept | politics, aesthetics
- [[Concepts/Enthymeme]] - Rhetorical syllogism from probabilities and signs; central to persuasion alongside *ethos*, *logos*, *pathos*; three branches of oratory (Aristotle Rhetoric Book I).
- [[Concepts/Modal Copula (Aristotelian Modality)]] - Modality via modalized copula (weak vs strong necessity) grounded in essentialism (essence/accident); link to scientific demonstration; critique of de dicto/de re (Organon/Prior Analytics via Patterson).
- [[Concepts/Eternity of the World (Ibn Rushd)]] - Aristotelian thesis (defended by Averroes against Ghazali) of an eternal ordered cosmos with no absolute temporal beginning; God as eternal simultaneous cause. Key tensions with temporal creation, potentiality, and divine will. Primary Islamic source for the debate now in vault.
- [[Concepts/Occasionalism vs Natural Causation (Ghazali and Ibn Rushd)]] - Ghazali/Ash‘arite denial of necessary connections or inherent powers in things (God creates every effect directly; “habit” not necessity) vs. Averroes’ Aristotelian defense of real essences, four causes, and observed secondary causation as conditions of intellect and science.
