Directly grounds Aquinas’ natural law and “grace perfects nature.” *(2026-06-29: primary Ghazali text added — incl. his two distinct causal theories and the fire-and-cotton passage.)*
- [[Concepts/Negative Theology and Divine Attributes (Maimonides)]] - God absolutely simple; no positive essential attribute predicable of Him; known only by negations + attributes of action; scriptural anthropomorphisms are homonyms. Aquinas softens to analogy; Spinoza inverts to immanent substance. | concept | metaphysics, theology
- [[Concepts/Critique of the Kalam (Maimonides)]] - *Guide* I.71–76: reconstructs and REJECTS the Mutakallimun's atomism + occasionalism (the doctrine Ghazali defends) as physics bent to dogma; nature has real stable forces. Allies Maimonides with Averroes/Aristotle on causation. | concept | metaphysics, epistemology
- [[Concepts/Creation vs Eternity (Maimonides)]] - Neither eternity nor creation is demonstrable; God proved even granting eternity; creation ex nihilo accepted on prophecy + a design argument. Distinct third position vs. Ghazali (disproof) and Averroes (eternity); adopted by Aquinas. | concept | metaphysics, cosmology
- [[Concepts/Prophecy (Maimonides)]] - Naturalized: an emanation from the Active Intellect through the rational + imaginative faculties; requires perfected intellect, imagination, morals; Moses the homonymous exception. | concept | philosophy-of-mind, theology
- [[Concepts/Providence and the Problem of Evil (Maimonides)]] - Evil as privation, mostly self-inflicted; providence proportional to intellectual perfection (species-only for animals); five theories of providence; Job as parable with an intellectual resolution. | concept | metaphysics, theology
- [[Concepts/Reasons for the Commandments (Maimonides)]] - Ta'amei ha-mitzvot: no precept arbitrary; Law serves welfare of soul + body; ritual (esp. sacrifice) as pedagogical weaning from Sabean idolatry; intellectual perfection + imitatio Dei as the end. | concept | ethics, political-philosophy
- [[Concepts/Criterion of Demonstrability (Ghazali)]] - Al-Ghazali's governing method: scripture stands literally unless its impossibility is *demonstrated* (strict *burhan*, by the philosophers' own logic); the *Tahafut* "only refutes," pressing every sect's objections without defending one. | concept | epistemology, theology
- [[Concepts/Eternal Will and Temporal Creation (Ghazali)]] - The world created in time by an eternal will that specifies its beginning-moment; will = "the attribute that differentiates a thing from its similar," needing no further determinant; "duration and time are both created." First of the 3 condemned doctrines. | concept | metaphysics, theology
- [[Concepts/God's Knowledge of Particulars (Ghazali)]] - Disc. 13: Avicenna's claim that God knows only universals (to protect immutability) vs. al-Ghazali's charge that it denies omniscience and "uproots religious laws." Second condemned doctrine. | concept | metaphysics, theology
- [[Concepts/Bodily Resurrection and the Immaterial Soul (Ghazali)]] - Disc. 18–20: the soul's immateriality is undemonstrated, but *even if* granted, bodily resurrection remains possible and is scripturally required. Third condemned doctrine + the takfir verdict. | concept | metaphysics, eschatology
- [[Concepts/Power, Empire, and Revolt (Thucydides)]] - Empire generates revolt when “alliances” become domination; power asymmetry, diplomacy vs force, siege strategy, rhetoric of justification (honor, necessity, justice) in interstate relations (History Book III, Mytilene revolt).
- [[Concepts/Dichotomy of Control (Epictetus)]] - What is in our power (*prohairesis* – rational choice/judgment, our own doing) vs not (externals: body, reputation, fate); focus desire/aversion only on the former for freedom and equanimity (Enchiridion and related).
- [[Concepts/Prohairesis (Moral Purpose)]] — The faculty of rational choice; for Epictetus the *only* thing genuinely ours and therefore the sole seat of good, evil, and freedom. The metaphysical ground of the dichotomy of control. | concept | ethics
- [[Concepts/Proper Use of Impressions and the Discipline of Assent (Epictetus)]] — The one power Zeus left wholly in our hands: to test each impression (*phantasia*) before assenting; disturbance comes from the judgment, not the event. Logic as instrumental assay. | concept | ethics, epistemology
- [[Concepts/Preconceptions (Prolepseis) (Epictetus)]] — Innate general notions (good, just, fitting) shared by all and correct in the abstract; philosophy begins in the conflict over their application to particulars and the search for a criterion (Disc. II.11). | concept | ethics, epistemology
- [[Concepts/Kinship with God (Epictetus)]] — Humans as *sungeneis* (kin) and portions of the divine reason, watched by an inner *daemon*; grounds dignity, fearlessness, and glad piety (willing what happens). | concept | ethics, philosophy-of-religion
- [[Concepts/The Three Fields of Training - Desire, Action, and Assent (Epictetus)]] — Epictetus's own curriculum (Disc. III.2): the three *topoi* — desire, action/duty, assent — worked by *askēsis*; the primary-text root of Hadot's three disciplines. | concept | ethics
- [[Concepts/The Cynic Ideal - Scout of Zeus (Epictetus)]] — The true Cynic (Disc. III.22) as *kataskopos*, God's possessionless scout sent to show mankind where good and evil lie; the Stoic sage made visible. | concept | ethics, philosophy-of-religion
- [[Concepts/Stoic Freedom (Eleutheria) (Epictetus)]] — Freedom as a condition of the will, not legal status (Disc. IV.1): want only what is up to you and nothing can enslave you; identical with willing what happens. Exemplars Socrates and Diogenes. | concept | ethics, political-philosophy
- [[Concepts/Teachability of Virtue (Plato Protagoras Meno)]] - Can *arete* (virtue) be taught? Intellectualism (virtue as knowledge/measurement, all wrongdoing ignorance); recollection (*anamnesis*); hypothesis method; true opinion vs knowledge (Protagoras, Meno, related dialogues).
- [[Concepts/Pleasure vs Intelligence as the Good (Philebus)]] - Whether *hedone* (pleasure) or *phronesis* (intelligence) is the good; mixed life with intellect as cause of measure; *apeiron*/*peras*/mixture/cause ontology (Philebus).
- [[Concepts/Ataraxia]] - Epicurean goal of mental tranquility/peace of mind (absence of pain/fear via prudent pleasure, "death is nothing," "live unnoticed," friendship, simple life). Refined hedonism vs. vulgar excess; links to agency and equanimity.
- [[Concepts/Epicurean Atomism (Swerve)]] - Atoms (finite types, inherent motion) + void (infinite space) as fundamental; infinite worlds formed/dissolved by blind collisions; swerve (clinamen) for indeterminacy, world-formation, and free will. Empiricist epistemology (senses criterion, multiple explanations). Anti-teleological materialism.
- [[Concepts/Live Unnoticed (Lathe Biosas)]] - Epicurean maxim: strategic withdrawal from public ambition/turmoil for ataraxia ("pass your life making sure no one notices you"). Simple, private life of modest pleasures and friendship; society as contract for security (not natural sociability).
- [[Concepts/The One (To Hen)]] - Plotinus/Neoplatonism: the ultimate, transcendent, ineffable source of all reality (the Good beyond being/intellect). Emanates hierarchically without loss or will (One → Intellect → Soul → matter); object of all desire and the telos of the soul's return (henosis). Dionysius: "most important Title of all" (DN XIII), then unsays even "one" and "unity" (MT V).
- [[Concepts/Emanation (Proodos)]] - Plotinus: the necessary, undiminished "overflow" (procession) by which the One generates the hierarchy of reality (hypostases). Each level an image of the prior; complements return (epistrophe). Dionysius: names as "beneficent Emanations"; creation as ecstasy of Divine Love that stays within itself (DN IV.13).
- [[Concepts/Super-Essential Godhead (Dionysius)]] - Hidden Godhead beyond being, mind, life, unity, and every name: "Universal Cause of existence while Itself existing not" (DN I.1). Rolt's Bradley-Absolute reading flagged as interpolation.
- [[Concepts/Cataphatic and Apophatic Theology (Dionysius)]] - Complementary methods: affirm the scriptural names from effects; deny them, then deny the denials. No contradiction, because the Cause precedes both (MT I).
- [[Concepts/Divine Names (Dionysius)]] - Scriptural titles name powers that stream to us, not the Mystery; undifferenced titles apply to the whole Godhead; Father/Son/Spirit do not interchange.
- [[Concepts/Divine Darkness and Unknowing (Dionysius)]] - Term of *Mystical Theology*: Moses meets not God but the place, then plunges into dazzling obscurity; knowledge that exceeds understanding by rejection of all knowledge.
- [[Concepts/Ecstasy of Divine Love (Dionysius)]] - Good extends itself by existing (DN IV.1); Yearning "allowed Him not to remain unfruitful in Himself" (IV.10); Creator transported outside Himself yet stays within (IV.13). Lovejoy's plenitude line, primary.
- [[Concepts/Hierarchy of Existence, Life, and Intelligence (Dionysius)]] - One God under more universal / more particular names (Being > Life > Wisdom); creatures rank the other way (intelligences include the lower gifts). Christian rewriting of the Neoplatonic Being–Life–Intelligence triad.
- [[Concepts/Return of the Soul (Epistrophe)]] - Plotinus: the soul's natural ascent/reversion from descent (embodiment, multiplicity, forgetting) back to the One via purification, virtue as "likeness to God," dialectic, and contemplation (henosis/union). Telos of the philosopher; "flight of the alone to the Alone." Model for wiki maintenance (integration of scattered sources into unified vision), career ascent, and contemplative craft.
- [[Concepts/Theurgy (Iamblichus)]] - "Divine work": ritual practice from the *Chaldaean Oracles* aimed at union with the gods via cosmic correspondence and divine grace; rejected by Plotinus, limited by Porphyry, made the primary means of salvation by Iamblichus and the Athenian School. | concept | philosophy-of-religion, metaphysics
- [[Concepts/The Undescended Soul (Plotinus vs Iamblichus)]] - Plotinus's most controversial innovation: a level of every soul never leaves the Intelligible world (Enn. IV.8.8), so contemplation can complete the return; Iamblichus denied it (soul fully descends), grounding the case for theurgy and grace. | concept | metaphysics, philosophy-of-mind
- [[Concepts/The Henads (Proclus)]] - Proclus's divine Unities within the First Hypostasis: participated intermediaries between the unknowable One and everything below — metaphysical bridge, philosophical home of the pagan gods, knowable by analogy. | concept | metaphysics, philosophy-of-religion
- [[Concepts/The Three Gods - Father, Creator, and Creation (Numenius)]] - Numenius's graded triad: First God (simple uncreating Intellect / the Good, "the alone with the alone"), Second God (the "double" Demiurge who orders matter, the pilot steering the ship of cosmos), Third God (the world itself). The clearest named ancestor of Plotinus's One–Intellect–Soul hypostases; undiminished giving (candle-from-candle). | concept | metaphysics, philosophy-of-religion
- [[Concepts/Matter as a Malevolent Principle (Numenius)]] - Numenius's dualist doctrine: matter is not neutral (Stoics) or mere privation (Plotinus) but a positive, uncreated, evil Indefinite Dyad coeval with God; two world-souls (one beneficent, one malign); evil necessary; incarnation as fall. The dualism Plotinian monism defined itself against. | concept | metaphysics, philosophy-of-religion
- [[Concepts/Procession, Remaining, and Return (Neoplatonic Triad)]] - Monē–proodos–epistrophē: every effect abides in, proceeds from, and reverts upon its cause — the Athenian School's universal three-beat law of reality, generalizing Plotinus's two-stage emanation; ancestor of the Hegelian triad. | concept | metaphysics
- [[Concepts/Epoché (Suspension of Judgment)]] - Pyrrhonian (Sextus): state of intellect in which we neither affirm nor deny (non-evident); produced by equipollence of opposed claims/appearances (10+5 Modes); followed by ataraxia. Not paralysis — live by appearances + customs without belief ("chronicler"); "continue to search." Third Hellenistic path (vs. Epicurean physics or Stoic control). Method for wiki: opposition before synthesis, epistemic humility, flag equipollence in contradictions sections.
- [[Concepts/Equipollence (Isosthenia) and the Modes]] - Pyrrhonian: equality of credibility among inconsistent statements/appearances (no one takes precedence) → epoché. 10 Modes (Aenesidemus: animals [dog "dialectic"], humans, senses, circumstances, relativity as generic, customs etc.); 5 Modes (Agrippa: disagreement, regress, hypothesis, circularity); "to every argument an equal argument is opposed" (core practice/slogan). Engine for skeptical method; modern parallels in reasoning scaffolds, self-critique, cross-model opposition. Tool for lint/ingest (diagnose underdetermined claims).
- [[Concepts/Beatific Vision - Last End (Aquinas)]] - Aquinas (Summa I-II): ultimate happiness as the beatific vision (direct contemplation of God in His essence by the glorified intellect). Last end is God (uncreated); for man, the created participation/operation of union with Him. Elevates Aristotle's eudaimonia (natural happiness via virtue/activity) to supernatural beatitude (requires grace; imperfect in this life, perfect in heaven). "Grace does not destroy nature but perfects it."
- [[Concepts/Natural Law (Aquinas)]] - Aquinas: rational creature's participation in eternal law ("the light of natural reason whereby we discern what is good and what is evil"). First practical principle: "good is to be done and pursued, and evil avoided." Foundation for human law (common good, promulgated by authority) and ethics. Grounds/completes Aristotelian natural right; directed ultimately to supernatural end via grace/divine law.
- [[Concepts/Restless Heart - Rest in God (Augustine)]] - Augustine (*Confessions*): "You have made us for yourself and restless is our heart until it comes to rest in you." The soul is ordered to the infinite Good (God); all finite striving (pleasure, honor, even natural virtue) is partial or distorted search for this rest. Grace transforms the divided will; ultimate rest = beatific vision (elevating eudaimonia). Experiential ground for Aquinas' last end. See [[Sources/Confessions - Augustine]], [[Thinkers/Augustine]].
- [[Concepts/Memory - Interiority and Self-Knowledge (Augustine)]] - Augustine (Book X): memory as vast "fields," "palaces," "caverns" containing images, abstract knowledge (numbers, rules), and the self's history. "Let me know you, O my Knower." God sought within (more inward than inmost self) yet transcendent. Confession as deliberate, honest self-examination in God's presence for knowledge, humility, praise, and edification of others. Model for the wiki as externalized examined memory. See [[Sources/Confessions - Augustine]], [[Thinkers/Augustine]].
- [[Concepts/Time and Eternity - Distentio Animi (Augustine)]] - Augustine (Book XI): "What then is time? If no one asks me, I know; if I wish to explain it to one that asketh, I know not." Time is subjective distention of the soul (past in memory, present in attention, future in expectation); co-temporal with creation; God abides in eternity (simple, unchanging "today"). Grounds narrative, memory, and the possibility of rest. See [[Sources/Confessions - Augustine]], [[Thinkers/Augustine]].
- [[Concepts/Two Cities (Augustine)]] - *City of God*'s master framework: two cities by two loves — earthly city (love of self to contempt of God) vs. City of God (love of God to contempt of self). Interwoven throughout history from Cain/Abel to Last Judgment. Citizens of the City of God are pilgrims who use but do not worship earthly peace. "Remove justice, and what are kingdoms but gangs of criminals on a large scale?"
- [[Concepts/Tranquillitas Ordinis - Peace as Order (Augustine)]] - Augustine's definition of peace (*City of God* XIX.13): *pax omnium rerum tranquillitas ordinis* — "the peace of all things is the tranquillity of order." Covers body, soul, household, city, cosmos, and the heavenly city. "Even wars are waged with peace as their object." Supreme Good = peace in life everlasting. Foundation of just war theory.
- [[Concepts/Universal Way of Salvation (Augustine)]] - Porphyry admitted he had not found a *via universalis* for soul liberation. Augustine: Christ is that universal Way — "as God, he is the goal; as man, he is the way." The Platonists see the destination but lack the road; pride bars them from accepting the humble Mediator. Contrast with Plotinus's epistrophe and Pyrrhonian suspension.
- [[Concepts/Philosophy as Medicine (Seneca)]] - Seneca's governing metaphor across the 124 letters: philosophy diagnoses and treats diseases of the soul (fear of death, slavery to fortune, crowd-dependence, discursive reading, utility-friendship). Progress (*profectus*) as the realistic category for those not yet wise. Retirement (*otium*) as therapeutic withdrawal. The letter form enacts the therapy. Contrasts with Epicurean pleasure-management and Pyrrhonian suspension; relates to Augustinian confession as grace-enabled healing.
- [[Concepts/Ruling Faculty and the Daemon (Marcus Aurelius)]] — inner citadel / *hegemonikon* (portion of Zeus) as true self and guide; emperor's lived discipline keeping it pure amid power and campaign | concept | ethics
- [[Concepts/View from Above and Cosmic Perspective (Marcus Aurelius)]] — meditative rise to see whole earth as point, affairs tiny/transient, fame vapour; yields equanimity + focus on ruling faculty | concept | ethics
- [[Concepts/Every Act as if It Were the Last (Marcus Aurelius)]] — memento mori: do every act with simple dignity and justice as if it is the last; presence and relief from distraction | concept | ethics

**Practical Stoicism (Ryan Holiday, *The Obstacle Is the Way*, 2014)**:
- [[Concepts/The Three Disciplines - Perception, Action, and Will (Hadot)]] — Hadot's reconstruction of Epictetus's three *topoi* as lived physics/ethics/logic (desire, action, assent). Holiday's Perception → Action → Will tracks Marcus's *present* triad, not Epictetus's school order. Primary now in [[Sources/Philosophy as a Way of Life - Pierre Hadot (1995)]]. | concept | ethics
- [[Concepts/Philosophy as a Way of Life (Hadot)]] — Greco-Roman *philosophia* is a choice of existence; discourse *about* philosophy is not philosophy. University philosophy is usually a professor's form of life; ancient attitudes remain usable. | concept | ethics, metaphilosophy
- [[Concepts/Spiritual Exercises (Hadot)]] — Inner practices in which thought modifies itself; *askēsis* ≠ asceticism; four learnings (live, dialogue, die, read); *prosoche* as the key. | concept | ethics, metaphilosophy
- [[Concepts/Creative Mistakes and Philosophical Exegesis (Hadot)]] — Two millennia of philosophy as exegesis of authentic texts; forced readings (*contresens*) generate new ideas (Porphyry on the *Parmenides*). | concept | history-of-philosophy, hermeneutics
- [[Concepts/The Present Instant (Hadot)]] — Happiness only in the present; one instant = an eternity. Epicurean enjoys the instant, Stoic wills it. Goethe's formula; Nietzsche's yes to one moment. | concept | ethics, philosophy-of-time
- [[Concepts/Turning Obstacles Upside Down (Holiday)]] — the book's thesis: every impediment is convertible into advantage ("what stands in the way becomes the way," *Meditations* 5.20); adversarial growth; caveat on instrumentalizing virtue. | concept | ethics
- [[Concepts/Amor Fati (Love of Fate)]] — GS §276 primary: see the necessary as beautiful, make things beautiful, Yes-sayer. Not in-text the same as recurrence. Stoic cousin and Holiday's "fuel" are reception. | concept | ethics
- [[Concepts/Premeditatio Malorum (Premeditation of Evils)]] — Stoic rehearsal of adversity in advance (negative visualization); Seneca's model; ancestor of the corporate "premortem." | concept | ethics
- [[Concepts/Memento Mori (Stoic Practice)]] — "remember you are mortal"; mortality-awareness as clarifying discipline (Montaigne, Seneca, Marcus); contrast with Epicurus's "death is nothing." | concept | ethics
- [[Concepts/The Inner Citadel (Hadot)]] — Hadot's fortress-of-the-mind (the *hēgemonikon* as impregnable retreat); built, not born (T. Roosevelt); resilience through preparation. | concept | ethics

**Early Modern / Rationalism (Descartes, 2026-06-04)**:
- [[Concepts/Method of Hyperbolic Doubt (Descartes)]] - Systematic, universal, methodical doubt (senses, dream argument, evil demon) as tool to demolish prejudices and reach bedrock certainty for reconstruction (not Pyrrhonian suspension/ataraxia). First Meditation; model for vault ingest/lint and reasoning scaffolds.
- [[Concepts/Cogito (Descartes)]] - "I am, I exist" necessarily true whenever conceived (even under maximal deception). The 'I' is a thinking thing (*res cogitans*). Archimedean point; performative self-certainty.
- [[Concepts/Clear and Distinct Perception (Descartes)]] - Criterion of truth: whatever is perceived very clearly and distinctly is true (secured by God as non-deceiver). Requires attention and withdrawal from senses/prejudices. Central epistemic standard; vault quality bar for claims and syntheses.
- [[Concepts/Res Cogitans and Res Extensa (Descartes)]] - Two really distinct substances (thinking/non-extended mind vs. extended/non-thinking body) yet closely conjoined/fused in the human composite (not pilot-in-ship). Primary vs. secondary qualities. Implications for vault layered architecture (raw vs. wiki, intellect vs. sensation).
- [[Concepts/Causal Argument for God's Existence from Objective Reality (Descartes)]] - Idea of God has more objective reality than any finite cause (including self) can supply; causal principle ("at least as much reality in cause as effect") requires God as cause. Plus conservation as continuous creation. Complements ontological; model for tracing sufficient causes.
- [[Concepts/The Four Precepts of the Method (Descartes)]] - Descartes's four-rule reduction of logic/analysis/algebra (Discourse Part II): evidence (accept only the clear and distinct), analysis (divide difficulties), order (simple → complex), enumeration (complete review). Modeled on geometry; charter for disciplined method and vault ingest.
- [[Concepts/Provisional Morality (Descartes)]] - The provisory three-maxim code (Discourse Part III) that keeps life livable while belief is rebuilt: conform to custom/religion; be resolute once decided; master yourself rather than fortune. The third maxim is a rationalist restatement of the Stoic dichotomy of control. | concept | ethics, epistemology
- [[Concepts/The Two Tests and the Animal-Machine (Descartes)]] - The body as a God-made machine and the *bête-machine* thesis (animals have no reason); the two tests — language and universal reason — that no machine or beast could pass (Discourse Part V). Direct ancestor of the Turing Test; Turing keeps the criterion but rejects the dualist verdict. | concept | philosophy-of-mind, metaphysics

**Early Modern / Rationalism (Spinoza, 2026-06-04)**:
- [[Concepts/Deus sive Natura - Substance Monism (Spinoza)]] - Only one substance (God or Nature); everything is in God and cannot be or be conceived without God. Finite things are modes, not separate substances or parts. Natura naturans (active attributes) vs. natura naturata (modes). Immanent cause; no transcendent creator.
- [[Concepts/Attributes and Parallelism (Spinoza)]] - God has infinite attributes (we know thought and extension), each expressing the essence of the one substance. "The order and connection of ideas is the same as the order and connection of things" (2p7). Mind and body (or sources and wiki representations) are one and the same thing expressed under different attributes; no cross-attribute causation.
- [[Concepts/Conatus (Spinoza)]] - Each thing, as far as it can by its own power, strives to persevere in its being; this striving is the actual essence of the thing (3p6–7). Foundation of affects (joy = increase in power; sadness = decrease) and ethics (virtue = power; highest good = understanding that most augments power).
- [[Concepts/Affects and Active vs Passive Emotions (Spinoza)]] - Affects are bodily affections (changes in power of acting) together with their ideas. Active affects (actions) arise from adequate ideas (we act); passive affects/passions arise from inadequate ideas (we are acted on). Ethical task: increase active affects and freedom through knowledge.
- [[Concepts/Geometrical Method - Rationalism (Spinoza)]] - *More geometrico*: definitions of essences → axioms → deductive chain of propositions. Because conceptual order mirrors causal order in nature (1a4 + parallelism), the method reveals the actual natures and relations of things. Rationalism as the thorough intelligibility of reality.
- [[Concepts/Freedom and Blessedness - Intellectual Love of God (Spinoza)]] - Freedom = acting from the necessity of one’s own nature (adequate ideas). Blessedness (*beatitudo*) is the highest joy accompanying the intellectual love of God (*amor Dei intellectualis*)—the joy of intuitive knowledge (*scientia intuitiva*) of God/Nature as cause of our joy. Eternal (sub specie aeternitatis); our greatest virtue and happiness. "All things excellent are as difficult as they are rare."

**Early Modern / Empiricist Foundations (Locke, 2026-06-04)**:
- [[Concepts/Tabula Rasa - Blank Slate or Empty Cabinet (Locke)]] - The mind at birth is an empty cabinet or white paper, void of all characters; no innate ideas or principles. All ideas and knowledge are acquired through experience (sensation and reflection). The constructive starting point of British empiricism and direct foundation for Hume's Copy Principle.
- [[Concepts/No Innate Principles or Ideas (Locke)]] - Systematic refutation (Book I): no universal assent to candidate speculative maxims ("what is, is"; law of non-contradiction) or practical/moral rules; children, idiots, and diverse cultures lack them; "when we come to the use of reason" and "assent on first hearing" fail as proofs; component ideas (identity, God, etc.) are themselves acquired; conscience and moral practice are shaped by custom and interest. Primary anti-innatist text; explicit contrast to Descartes and Plato.
- [[Concepts/Sensation and Reflection as the Two Fountains of Ideas (Locke)]] - All ideas originate in EXPERIENCE via two sources: SENSATION (external objects → sensible qualities) and REFLECTION (mind's own operations → perceiving, thinking, doubting, willing, etc.). Mind starts as white paper; no other originals. Direct basis for Hume's impressions/ideas.
- [[Concepts/Simple Ideas (Locke)]] - Uncompounded uniform appearances (e.g., coldness/hardness as distinct as smell/whiteness); the atomic materials of knowledge. Mind passive in receiving them from the two fountains; cannot invent or destroy simple ideas, only compound them.
- [[Concepts/Primary and Secondary Qualities (Locke)]] - Primary (solidity, extension, figure, number, motion/rest): real, inseparable from body, resemblances in ideas. Secondary (colors, sounds, tastes, etc.): powers in bodies (depending on primary) to produce sensations in us; no resemblance in the bodies. Ideas (mind) carefully distinguished from qualities (bodies). Key for perception, science, and contrasts with Lucretius/Descartes/Hume.
- [[Concepts/State of Nature, Property, and Revolution (Locke)]] - State of nature (perfect freedom/equality under law of preservation); property by labor mixing with commons (spoilage + "enough and as good" provisos); government by consent to protect rights; right of revolution on breach of trust.
- [[Concepts/Toleration (Locke)]] - True religion = inward persuasion; magistrate force limited to civil interests (life/liberty/property); church voluntary; limits for atheists (no oaths), foreign-loyalty religions, intolerant sects.
- [[Concepts/Nominal and Real Essences (Locke)]] - Nominal essence = abstract idea/qualities by which we name/classify sorts (conventional); real essence = underlying insensible constitution (largely unknown). Explains limits on substance knowledge.
- [[Concepts/Association of Ideas (Locke)]] — custom or chance creates "wrong connexion" of ideas not allied by nature; leads to obstinacy, antipathies, "a degree of madness" in most men | concept | epistemology
- [[Concepts/Complex Ideas, Modes, Substances, and Relations (Locke)]] — mind's voluntary combinations of simples: modes (affections), substances (particular things + idea of substratum), relations (comparing) | concept | epistemology
- [[Concepts/Faith and Reason - Enthusiasm (Locke)]] — reason as natural revelation; faith as assent on divine credit above but not contrary to reason; enthusiasm (private "revelation" without evidence) is dangerous madness; reason last judge | concept | epistemology, religion
- [[Concepts/Identity and Diversity - Personal Identity (Locke)]] — identity = same existence at determined time/place; for persons, consciousness (not same substance or soul); personal identity extends as far as consciousness of past actions | concept | metaphysics
- [[Concepts/Knowledge as Perception of Agreement or Disagreement of Ideas (Locke)]] — knowledge = perception of agreement/disagreement of ideas (4 sorts: identity, relation, coexistence, real existence); degrees intuitive/demonstrative/sensitive | concept | epistemology
- [[Concepts/Words as Signs of Ideas and Language (Locke)]] — words voluntary signs of ideas in speaker's mind (arbitrary, not natural); general terms stand for abstract ideas; abuse when used without clear ideas or for things directly | concept | philosophy of language

**Early Modern / Immaterialism (Berkeley, 2026-06-11 Ingest)**:
- [[Concepts/Subjective Idealism (Berkeley)]] — The metaphysical doctrine that physical objects do not exist independently of minds; they are collections of ideas perceived by minds (esse est percipi), sustained continuously by the perception of God. | concept | metaphysics, epistemology

**Kantian Ethics (Kant, 2026-06-04)**:
- [[Concepts/Good Will (Kant)]] - The only thing good without limitation. Good in itself through its willing/principle (from duty/respect for law), not through effects or success in achieving ends. Even if it accomplishes nothing, it "shines like a jewel for itself." Nature's purpose for reason is a good will (the highest good and condition for all else), not happiness (which instinct could secure better).
- [[Concepts/Duty (Kant)]] - The necessity of an action from respect for the law. An action has moral worth only if done from duty (not merely in conformity with duty from inclination or prudence). Examples: preserving life or beneficence from duty when no inclination remains; promoting happiness from duty when inclinations conflict. Duty makes the good will visible under human hindrances.
- [[Concepts/Categorical Imperative - Universal Law Formulation (Kant)]] - "I ought never to conduct myself except so that I could also will that my maxim become a universal law." (Act only according to that maxim whereby you can at the same time will that it should become a universal law.) Test for duty: can the maxim be consistently willed as universal without self-contradiction or destroying the practice it presupposes? False promise example (universalized, promises lose all point). Recognized by common rational moral cognition; a priori formal law independent of consequences/inclination/happiness.
- [[Concepts/Hypothetical and Categorical Imperatives (Kant)]] - Hypothetical (conditional on ends willed: skill/technical or prudence for happiness) vs categorical (unconditional moral law, form/universal lawfulness only; apodictic, binding all rational wills). Only categorical yields practical law; all prior ethics reduce to hypothetical/heteronomous.
- [[Concepts/Formula of Humanity as End in Itself (Kant)]] - "Act so that you use humanity... always at the same time as end and never merely as means." Rational nature has absolute worth as objective end (not conditioned by inclination); persons limit arbitrary choice; grounds non-instrumentalization (examples: suicide, lying promise).
- [[Concepts/Kingdom of Ends (Kant)]] - Realm of rational beings as ends through their own universal self-legislation ("act as if... legislative member in a universal realm of ends"). "As if" the moral community were realized via maxims all could author; retains categorical force independent of others' compliance. Complements universal law + humanity.
- [[Concepts/Autonomy and Heteronomy of the Will (Kant)]] - Autonomy (will law to itself via maxims' suitability for universal legislation) = sole supreme principle of morality and ground of dignity. Heteronomy (law from any external object/inclination/"alien" source) yields only conditioned imperatives and cannot ground morality; diagnosis of all prior systems (happiness, feeling, perfection, divine will).
- [[Concepts/Freedom as the Key to Autonomy (Kant)]] - Freedom (independence of alien causes) flows into positive law-governed causality of special kind = autonomy = CI/moral law. "Free will and will under moral laws are the same." Must be presupposed for all rational beings under the *idea* of freedom (practical "as if"); synthetic a priori requires "third" (positive freedom) pointing beyond sense.
- [[Concepts/Two Standpoints: Sensible World and Intelligible World (Kant)]] - Same subject: as appearance in world of sense (natural laws, heteronomy, affected) vs as intelligence/thing in itself in intelligible world (free, autonomous, self-given moral law). Moral 'ought' = intelligible self's necessary volition for sensible self. "As if" member of intelligible makes CI possible; resolves freedom vs natural necessity dialectic without contradiction.
- [[Concepts/Dignity of Rational Nature (Kant)]] - *Würde* vs *Preis*: what has a price has an equivalent; dignity is raised above all price (4:434–435). Only morality, and humanity capable of it, has dignity. Autonomy is the ground. Korsgaard: dignity = share in giving universal laws / fitness for a kingdom of ends.
- [[Concepts/Copernican Turn (Kant)]] - Objects must conform to our cognition; we cognize a priori only what we put into them (B xvi–xviii). Limit that makes room for faith (B xxx).
- [[Concepts/Synthetic A Priori Judgments (Kant)]] - "How are synthetic judgments a priori possible?" (B 19). Analytic vs synthetic × a priori vs a posteriori; 7+5=12; challenge to Hume's Fork.
- [[Concepts/Transcendental Idealism (Kant)]] - Space, time, and objects in them empirically real, transcendentally ideal. Think vs cognize things in themselves.
- [[Concepts/Forms of Intuition - Space and Time (Kant)]] - Pure forms of sensibility; not Newtonian containers, not Leibnizian relations. Geometry possible only as construction in pure intuition.
- [[Concepts/Categories of the Understanding (Kant)]] - Twelve pure concepts of an object in general from the table of judgments. Metaphysical deduction (B 159).
- [[Concepts/Transcendental Deduction and Unity of Apperception (Kant)]] - *Quid juris*. "The I think must be able to accompany all my representations" (B 131–132). Categories apply only to possible experience.
- [[Concepts/Schematism and Principles of Pure Understanding (Kant)]] - Temporal schemata; Analogies (substance, causality, community); B Refutation of Idealism (B 275).
- [[Concepts/Phenomena and Noumena (Kant)]] - Noumenon as boundary concept, negative use only. Amphiboly vs Leibniz.
- [[Concepts/Transcendental Illusion and Ideas of Reason (Kant)]] - Natural, unavoidable dialectic. Three ideas: soul, world, God.
- [[Concepts/Paralogisms of Pure Reason (Kant)]] - "I think" is the sole text of rational psychology; no doctrine of a substantial/simple/personal soul.
- [[Concepts/Existence is Not a Real Predicate (Kant)]] - Being posits, it does not determine (A 598 / B 626). 100 dollars. Ontological, cosmological, and design proofs fail.
- [[Concepts/Regulative Use of the Ideas of Reason (Kant)]] - *Focus imaginarius* for systematic unity of inquiry; not constitutive objects.
- [[Concepts/Canon of Pure Reason - Moral Faith (Kant)]] - What can I know / should I do / may I hope? Highest good; "I am morally certain." Not yet the Groundwork.
- [[Concepts/Perfect and Imperfect Duties (Kant)]] - *Groundwork* §II four examples as a 2×2 (self/others × perfect/imperfect). Contradiction in *conception* (suicide, false promise) vs in the *will* (neglected talents, non-beneficence). Official tables reserved for the 1797 *MdS*.

**German Idealism & Post-Kantian Voluntarism (Hegel & Schopenhauer, 2026-06-11 Ingest)**:
- [[Concepts/Dialectical Method (Hegel)]] — Hegel's dynamic, self-correcting logic where concepts unfold through internal contradiction, negation, and speculative unification (sublation).
- [[Concepts/Geist (Absolute Spirit)]] — Spirit or Mind as a unified, historical, and rational process of self-folding development.
- [[Concepts/Master-Slave Dialectic (Hegel)]] — The developmental stage of self-consciousness where identity is forged through a struggle for recognition, labor, and material transformation.
- [[Concepts/Aufhebung (Sublation)]] — The threefold logical mechanism of the dialectic that simultaneously negates one-sidedness, preserves essential truth, and elevates concepts to a higher unity.
- [[Concepts/Cunning of Reason (Hegel)]] — The teleological historical process whereby Spirit uses the subjective passions and actions of individuals to realize the objective progress of freedom.
- [[Concepts/The Will (Schopenhauer)]] — The blind, striving, non-rational metaphysical force that constitutes the noumenal thing-in-itself, driving all desire and resulting in inevitable suffering. *Parerga* I: “the will alone is left as the real”; immanent dogmatism. *Fourfold Root*: Will is **outside** PSR. | concept | metaphysics, ethics
- [[Concepts/Fourfold Root of the Principle of Sufficient Reason (Schopenhauer)]] — Wolff's formula as abstraction; four a priori laws (becoming, knowing, being, acting); no ground *per se*; PSR does not apply to the world as totality | concept | epistemology, metaphysics
- [[Concepts/Intellectual Nature of Intuition (Schopenhauer)]] — FR §21 (comprehensive) + *Vision and Colours* §1 (first rehearsal): understanding creates the outer world from sensation; stereoscope; illusion vs error; animals have understanding | concept | epistemology, philosophy-of-mind
- [[Concepts/Physiological Theory of Colour (Schopenhauer)]] — Colour = qualitatively divided activity of the retina. Complements restore white. Polarity is successive and retinal. Goethe's data; Newton's wrong locus. Not Will. | concept | epistemology, philosophy-of-mind, philosophy-of-science
- [[Concepts/Doctrine of the Ideal and the Real (Schopenhauer)]] — Descartes’ cut: what in cognition is objective vs subjective. Kant completes it; Schopenhauer assigns the real to will. Fichte/Schelling/Hegel are sophists. | concept | epistemology, metaphysics
- [[Concepts/University Philosophy (Schopenhauer)]] — Philosophy for hire vs love of wisdom; Socrates vs sophists; Hegel as apotheosis of philistinism. Teach only logic + Thales-to-Kant. | concept | metaphilosophy, ethics
- [[Concepts/Transcendent Fatalism (Schopenhauer)]] — Demonstrable necessity of acts vs a life’s moral/inner necessity. Labeled metaphysical fantasy; will as secret impresario. *Amor fati* absent. | concept | metaphysics, ethics
- [[Concepts/Spirit-Seeing and Idealistic Explanation (Schopenhauer)]] — Animal magnetism treated as fact; no spirits; idealistic explanation via will beyond space/time. | concept | metaphysics, philosophy-of-mind
- [[Concepts/Eudaemonology as Accommodation (Schopenhauer)]] — Instructions to a happy existence written from the error the system has judged. Even the term is a euphemism. | concept | ethics
- [[Concepts/What One Is, Has, and Represents (Schopenhauer)]] — Three goods of a human lot. Personality outweighs property and the opinion of others. | concept | ethics
- [[Concepts/Compassion and Justice (Schopenhauer)]] — *Mitleid* is the only genuine moral incentive; *tat tvam asi*. Right is the negation of injury. | concept | ethics, political-philosophy
- [[Concepts/Indestructibility of Our True Nature (Schopenhauer)]] — After death you are what you were before birth. Intellect dies; will does not. Palingenesis, not metempsychosis. | concept | metaphysics, philosophy-of-mind
- [[Concepts/Vanity and Suffering of Existence (Schopenhauer)]] — Vanity is the form of existence; pain is positive; well-being negative. Attacks optimism without self-naming “pessimism.” | concept | ethics, metaphysics
- [[Concepts/Affirmation and Denial of the Will-to-Live (Schopenhauer)]] — Denial is *nolle*, not annihilation. Suicide is not denial. Generation is the world-knot. | concept | ethics, metaphysics
- [[Concepts/Religion as Allegory (Schopenhauer)]] — No true religion *sensu proprio*. True at best *sensu allegorico*. The real cut among religions is optimism vs pessimism. | concept | philosophy-of-religion, metaphysics
- [[Concepts/Thinking for Oneself (Schopenhauer)]] — We know only what we have thought out. Reading is thinking with someone else’s head. | concept | epistemology, metaphilosophy, ethics
- [[Concepts/On Women (Schopenhauer)]] — Ch. XXVII primary rant. Record it; do not sanitize; do not present it as vault fact. | concept | ethics
- [[Concepts/Theory of the Ludicrous (Schopenhauer)]] — Laughter is sudden incongruity of concept and percept. Only man laughs. *WWR* II Ch. VIII. | concept | aesthetics, epistemology
- [[Concepts/Man's Need for Metaphysics (Schopenhauer)]] — *Animal metaphysicum*. Philosophy *sensu stricto*; religion *sensu allegorico*. *WWR* II Ch. XVII. | concept | metaphysics, metaphilosophy, philosophy-of-religion
- [[Concepts/Primacy of the Will in Self-Consciousness (Schopenhauer)]] — Will is substance; intellect is tool. Nearest phenomenon, not quite naked. *WWR* II Chs. XVIII–XXII. | concept | metaphysics, philosophy-of-mind
- [[Concepts/Genius (Schopenhauer)]] — Surplus perceptive intellect beyond service of the will. Kinship with madness. *WWR* II Chs. XXIX–XXXII. | concept | aesthetics, philosophy-of-mind
- [[Concepts/History as Particular (Schopenhauer)]] — History is not a science. *Eadem, sed aliter.* *WWR* II Ch. XXXVIII. | concept | philosophy-of-history, epistemology
- [[Concepts/Metaphysics of Sexual Love (Schopenhauer)]] — Love is the species choosing the next generation. Individual is the dupe. *WWR* II Chs. XLII–XLIV. | concept | metaphysics, ethics

**Nietzsche and the Revaluation of Values (Nietzsche, 2026-06-04)**:
- [[Concepts/Will to Power (Nietzsche)]] - First published in *Zarathustra* (Part I table of a people; Part II Self-Surpassing: Life must ever surpass itself, not Will to Life). BGE later: most spiritual Will to Power as "creation of the world" / causa prima behind the will to truth. GS 1882 has only the feeling of power.
- [[Concepts/Perspectivism (Nietzsche)]] - All "truths," valuations, and claims are provisional perspectives ("frog perspectives" from some corner or "below"), often physiological or value-driven. No absolute antitheses of values or pure disinterested "Truth"; higher value may lie in what was called "evil" (pretense, will to delusion). "To recognize untruth as a condition of life."
- [[Concepts/Philosophy as Involuntary and Unconscious Autobiography (Nietzsche)]] - Every great philosophy is the "confession of its originator, and a species of involuntary and unconscious auto-biography"; the moral (or immoral) purpose is the "true vital germ." To understand abstruse claims, ask "What morality do they aim at?" Nothing impersonal in the philosopher; morality testifies to the order of his deepest impulses.
- [[Concepts/Beyond Good and Evil (Nietzsche)]] - To move beyond the traditional moral antitheses ("good/evil") and the dogmatist belief in value-antitheses (Plato's Good in Itself, Kant's categorical, etc.). "Recognize untruth as a condition of life" and operate with "dangerous 'Perhapses'" as prelude to revaluation by new "philosophers of the dangerous 'Perhaps'" and "free, very free spirits" (we still have the tension of the bow from the struggle against Platonism/Christianity).
- [[Concepts/Belief in Antitheses of Values (Nietzsche)]] - The deepest "prejudice of philosophers" (BGE aph. 2): the metaphysicians' unexamined faith that the highest-value things must have a separate, pure origin (Being, the Thing-in-itself, God) opposed to the low/"evil" — when perhaps the high and low are "insidiously related, knotted, and crocheted," even "essentially identical." A master lint heuristic (question the binary) and the root of the genealogical method.
- [[Concepts/Philosophers of the Dangerous "Perhaps" (Nietzsche)]] - New philosophers as commanders/legislators (§211), not the present free spirits (heralds, §44) and not *libres-penseurs*. Dare the "dangerous 'Perhaps'"; create values. See [[Concepts/Free Spirit (Nietzsche)]].
- [[Concepts/Soul as Social Structure (Nietzsche)]] - After soul-atomism (§12): mortal soul, multiplicity, social structure of instincts; will as command/obey; strong vs weak wills (§§16–21). Not a subsection of Will to Power.
- [[Concepts/Pathos of Distance and Order of Rank (Nietzsche)]] - Social looking-down that enables inner self-surmounting (§257); innate rank tested by reverence (§§263, 287). Condition of master morality; not Berlin bent-twig.

**Nietzsche Genealogy of Morals (1887)**:
- [[Concepts/Ressentiment (Nietzsche)]] — the "venomous eye" / creative brooding of the weak that inverts values, constructs the "evil enemy," and fuels the slave revolt in morals (Rome vs. Judaea) | concept | ethics
- [[Concepts/Master Morality and Slave Morality (Nietzsche)]] — **BGE §260 first** (typology; can live in one soul); *Genealogy* historicizes (ressentiment, priest, Rome vs Judaea). Master good/bad ≠ slave good/evil. | concept | ethics
- [[Concepts/Bad Conscience (Nietzsche)]] — internalization of instincts of freedom under peace/society ("illness" that is also "pregnancy" of soul); "ought" from "owe"; sovereign individual as one competent to promise | concept | ethics
- [[Concepts/Ascetic Ideal (Nietzsche)]] — *horror vacui*: will sooner will nothingness than not will; priest as physician who turns resentment inward; science is the newest form of the ideal, not its opposite; last unbelievers still believe in the truth | concept | ethics
- [[Concepts/Sovereign Individual (Nietzsche)]] — the autonomous "super-moral" being with proud conscience as memory of the will; the ripened fruit of the whole morality tree | concept | ethics

**Nietzsche Human, All Too Human (1878 / 1886 Preface)**:
- [[Concepts/Free Spirit (Nietzsche)]] — 1878 chemist (no eternal facts); 1886 Preface invented comrades / great liberation / *Rangordnung*; BGE Ch. II heralds, not legislators. Three voices; do not flatten.
- [[Concepts/Historical Philosophising and the Chemistry of Concepts (Nietzsche)]] — no antithesis except in exaggeration; no eternal facts / no absolute truths; magnificent results from despised ingredients | concept | epistemology, ethics
- [[Concepts/Two-fold Historical Origin of Good and Evil (Nietzsche)]] — 1878: ruling requital vs. subjugated hostility; custom vs. emancipation; guiltlessness. Not 1887 ressentiment | concept | ethics

**Nietzsche The Gay Science (1882 / 1887)**:
- [[Concepts/Death of God (Nietzsche)]] — GS sequence: shadows (§108), madman telling unbelievers they have come too early (§125); 1887 still pious toward truth. *Zarathustra*: saint has not heard; God dies of pity; ugliest man murders the witness; ass-festival relapse | concept | ethics, philosophy-of-religion
- [[Concepts/Eternal Recurrence (Nietzsche)]] — GS §341 demon's *if*. *Zarathustra*: dwarf's circle vs gateway Moment vs animals' hurdy-gurdy; nausea that the smallest man returns. Not Deleuze's selective ontology | concept | ethics, metaphysics
- [[Concepts/Intellectual Conscience (Nietzsche)]] — §2: most people live by unexamined valuations; desire for certainty separates higher from lower. §344 later suspects the same honesty | concept | epistemology, ethics
- [[Concepts/The Great Health (Nietzsche)]] — health acquired by being given up; philosophies as symptoms of bodies; Preface + §382 | concept | ethics, metaphilosophy

**Nietzsche Thus Spoke Zarathustra (1883–85)**:
- [[Concepts/The Superman (Nietzsche)]] — *Übermensch* (Common: Superman): meaning of the earth; man as rope/bridge; never yet been; not the higher man; not Kaufmann's overman or Deleuze's superfold | concept | ethics, metaphysics
- [[Concepts/The Last Man (Nietzsche)]] — Prologue §5: most contemptible; discovered happiness; blink; herd without shepherd; the market prefers him | concept | ethics, political-philosophy
- [[Concepts/Three Metamorphoses (Nietzsche)]] — camel (bear) → lion (holy Nay / I will) → child (holy Yea / create). Lion cannot yet create values | concept | ethics, metaphilosophy
- [[Concepts/Redemption of the It Was (Nietzsche)]] — "It was" → "Thus would I have it." Revenge = Will's antipathy to time. Hinge that makes return more than cosmology | concept | ethics, metaphysics
- [[Concepts/The Higher Man (Nietzsche)]] — Part IV cave-guests: bridges and failures, not the Superman; pity is the last sin; ass-festival; the sign (lion + doves) | concept | ethics

**Nietzsche On Truth and Lies (1873, unpublished)**:
- [[Concepts/Truth as Worn-Out Metaphor (Nietzsche)]] — movable host of forgotten metaphors; concept = equation of unequals; to be truthful is to lie with the herd. Not Kaufmann’s “mobile army” | concept | epistemology
- [[Concepts/Intuitive Man and Rational Man (Nietzsche)]] — metaphor-drive escapes into myth and art; stoical man as masterpiece of deception in misfortune | concept | aesthetics, epistemology

**Nietzsche The Birth of Tragedy (1872)**:
- [[Concepts/Apollinian and Dionysian (Nietzsche)]] — dream / intoxication; *principium individuationis* held vs shattered; Kaufmann *Apollinian*; two Dionysuses (1872 Oneness ≠ late over-fullness) | concept | aesthetics, metaphysics
- [[Concepts/Aesthetic Justification of Existence (Nietzsche)]] — only as aesthetic phenomenon is the world justified; answers Silenus, not Leibniz; man = dissonance become man | concept | aesthetics, metaphysics
- [[Concepts/Socratism and the Theoretical Man (Nietzsche)]] — tragedy died by suicide; beauty = intelligible; theoretical optimist; 1872 type, not Twilight physiology | concept | aesthetics, epistemology
- [[Concepts/Tragedy from the Spirit of Music (Nietzsche)]] — chorus first; music as copy of the Will; opera vs music-drama; hoped German rebirth via Wagner | concept | aesthetics, cultural-criticism

**Nietzsche The Case of Wagner (1888)**:
- [[Concepts/Decadence (Nietzsche)]] — master problem of which good and evil is a subdivision; style-formula: life no longer animates the whole; Nietzsche includes himself | concept | aesthetics, ethics
- [[Concepts/Aesthetic as Applied Physiology (Nietzsche)]] — NCW: objections are physiological; aesthetic formulae are clothing; unending melody overthrows dance/measure | concept | aesthetics
- [[Concepts/Wagner as Cagliostro of Modernity (Nietzsche)]] — Wagner = modernity concentrated; philosopher must first be a Wagnerite; *Parsifal* as masterpiece of seduction | concept | aesthetics, ethics
- [[Concepts/Histrionics and Theatrocracy (Nietzsche)]] — musician becoming actor; three requisitions; Bayreuth as *plébiscite* against good taste | concept | aesthetics, ethics

**Nietzsche Twilight of the Idols (1888)**:
- [[Concepts/How the True World Became a Fable (Nietzsche)]] — six-stage history of an error (Plato → Christian → Königsberg → positivism → free spirits → noon). Stage 6 abolishes the apparent world with the true one. *INCIPIT ZARATHUSTRA* | concept | metaphysics, epistemology
- [[Concepts/The Four Great Errors (Nietzsche)]] — confusing cause and effect; false causality (will as cause is a fable); imaginary causes; free will as the foulest theological fiction. Not Humean compatibilism | concept | epistemology, ethics
- [[Concepts/Morality as Anti-Nature (Nietzsche)]] — church castratism vs spiritualization of passion; no moral facts; improvers tame or breed; both means immoral | concept | ethics

**Nietzsche The Antichrist (1888)**:
- [[Concepts/Formula of Values (Nietzsche)]] — A §2: good = power / will to power / feeling of power; evil = weakness; happiness = power increases. First “charity”: the botched shall perish. Not the notebooks and not *Zarathustra*’s self-surpassing | concept | ethics
- [[Concepts/Pity as Practice of Nihilism (Nietzsche)]] — A §7: pity is the *technic* of nihilism (Schopenhauer right as description). Not *Zarathustra*’s God-dies-of-pity scene | concept | ethics
- [[Concepts/Psychology of the Redeemer (Nietzsche)]] — A §§28–35: not Renan’s hero or genius; resist not evil; kingdom as state of the heart; Dostoevsky named. Not a Jesus thinker page | concept | ethics, philosophy-of-religion
- [[Concepts/Paul as Falsifier of the Evangel (Nietzsche)]] — A §§36–47: only one Christian, and he died on the cross; Paul the dysangelist; *deus, qualem Paulus creavit, dei negatio* | concept | ethics, philosophy-of-religion

**Nietzsche The Will to Power notebooks (Kaufmann excerpt)**:
- [[Concepts/European Nihilism (Nietzsche)]] — highest values devaluate themselves; Christian-moral root; active vs passive; complete vs incomplete; n.55 recurrence as extreme nihilism. Not a published book | concept | ethics, metaphysics
- [[Concepts/Will to Power as Knowledge (Nietzsche)]] — knowledge as tool of power; n.481 no facts only interpretations; truth created; n.617 impose being on becoming. Not n.1067 | concept | epistemology, metaphysics

**American Founding (Publius / The Federalist Papers, 2026-06-04)**:
- [[Concepts/Faction (Federalist 10)]] — number of citizens united by passion/interest adverse to others' rights or aggregate community interests; causes in human nature; remedy via extended republic (representation + multiplicity of interests/sects) | concept | political-philosophy
- [[Concepts/Checks and Balances (Federalist 51)]] — "ambition must be made to counteract ambition"; connect personal motives of office-holders to constitutional rights of the department via mutual relations | concept | political-philosophy
- [[Concepts/Federalism and Compound Republic (Federalist 51)]] — double security: power first divided between federal/state governments, then each subdivided among departments; governments control each other while self-controlled | concept | political-philosophy
- [[Concepts/Independent Judiciary and Judicial Review (Federalist 78)]] — judiciary weakest (judgment not will/force); good-behavior tenure + power to void legislative acts contrary to Constitution's "manifest tenor" as bulwark | concept | political-philosophy

**Early Modern / Empiricism & Scepticism (Hume, 2026-06-04)**:
- [[Concepts/Impressions and Ideas - Copy Principle (Hume)]] - Perceptions divide into impressions (lively) and ideas (faint copies), differing in force and vivacity; every simple idea copies a prior simple impression. Meaning-test: "from what impression is that supposed idea derived?" (Hume's microscope; ancestor of verificationism). The missing-shade-of-blue counterexample. Anti-innatist engine; symbol-grounding parallels.
- [[Concepts/Hume's Fork (Relations of Ideas vs Matters of Fact)]] - Exhaustive division of inquiry: relations of ideas (a priori, demonstrative, contrary self-contradictory — math) vs. matters of fact (contingent, contrary always conceivable; known only by experience). Conceivability proves possibility, never existence. Detonates rationalist metaphysics; sets up induction; yields the "flames" meaning-filter. Ancestor of analytic/synthetic.
- [[Concepts/Problem of Induction (Hume)]] - All factual inference presupposes the future will resemble the past, a Uniformity Principle provable neither by demonstration (no contradiction in change) nor by experience (circular). So induction is not founded on reason. The defining problem of the epistemology of science; the foundational caveat on ML generalization / distribution shift. Answered (Sec. V) by custom, not reason.
- [[Concepts/Necessary Connexion and the Two Definitions of Cause (Hume)]] - No impression of power in objects ("conjoined, but never connected"); the idea of necessity copies the mind's customary transition after constant conjunction. Two definitions of cause: regularity (with counterfactual gloss) + psychological. Rejects occasionalism (reopens the Ghazali/Ibn Rushd debate empirically). The "New Hume" (regularity vs. sceptical realism) crux.
- [[Concepts/Custom and Belief (Hume)]] - The "sceptical solution": custom (habit) is "the great guide of human life"; belief is not a separate idea but a more vivid, involuntary manner of conceiving (vivacity) produced by a present impression. "Reasoning is a species of instinct." Naturalized epistemology; strong predictive-processing / connectionist resonance (confidence-as-vivacity, inference-as-trained-transition).
- [[Concepts/Compatibilism - Liberty and Necessity (Hume)]] - The dispute is verbal. Necessity = constant conjunction + inference (and it holds of human action — the uniformity of human nature; natural and moral evidence form one chain). Liberty = unconstrained action per the will (not absence of cause = chance). Both essential to morality. The classic compatibilist position; contrasts Spinoza's harder necessitarianism, Stoic fate, Epicurean swerve.
- [[Concepts/Of Miracles (Hume)]] - "A wise man proportions his belief to the evidence." A miracle violates a law of nature backed by uniform experience; no testimony suffices unless its falsehood would be more miraculous than the event — and (Part II) none ever has (witnesses, love of wonder, barbarous provenance, mutual cancellation of rival religions). Proto-Bayesian testimony calculus; high practical value for source reliability and risk judgment.
- [[Concepts/Mitigated Scepticism (Hume)]] - The vault's third path on scepticism: Pyrrhonism is powerless in life ("Nature is always too strong for principle"); its useful residue is durable modesty + limitation of inquiry to quantity/number (demonstration) and matters of fact (experience). "Commit it then to the flames." Includes Sec. XI's proportionality critique of the design argument. The ideal maintainer temperament (calibrated, scoped, anti-dogmatic).

**American Pragmatism (James, 2026-06-04 / *Varieties* 2026-08-12)**:
- [[Concepts/Medical Materialism and the Fruits Test (James)]] - Origins do not settle value. "By their fruits ye shall know them, not by their roots." Immediate luminousness, philosophical reasonableness, moral helpfulness. | concept | philosophy-of-religion, psychology
- [[Concepts/Healthy-Mindedness and the Sick Soul (James)]] - Once-born vs twice-born (Newman). Systematic healthy-mindedness excludes evil; the sick soul hits a misery-threshold and cries "Help! help!" Completest religions are religions of deliverance. Not the 1907 tender/tough table. | concept | philosophy-of-religion, psychology
- [[Concepts/Conversion and the Subliminal Self (James)]] - Divided self unified; suddenness is idiosyncrasy; subliminal (Myers) is the door, not a substitute deity. Saintliness = ripe fruits. | concept | philosophy-of-religion, psychology
- [[Concepts/Mystical States - Four Marks (James)]] - Ineffability + noetic quality suffice; transiency and passivity usual. Authoritative for the mystic, bind no outsider, break exclusive rational authority. Classifier, not henosis. | concept | philosophy-of-religion, psychology
- [[Concepts/Over-Beliefs and Piecemeal Supernaturalism (James)]] - Nucleus: uneasiness + deliverance via a MORE. Hither side = subconscious; farther side = a larger power that produces real effects and need not be infinite or unique. | concept | philosophy-of-religion, metaphysics
- [[Concepts/Pragmatic Method (James)]] - Settle disputes / fix meaning by tracing each alternative's concrete practical consequences ("no difference anywhere that doesn't make a difference elsewhere"). Forward-looking, deflationary toward verbal disputes; theories as instruments, not "solving names." Peirce's principle generalized; the squirrel anecdote. A sharp lint heuristic. Already applied in *Varieties* XVIII to God's attributes.
- [[Concepts/Pragmatic Theory of Truth (James)]] - Truth is not static copying but a *verification-process*: "truth *happens* to an idea… is made true by events." Agreement = fruitful *leading*; truth lives "on a credit system"; "the true is only the expedient in the way of our thinking"; truth is *in rebus* (like health/wealth/strength), grows out of prior truths. Rebuts the "whatever is pleasant" charge. The vault's third path on truth.
- [[Concepts/Tender-Minded and Tough-Minded (James)]] - Philosophy's history as a clash of temperaments: tender-minded (rationalist/idealist/religious/monist/dogmatic) vs. tough-minded (empiricist/materialist/sceptical/pluralist). "The potentest of all our premises is never mentioned." Pragmatism as the mediator that lets a fact-lover keep a religion. Kin to Nietzsche's "philosophy as autobiography."
- [[Concepts/The One and the Many (James)]] - "The most central of all philosophic problems," handled pragmatically: oneness and manyness "absolutely coordinate," true in as many namable respects as fail. Absolute monism (Vedanta) rests on mystical, not intellectual, grounds; pragmatism sides with pluralism and may replace the "Absolute" with the "Ultimate" (oneness at the *end*). Mirrors the vault's link-graph architecture.
- [[Concepts/Meliorism (James)]] - Between optimism (salvation inevitable) and pessimism (impossible): salvation as a *grounded possibility* whose probability rises as actual conditions (incl. our own acts) accumulate. "Possible chicken means actual egg." The world's-author wager; pluralistic theism (God as *primus inter pares*). The vault's native theology of the unfinished, compounding wiki.
- [[Concepts/Humanism - Reality as Malleable (Schiller and James)]] - Schiller's humanism: "to an unascertainable extent our truths are man-made products"; reality is "malleable," "we carve the statue." "The *that* is reality's own; the *what* depends on the *which*… on us" (constellations, 27, the audience). Guarded against caprice by reality's "resisting" parts. Constructivist edge of pragmatism; mirror for the vault's carving of structure from raw sources (with provenance).

**20th-Century Analytic Philosophy (Russell & Wittgenstein, 2026-06-11 Ingest)**:
- [[Concepts/Philosophy as the No Man's Land between Theology and Science (Russell)]] - Russell's definition: philosophy is reasoned speculation on questions where "definite knowledge has so far been unascertainable" — between dogmatic theology and settled science. Its chief gift: "to teach how to live without certainty, and yet without being paralyzed by hesitation." A map for filing claims (science / dogma / philosophy) and the honest temperament of the Open-Questions sections.
- [[Concepts/Social Cohesion vs. Individual Liberty (Russell)]] - The master political dialectic from 600 BC to now: disciplinarians (tighten bonds; dogma, "nobility," irrationalist, anti-science) vs. libertarians (relax them; scientific, utilitarian, rationalist). Civilizations oscillate between ossification and dissolution; **liberalism** (Locke) is the attempt to escape — "stability without more restraints than necessary." Pairs directly with the Federalist cluster.
- [[Concepts/Philosophy of Logical Analysis (Russell)]] - Russell's own school and the book's conclusion: make logical analysis the main business of philosophy; renounce any "higher" knowing; proceed piecemeal toward "successive approximations — improvement, not rejection." Science as tentative/probabilistic authority; **scientific truthfulness** as an ethical virtue; **Occam's razor**; the **"cosmic impiety"** warning — the canonical realist critique of [[Concepts/Pragmatic Theory of Truth (James)]].
- [[Concepts/Sense-Data vs. Physical Objects (Russell)]] - *Problems* chs. I–IV: the appearance/reality distinction; **sense-data** (immediately known) vs. the **physical object** (inferred); structural realism (we know matter's *relations*, not its intrinsic nature); realism defended by *simplicity* + instinctive belief; idealism (Berkeley's *esse est percipi*) refuted via the act/object equivocation.
- [[Concepts/Knowledge by Acquaintance and Knowledge by Description (Russell)]] - *Problems* ch. V: direct **acquaintance** (sense-data, memory, introspection, the Self, universals) vs. **description** ("the so-and-so"). "Every proposition we can understand must be composed wholly of constituents with which we are acquainted." Description lets us pass beyond private experience. Mirrors the vault's provenance/grounding rule.
- [[Concepts/Correspondence Theory of Truth (Russell)]] - *Problems* ch. XII: truth = correspondence of belief to **fact**; "minds do not *create* truth or falsehood." Coherence rejected as the *definition* of truth (more than one coherent system; presupposes logic) but kept as a *test*. The realist counter-pole to [[Concepts/Pragmatic Theory of Truth (James)]]; anti-pragmatism already explicit in 1912.
- [[Concepts/The Value of Philosophy (Russell)]] - *Problems* ch. XV: philosophy "studied not for the sake of definite answers… but rather for the sake of the questions themselves"; enlargement of the Self via union with the **not-Self**; the **free intellect**; explicit rejection of "Man is the measure… truth is man-made." Charter for the Open-Questions discipline.
- [[Concepts/Picture Theory of Language (Wittgenstein)]] — The view that language represents reality by mirroring its logical structure; a proposition is a logical picture of facts, and any statement that does not map to possible facts in the world is meaningless. | concept | philosophy-of-language, logic
- [[Concepts/Language-Games (Wittgenstein)]] — Language is not one mirror of reality but countless rule-governed activities ("language-games") woven into action; "to imagine a language means to imagine a form of life" (§19). The later Wittgenstein's replacement for the picture theory. | concept | philosophy-of-language, philosophy-of-mind
- [[Concepts/Meaning as Use (Wittgenstein)]] — "The meaning of a word is its use in the language" (§43); meaning is neither a named object nor an inner idea — "a sign is dead; in use it is alive" (§432). | concept | philosophy-of-language
- [[Concepts/Critique of Ostensive Definition (Wittgenstein)]] — Pointing-and-naming cannot found meaning, since "an ostensive definition can be variously interpreted in every case" (§28); it works only where a place in the language is already prepared. Opens the *Investigations* against Augustine. | concept | philosophy-of-language
- [[Concepts/Family Resemblance (Wittgenstein)]] — General terms (e.g. "game") share no single essence but a web of overlapping, criss-crossing similarities (§§65–67). "Don't think, but look!" Directly denies Plato's Forms. | concept | philosophy-of-language, logic
- [[Concepts/Rule-Following (Wittgenstein)]] — The §201 paradox (every action can be made to accord with a rule) shows that following a rule is "a practice," not the grasp of an interpretation; "I obey the rule blindly" (§219); justification reaches bedrock — "this is simply what I do" (§217). | concept | philosophy-of-language, epistemology
- [[Concepts/Private Language Argument (Wittgenstein)]] — A language referring only to private inner sensations is incoherent: it has no criterion of correctness (§258), and the "beetle in a box" cancels out (§293). A frontal attack on the Cartesian/Lockean inner theatre. | concept | philosophy-of-mind, epistemology
- [[Concepts/Forms of Life (Wittgenstein)]] — Justification ends not in theory but in shared human practice and natural reaction: "What has to be accepted, the given, is — so one could say — forms of life" (II §xi); agreement "in judgments," not opinions (§242). | concept | epistemology, philosophy-of-language
- [[Concepts/Aspect Seeing (Wittgenstein)]] — The duck-rabbit: "I see that it has not changed; and yet I see it differently." Seeing-as is "half visual experience, half thought," presupposing mastery of a technique — undercutting any pure sensory given. | concept | philosophy-of-mind, philosophy-of-perception
- [[Concepts/Philosophy as Therapy (Wittgenstein)]] — Philosophical problems are confusions, not discoveries: "Philosophy is a battle against the bewitchment of our intelligence by means of language" (§109); its aim is "to shew the fly the way out of the fly-bottle" (§309); it "leaves everything as it is" (§124). | concept | metaphilosophy, philosophy-of-language

**Strategy & Statecraft (Sun Tzu, 2026-06-04)**:
- [[Concepts/Calculation and the Five Factors (Sun Tzu)]] - War decided *before* it's fought by calculating five factors (Moral Law/*tao*, Heaven, Earth, Commander, Method) + seven considerations "in the temple"; "many calculations lead to victory." Plan-before-act discipline; a pre-mortem checklist. Not rigid — "modify your plans as circumstances are favorable."
- [[Concepts/Winning Without Fighting (Sun Tzu)]] - "Supreme excellence consists in breaking the enemy's resistance without fighting"; take things "whole and intact"; the hierarchy (balk plans > prevent junction > attack army > besiege cities = worst). The leveraged, value-preserving victory; resolve at the root, not by attrition.
- [[Concepts/Know the Enemy and Know Yourself (Sun Tzu)]] - "If you know the enemy and know yourself, you need not fear a hundred battles." Paired self- and adversary-knowledge as the precondition of secure victory; self-deception is the root of defeat. Converges with the vault's "know thyself" and orient-first disciplines.
- [[Concepts/All Warfare Is Based on Deception (Sun Tzu)]] - Control the enemy's *perception* (seem far when near, weak when strong; bait, provoke, surprise). A pre-modern theory of signaling and information asymmetry; outward discretion over inward honesty. Ethics tension with Kant flagged; legitimate as positioning/discretion in negotiation.
- [[Concepts/Shih - Momentum, Formlessness, and Striking Weakness (Sun Tzu)]] - The dynamic core: combine direct (*cheng*)/indirect (*qi*); generate momentum (*shih*); be formless like water — "avoid what is strong and strike at what is weak"; "no constant tactics"; wind/forest/fire/mountain. Adaptive policy over fixed plan; concentrate on the weak point.
- [[Concepts/Foreknowledge and the Use of Spies (Sun Tzu)]] - Foreknowledge ("cannot be obtained inductively from experience… only from other men") via the five classes of spies (local, inward, **converted**, doomed, surviving) — the cheap, decisive investment. Intelligence ≈ context-gathering; converted spy ≈ counter-intelligence; epistemology twist convergent with Hume/Russell on particulars.

**Craft, Leadership & Mastery (from 2026 MasterClass batch + CPCU)**:
- [[Concepts/Creative Leadership]] - Team building, direct feedback, conviction + delegation, hyper-organized personal routines (Wintour); values/profits not enemies + fiduciary/moral duty to community (Schultz); consumer-pain origin + leverage + self-made path (Blakely).
- [[Concepts/Expertise & Deliberate Practice]] - Study of masters, practice positions/workbooks, human judgment + creativity that complements calculation/AI (Kasparov chess); precision craft and repetition (culinary/BBQ classes, Keller fundamentals, Franklin technique).
- [[Concepts/Persuasion & Sales Science]] - Ambiverts as effective persuaders, loss aversion, information asymmetry/parity, confirmation bias, one-word equity/USP, primacy effect, framing (Daniel Pink Sales and Persuasion). Directly augments the agribusiness sales playbook.
- [[Concepts/Entrepreneurial Leap & Consumer-Driven Innovation]] - Start from personal frustration + small capital (Blakely Spanx prototype from cut pantyhose); "jump in" despite self-doubt and opportunity cost; regret of inaction is expensive (Schultz).
- [[Concepts/Values-Driven Leadership & Craft]] - Employee dignity and moral duty as core value (Schultz from father's experience); craft as sensory/precise/generous practice transferable across domains (BBQ notes + cooking classes).

*(Additional concepts referenced from Claude Guide (Artifacts, Projects, Skills, Claude Code) and Grok Tutorial (Grok Modes, Agent Roles e.g. Harper/Benjamin/Lucas, Custom Instructions/Memory, Imagine/Video, Tasks, Structured Outputs) will be expanded into dedicated pages on future passes or queries. More insurance concepts (Resume & Personal Brand, Networking Strategy, Retention & Cross-Sell, Commercial Property Forms, Business Income Coverage, Risk Assessment for Commercial Clients) to follow as CPCU study deepens. Future classical: Demiurge/Teleology, Receptacle, Platonic cosmology, Phronēsis, Topoi, Stoic daily practice applications, full Enneads treatises (e.g. V.1 Three Hypostases), more on Sextus, Aquinas (natural law details, grace/virtues, Summa II-II specific virtues). MasterClass themes (storytelling as influence, culinary precision as metaphor, morning routines of high performers, human creativity vs AI agents) available for expansion. New Stoic/Plato notes + Epicurus + Plotinus + Pyrrhonism + Aquinas + Augustine add Dichotomy of Control, teachability of virtue, pleasure vs intellect as good, ataraxia (now three routes), swerve, live unnoticed, the One, emanation, return of the soul, epoché, equipollence/isosthenia, the 10/5 Modes, skeptical slogans, "chronicler" reporting, beatific vision/last end, natural law, restless heart/rest in God, memory as interior path to self-knowledge/God, time as distentio animi (co-temporal with creation), grace transforming the divided will, evil as privation of good, Neo-Platonism as bridge to Christian resolution, etc.)*

**Philosophy of Science / Critical Rationalism (Popper, 2026-06-04)** — *catalog block added 2026-06-04 (4 concepts from LSD + 4 from C&R); fixes prior index drift*:
- [[Concepts/Falsifiability as Criterion of Demarcation (Popper)]] - A system is empirical/scientific iff it is **falsifiable** (its logical form permits contradiction by possible "basic statements"). Not verifiability (impossible for universal laws). The more a theory prohibits, the more it says. Now includes the Marx/Freud/Adler-vs-Einstein illustration + seven conclusions + "conventionalist twist" from *C&R*. | concept | epistemology, philosophy-of-science
- [[Concepts/Corroboration (Popper)]] - A theory "proves its mettle" / is **corroborated** by surviving severe attempted refutations — a qualitative *report*, not probability or verification. Tracks (does not prove) [[Concepts/Verisimilitude (Truthlikeness) (Popper)|verisimilitude]]; tied to the three requirements for growth. | concept | epistemology, philosophy-of-science
- [[Concepts/Basic Statements and the Empirical Basis (Popper)]] - Singular falsifying statements are not incorrigible reports of "the given" but intersubjectively testable hypotheses accepted by **decision**; objectivity = intersubjective testability; testability goes on ad infinitum. | concept | epistemology, philosophy-of-science
- [[Concepts/Problem of Demarcation (Kant and Popper)]] - "Kant's problem" — the line between empirical science and metaphysics/logic. Solved by falsifiability (a *proposal*, not a discovery). Includes the Carnap critique (demarcation ≠ a criterion of *meaning*) and "false yet irrefutable" (pure existential statements; determinism vs. indeterminism). | concept | epistemology, philosophy-of-science
- [[Concepts/No Authoritative Sources of Knowledge (Popper)]] - Neither observation nor reason is an authoritative *source*; both empiricism and rationalism share the false "truth is manifest" doctrine (→ the conspiracy theory of ignorance). The real question: "How can we detect and eliminate error?" | concept | epistemology, philosophy-of-science
- [[Concepts/Three Views Concerning Human Knowledge (Popper)]] - Against **essentialism** (ultimate explanation by essences) and **instrumentalism** (theories as mere tools), Popper defends **conjecturalism/scientific realism**: theories are fallible conjectures about reality, true or false, capable of predicting genuinely new kinds of events. | concept | epistemology, philosophy-of-science, metaphysics
- [[Concepts/Verisimilitude (Truthlikeness) (Popper)]] - Nearness to truth = **truth-content minus falsity-content** (Tarski's objective truth as regulative idea). What science values is content/truthlikeness, the *opposite* of probability; a bold, refuted theory can be nearer the truth. (Note: the formal measure was shown flawed in 1974; the intuition survives.) | concept | epistemology, philosophy-of-science
- [[Concepts/Critique of Historicism and Dialectic (Popper)]] - **Prophecy vs. conditional prediction**: long-term historical prophecy is baseless (society is not isolated/stationary/recurrent); critique of Hegelian/Marxist **dialectic** (illegitimate toleration of contradiction); the open society + **piecemeal engineering** vs. utopian violence. The vault's first primary-source Popperian politics. | concept | philosophy-of-science, political-philosophy

**History & Sociology of Science (Kuhn, 2026-06-04)**:
- [[Concepts/Paradigm (Kuhn)]] - A concrete scientific achievement (exemplar) that gives a community its model problems, methods, and standards and so constitutes it as a community; later split into **disciplinary matrix** (broad) and **exemplar** (narrow). Exemplars over explicit rules ("priority of paradigms"). | concept | epistemology, philosophy-of-science, sociology-of-science
- [[Concepts/Normal Science and Puzzle-Solving (Kuhn)]] - Most science is conservative **puzzle-solving** within a paradigm that it *presupposes* (not tests); "how little [its problems] aim to produce major novelties." Cumulative and efficient — which is what eventually exposes the paradigm-breaking anomaly. | concept | epistemology, philosophy-of-science
- [[Concepts/Anomaly and Crisis (Kuhn)]] - Discovery begins when something violates paradigm expectations; anomalies are **not bare refutations** (must be recognized; always present) and provoke **crisis** only when central and intractable. No paradigm abandoned without a successor. | concept | epistemology, philosophy-of-science
- [[Concepts/Scientific Revolutions and Paradigm Shifts (Kuhn)]] - Non-cumulative **paradigm shift** resolving a crisis — a gestalt-switch/**conversion**, "not the sort of battle resolved by proofs." Afterward scientists "work in a different world"; textbooks hide the discontinuity; progress is "**evolution from**, not toward" a goal. | concept | epistemology, philosophy-of-science, history-of-science
- [[Concepts/Incommensurability (Kuhn)]] - Across a revolution, standards, problems, and term-meanings shift (mass, space, earth), so rival paradigms can't be strictly compared and choice isn't logic-forced — yet Kuhn denies this means irrationality/relativism (shared values: accuracy, consistency, scope, simplicity, fruitfulness). | concept | epistemology, philosophy-of-science, philosophy-of-language
- [[Concepts/Entity Realism vs Theory Realism (Hacking)]] - Hacking's key distinction: realism about entities (the objects really exist and have causal powers we use) is independent of realism about theories (theories aim at/literally describe truth).
