Experiment grounds the former even when the latter is troubled. | concept | epistemology, philosophy-of-science, metaphysics
- [[Concepts/If You Can Spray Them Then They Are Real (Hacking)]] - Hacking's pragmatic criterion: we count as real the entities (e.g. electrons) we can reliably use as tools to intervene and affect other things ("spray positrons/electrons to charge the niobium ball"). Use, not full theoretical belief, commits the experimenter to entity realism. | concept | epistemology, philosophy-of-science
- [[Concepts/Creation of Phenomena (Hacking)]] - Many experiments create stable, repeatable phenomena (by refining apparatus) that did not exist in pure form in nature beforehand; "repeating" is usually improving until the effect is on-demand. Supports entity realism via use. | concept | epistemology, philosophy-of-science, history-of-science
- [[Concepts/Experiment Has a Life of Its Own (Hacking)]] - Theory and experiment have no fixed priority or relation; experiment, instrumentation, and technology often develop substantial independence. "Which comes first?" has no single answer. The experimenter must often be realist about entities used as tools even if anti-realist about theories. | concept | epistemology, philosophy-of-science
- [[Concepts/Observation as a Skill (Hacking)]] - Observation is trained skill, not bare given or always strongly theory-laden. Pre-theoretical observations exist; we observe via instruments; convergence across independent physical systems (different microscopes) underwrites belief. | concept | epistemology, philosophy-of-science

**Logical Positivism (Ayer, 2026-06-04)**:
- [[Concepts/Verification Principle (Ayer)]] - A statement is literally meaningful iff it is analytic or empirically verifiable (some possible sense-experience is relevant to its truth). All else is literally senseless (metaphysical pseudo-proposition). Weak form; 1946 directly/indirectly verifiable revision. | concept | epistemology, philosophy-of-language
- [[Concepts/Metaphysics as Meaningless (Ayer)]] - Metaphysical sentences express neither tautologies nor empirical hypotheses; they are literally senseless, not false. Sources: linguistic/grammatical confusions (substance, Being, subsistent entities). Condemns transcendent theology, the soul, rationalist metaphysics. | concept | epistemology, philosophy-of-language, metaphysics
- [[Concepts/Analytic Propositions as Tautologies (Ayer)]] - All a priori truths (logic, mathematics, geometry) are analytic — they record linguistic conventions and say nothing about the world. Defeats Kant's synthetic a priori and Mill's inductivist account. Tautologies can still surprise us through computational complexity. | concept | epistemology, philosophy-of-language, logic
- [[Concepts/Emotive Theory of Ethics (Ayer)]] - Normative ethical (and aesthetic) symbols are pseudo-concepts; they express feeling, not propositions. Moral judgments are neither true nor false. Moral argument is always really about facts, not values. Distinct from subjectivism (which still asserts something). 1923 ancestor: [[Concepts/Symbolic and Emotive Functions of Language (Ogden-Richards)]]. | concept | ethics, philosophy-of-language
- [[Concepts/Triangle of Reference (Ogden-Richards)]] - In any statement: Symbol, Thought/Reference, Referent. The base is empty: words do not stand for things except through interpretation. The diagram behind later fights over how symbols hook the world. | concept | philosophy-of-language, epistemology
- [[Concepts/Word Magic (Ogden-Richards)]] - Treating the name as a power, a soul, or a direct stand-in for the thing — filling in the triangle's base. From *nomina* as *numina* to Platonic Ideas as name-souls to Russell's World of Being. | concept | philosophy-of-language, metaphysics
- [[Concepts/Contextual Theory of Reference (Ogden-Richards)]] - A thought is "of" A when it is similar to thoughts caused by A; cause = recurrence of a context. Truth is whether the external context completes. Relocates correspondence. | concept | philosophy-of-language, epistemology
- [[Concepts/Symbolic and Emotive Functions of Language (Ogden-Richards)]] - Symbolic use = statement (truth-apt); emotive use = express or excite attitudes. Usually together, distinct in principle. Claim-to-truth test. Ayer's ancestor. | concept | philosophy-of-language, aesthetics, ethics
- [[Concepts/Canons of Symbolism (Ogden-Richards)]] - Six regulative postulates of prose (singularity, definition, expansion, actuality, compatibility, individuality). Logic as systematization of symbols. Technique of definition; phonetic / hypostatic / utraquistic subterfuges. | concept | philosophy-of-language, logic
- [[Concepts/Context of Situation and Phatic Communion (Malinowski)]] - Meaning lives in the situation speech is bound up with; phatic communion creates ties by exchanging words whose meanings barely matter. Language as a mode of action. | concept | philosophy-of-language, anthropology

**Analytic Philosophy & Post-Positivism (Quine, 2026-06-04)**:
- [[Concepts/Analytic-Synthetic Distinction (Quine)]] - The supposed fundamental cleavage between truths grounded in meanings independently of fact (analytic) and truths grounded in fact (synthetic). Quine argues it cannot be drawn sharply without circular appeal to synonymy or analyticity itself; the positivists' sharpened linguistic version (plus verification) fails internally. Direct attack on the ancestor [[Concepts/Hume's Fork (Relations of Ideas vs Matters of Fact)]]. | concept | epistemology, philosophy-of-language, philosophy-of-science
- [[Concepts/Reductionism and Verification Theory of Meaning (Quine)]] - Each meaningful statement is equivalent to a logical construct on immediate-experience terms (or has unique confirming sensory events). The verification theory (meaning = confirmation method) was meant to ground synonymy independently of analyticity. Quine shows radical and attenuated forms both fail (even Carnap's *Aufbau* leaves key connectives undefined; no isolated empirical content for statements). | concept | epistemology, philosophy-of-science, philosophy-of-language
- [[Concepts/Confirmation Holism (Duhem-Quine Thesis)]] - Statements about the external world face experience not individually but as a corporate body (the web of belief). The unit of empirical significance is the whole of science. Latitude in revision; no statement immune, none beyond pragmatic readjustment. Physical objects etc. as cultural posits. Underdetermination of theory by data. | concept | epistemology, philosophy-of-science
- [[Concepts/Naturalized Epistemology and the Web of Belief (Quine)]] - No first philosophy outside science that justifies or reconstructs it. Epistemology becomes a chapter of natural science (psychology, etc.). The conceptual scheme of science (including its posits and ontology) is a pragmatic tool for predicting experience; ontological questions are on a par with scientific hypotheses. | concept | epistemology, philosophy-of-science, metaphysics

**Epistemological Anarchism (Feyerabend, 2026-06-04)**:
- [[Concepts/Epistemological Anarchism (Feyerabend)]] - Science has no fixed method and needs none; every universal rule (incl. Popperian falsificationism) has been productively broken. "The only principle that does not inhibit progress is: anything goes" — an ironic *reductio*, not a positive rule (and not relativism or licence for sloppiness). | concept | epistemology, philosophy-of-science
- [[Concepts/Counterinduction (Feyerabend)]] - Advance/develop hypotheses that contradict well-confirmed theories *and* established facts; rival, refuted theories are often the only way to expose the theory-laden assumptions hidden in "the facts." An inversion of inductivism and a radicalization of Popper's bold conjectures. | concept | epistemology, philosophy-of-science
- [[Concepts/Natural Interpretations and the Galileo Case (Feyerabend)]] - "Facts" are constituted by theory-laden "natural interpretations" built into perception/language. Galileo advanced Copernicus by *replacing* them (relativity of motion; circular inertia), disguised as "reminiscence," plus an untrustworthy telescope and rhetoric — violating every empiricist rule. The vault's strongest theory-ladenness case. | concept | epistemology, philosophy-of-science, history-of-science
- [[Concepts/Proliferation and Tenacity (Feyerabend)]] - Theoretical pluralism: invent rival theories (proliferation) *and* hold promising ones through anomalies (tenacity); both rational. Uniformity impairs criticism and the free individual. Drawn from Mill's *On Liberty* and Bohr's practice. | concept | epistemology, philosophy-of-science
- [[Concepts/Separation of Science and State (Feyerabend)]] - Science is one tradition among many, not the one road to truth; "Reason" is not tradition-independent. So in a free society science should be separated from the state (as churches are), under democratic control, taught "as one view among many." | concept | philosophy-of-science, political-philosophy

**Modern Political & Ethical Philosophy (Hobbes & Rousseau & Mill & Marx, 2026-06-11 Ingest)**:
- [[Concepts/State of Nature (Hobbes)]] — Human life before government, characterized by constant competition, fear, and conflict, where life is "solitary, poor, nasty, brutish, and short." | concept | political-philosophy, ethics
- [[Concepts/Social Contract (Hobbes)]] — The rational agreement by which self-interested individuals alienate all their rights (except self-defense) to an absolute sovereign to secure peace and physical protection. | concept | political-philosophy, ethics
- [[Concepts/State of Nature (Rousseau)]] — The hypothetical pre-social state of humanity, characterized by natural freedom, simplicity, self-love (amour de soi), and natural pity, in contrast to Hobbes' warlike view. | concept | political-philosophy, ethics
- [[Concepts/General Will (Rousseau)]] — The collective, unified will of the citizenry directed toward the common good and public interest, forming the basis of popular sovereignty and legitimate law. | concept | political-philosophy, ethics
- [[Concepts/Harm Principle (Mill)]] — The political principle that the only purpose for which power can be rightfully exercised over any individual against his will is to prevent harm to others. | concept | political-philosophy, ethics
- [[Concepts/Negative and Positive Liberty (Berlin)]] — 1958 pairing (reception): negative = area of non-interference / open doors; positive = self-mastery / who governs me? Organized postwar debate; this page is not the lecture. | concept | political-philosophy
- [[Concepts/Value Pluralism (Berlin)]] — Ultimate goods are many, genuine, and clash; the perfect whole is conceptually incoherent. Now primary from *Crooked Timber*; 1958 lecture still reception-only. | concept | political-philosophy, ethics, history-of-ideas
- [[Concepts/Pluralism versus Relativism (Berlin)]] — Many objective ends inside a human horizon, not “coffee / champagne.” Vico and Herder are not relativists. | concept | history-of-ideas, ethics, political-philosophy
- [[Concepts/Utopianism and the Pursuit of the Ideal (Berlin)]] — Three-legged monism makes a perfect society look possible; it is incoherent, and the search is a recipe for bloodshed. | concept | history-of-ideas, political-philosophy, ethics
- [[Concepts/Counter-Enlightenment (Berlin)]] — Vico/Herder (healthy fruit) and Maistre (dark pole) against Enlightenment uniformitarianism. Berlin’s grouping, not a school they joined. | concept | history-of-ideas, political-philosophy
- [[Concepts/Romantic Will (Berlin)]] — Ends are made, not found (Fichte). One trunk, two branches: man-as-end and human-material politics. | concept | history-of-ideas, political-philosophy
- [[Concepts/Bent Twig Nationalism (Berlin)]] — Nationalism is inflamed national consciousness, usually from wounds. Zionism and Palestinian nationalism as mirror cases. | concept | history-of-ideas, political-philosophy
- [[Concepts/Liberty versus the Conditions of Liberty (Berlin)]] — Liberty is one thing, the conditions of its exercise another. The hinge Tully/Gould refuse and Crowder defends. | concept | political-philosophy
- [[Concepts/Greatest Happiness Principle (Mill)]] — The foundational standard of utilitarian ethics, holding that actions are right in proportion as they tend to promote happiness, with qualitative distinctions of pleasure. | concept | ethics
- [[Concepts/Historical Materialism (Marx)]] — The theory that human history is driven by developments in the economic base (productive forces and relations of production) rather than ideas or Spirit. | concept | philosophy-of-history, political-philosophy, economics
- [[Concepts/Class Struggle (Marx)]] — The historical engine of social change, driven by structural conflicts between different economic classes over control of the means of production. | concept | political-philosophy, economics

**Rationalism & Metaphysics (Leibniz, 2026-06-11 Ingest)**:
- [[Concepts/Monad (Leibniz)]] — A simple, immaterial, indivisible, non-physical substance that mirrors the entire universe from its own perspective; the windowless fundamental building block of reality. | concept | metaphysics, philosophy-of-mind
- [[Concepts/Pre-Established Harmony (Leibniz)]] — The rationalist solution to mind-body causality, proposing that God pre-synchronized all windowless monads at creation so they function in perfect coordination without direct interaction. | concept | metaphysics, philosophy-of-mind

**Phenomenology & Existentialism (Heidegger VSI, 2026-06-29 Ingest)**:
- [[Concepts/Being and the Ontological Difference (Heidegger)]] — The question "What is Being?" and the distinction between Being (*Sein*) and beings (*Seiendes*). Heidegger's tripartite that/what/how-being; critique of Aristotle and Descartes for homogenising being as present-at-hand; epistemology "sharpens the knife but never gets round to cutting." | concept | ontology, metaphysics
- [[Concepts/Dasein (Heidegger)]] — The entity whose own being is an issue for it; "the essence of Dasein lies in its existence." Existentials replace categories; mineness (*Jemeinigkeit*); *das Man* (the "they"); authenticity/inauthenticity; possibility prior to actuality; thrownness; embodiment as inconspicuous. | concept | ontology, phenomenology, philosophy-of-mind
- [[Concepts/Being-in-the-World (Heidegger)]] — Dasein's fundamental constitution: always already practically engaged in a web of significance, not a Cartesian subject confronting objects. World as referential totality; space as significance; being-with-others (*Mitsein*); moods as attunement (*Befindlichkeit*); understanding and interpretation (fore-structures). | concept | ontology, phenomenology, epistemology
- [[Concepts/Readiness-to-Hand and Presence-at-Hand (Heidegger)]] — Equipment in use (*zuhanden*) withdraws into transparency; breakdown (conspicuousness, obtrusiveness, obstinacy) reveals the referential structure. Present-at-hand (*vorhanden*) is derivative, not primary. Critique of Aristotle/Descartes for treating all entities as bearers of properties. | concept | ontology, phenomenology, epistemology
- [[Concepts/Truth as Unconcealment (Heidegger)]] — Truth is *aletheia* (unconcealment), not correspondence. "There is truth only in so far as Dasein is." Assertions derivative of pre-predicative disclosure; falsehood as covering-up; truth as degree; Newton's laws "became true through Newton." | concept | ontology, epistemology
- [[Concepts/Care (Sorge) (Heidegger)]] — Dasein's unifying structure: "ahead-of-itself-Being-already-in-(the-world) as Being-alongside." Three moments (existence/facticity/fallenness) map onto temporality. Concern (*Besorgen*) toward equipment, solicitude (*Fürsorge*) toward others. The "scandal of philosophy" dissolved. | concept | ontology, phenomenology
- [[Concepts/Being-toward-Death and Authenticity (Heidegger)]] — Death as Dasein's ownmost, non-relational, not-to-be-outstripped possibility. Anxiety (*Angst*) vs. fear; conscience as call to choose-to-choose; primordial guilt as nullity; resoluteness (*Entschlossenheit*) as authentic disclosedness — but no objective moral criteria ("Hitler was no less resolute than Christ or Socrates"). | concept | ontology, ethics, phenomenology
- [[Concepts/Temporality (Heidegger)]] — Dasein's deepest layer: three ecstases (future/having-been-ness/present) grounding care. Four levels of time (primordial → inauthentic → world-time → now-time). Transcendence and freedom grounded in ecstatic temporality. Rejects all atemporal entities. | concept | ontology, metaphysics
- [[Concepts/Historicality and the Turn (Heidegger)]] — "History is not dead and gone, history is what we are." Fate (*Schicksal*), destiny (*Geschick*), repetition/retrieval. BT's incompleteness and the Turn (*Kehre*) from Dasein-centred analysis to Being's self-disclosure through art, language, thinking. "Language speaks, not man." | concept | ontology, philosophy-of-history
- [[Concepts/Art and Truth (Heidegger)]] — Art as "the truth of beings setting itself to work" (*Origin of the Work of Art*). World/earth strife; rift (*Riss*); founding of truth (bestowing/grounding/beginning). Poetry (*Dichtung*) prior to all arts as projective saying. Van Gogh shoes, Greek temple, Hölderlin. | concept | aesthetics, ontology

**Being and Time Primary Text (Heidegger, 2026-07-01 Ingest)**:
- [[Concepts/Phenomenological Method and Destruktion (Heidegger)]] — "Ontology is possible only as phenomenology": phenomenon/logos/aletheia, hermeneutics, the Gefragtes/Befragtes/Erfragtes question-structure, ontic/ontological priority of Dasein, and the positive Destruktion of the history of ontology (Kant→Descartes→Aristotle; being as *ousia* = presence). | concept | phenomenology, ontology, methodology
- [[Concepts/The They (das Man) and Falling Prey (Heidegger)]] — Mitsein and the with-world; "everyone is the other, and no one is himself"; distantiality, averageness, leveling down, publicness; idle talk, curiosity, ambiguity; Verfallen as ontological movement; authenticity as existentiell modification of the they. | concept | ontology, phenomenology, social-philosophy
- [[Concepts/Disclosedness — Attunement, Understanding, Discourse (Heidegger)]] — Dasein as its "there": mood disclosing thrownness; understanding as thrown project ("become what you are!"); interpretation, fore-structure, hermeneutic circle ("get in it in the right way"); statement as derivative (hermeneutical→apophantical "as"); Rede as ground of language. | concept | ontology, epistemology, philosophy-of-language
- [[Concepts/Anxiety (Angst) (Heidegger)]] — The objectless fundamental attunement: world as "nothing and nowhere," complete insignificance, uncanniness; individualizes Dasein, makes fear possible; attunement of being-toward-death and resoluteness. | concept | ontology, existentialism
- [[Concepts/Reality and the External World (Heidegger)]] — §43: the "scandal of philosophy" is that proofs of the external world are attempted at all; against Kant's Refutation of Idealism, realism, and idealism; "reality (not the real) is dependent upon care." | concept | ontology, epistemology
- [[Concepts/Conscience, Guilt, and Resoluteness (Heidegger)]] — The silent call of care from uncanniness; existential guilt as "being the (null) ground of a nullity" prior to morality; resoluteness (§60) and anticipatory resoluteness; selfhood as self-constancy vs. Kant's "I think." | concept | ontology, ethics, existentialism
- [[Concepts/World-Time and the Vulgar Concept of Time (Heidegger)]] — Temporality→world-time→now-time: datability, spannedness, publicness, significance; the sun as natural clock; Aristotle's "number of motion" as paradigm of vulgar time; against Hegel on time and spirit; §83's unanswered closing questions. | concept | ontology, philosophy-of-time

**Power, Knowledge, and the Subject (Michel Foucault, 2026-06-29 Ingest)**:
- [[Concepts/History of Madness (Foucault)]] — "Madness exists only within a society": mental illness is not a natural category but the product of reason's institutional exclusion of unreason. Traces three eras (Renaissance, Classical confinement, psychiatric liberation-as-control). | concept | philosophy, history, political-philosophy
- [[Concepts/Episteme and the Order of Things (Foucault)]] — Each historical period governed by an unconscious episteme determining what can count as knowledge. Three Western epistemes: Renaissance similitude, Classical mathesis/taxinomia, Modern human sciences ending with the predicted "death of man." | concept | epistemology, philosophy-of-science
- [[Concepts/Archaeology of Knowledge (Foucault)]] — Method mapping the anonymous rules (historical a priori, discursive formations, archive) that determine what statements can be made and recognized as true in a period — bracketing authorship, meaning, and reference. | concept | epistemology, methodology
- [[Concepts/Genealogy (Nietzsche)]] — Critique of the *value* of moral values, written in gray (documented past) not blue English origin-hypotheses; rumination; Essay III as model of interpretation; origin ≠ purpose (II.12). Not Foucault's "history of the present." | concept | ethics, methodology
- [[Concepts/Genealogy (Foucault)]] — "History of the present": four rules in *Discipline and Punish* I.1; invert the prison's success→failure→reform chronology. Nietzschean method; Nietzsche unnamed in that chapter. | concept | methodology, political-philosophy, history
- [[Concepts/Power-Knowledge (Foucault)]] — "Power produces knowledge"; the knowing subject is an effect. Canonical sentence from *Discipline and Punish* I.1; HS1 adds power-knowledge-pleasure, the analytics of power (IV.2), and reverse discourse. | concept | political-philosophy, epistemology
- [[Concepts/Political Technology of the Body (Foucault)]] — Micro-physics and political anatomy: the body as productive *and* subjected force; "the soul is the prison of the body"; docile bodies (distributions, activity, geneses, forces). | concept | political-philosophy, epistemology, history
- [[Concepts/Sovereign Power and the Spectacle of the Scaffold (Foucault)]] — *Supplice* as technique reconstituting injured sovereignty by terror, not justice; confession and judicial torture; popular inversion of the scaffold; contrast pole of panopticism. | concept | political-philosophy, history
- [[Concepts/Disciplinary Society and the Panopticon (Foucault)]] — Discipline "'makes' individuals" via hierarchical observation, normalizing judgement, examination. Panopticon is a *diagram*, not a building; disciplinary society = dissemination, not capture by the prison. | concept | political-philosophy, sociology, history
- [[Concepts/The Delinquent and the Carceral Continuum (Foucault)]] — Prison succeeds by producing delinquency (usable illegality); carceral archipelago/continuum; judges of normality; Mettray 1840. Not an abolitionist tract. | concept | political-philosophy, sociology, history
- [[Concepts/Biopolitics and Governmentality (Foucault)]] — 1976 primary: take life / let live → foster life / disallow it; anatomo-politics of the body + bio-politics of the population. Governmentality remains the 1978–79 lectures (not this book). | concept | political-philosophy, sociology
- [[Concepts/History of Sexuality (Foucault)]] — Rewritten from Hurley: not "why are we repressed?" but why we say we are; discursive fact; homosexual as species; sex as fictitious point; bodies and pleasures. | concept | political-philosophy, ethics, sociology
- [[Concepts/Repressive Hypothesis (Foucault)]] — Named target of HS1: Victorian/capitalist/work-imperative story plus speaker's benefit; three doubts; prohibition is local, not constitutive. | concept | political-philosophy, history
- [[Concepts/Scientia Sexualis and Ars Erotica (Foucault)]] — Two procedures for producing the truth of sex; confession produces (does not merely extract) truth; the West's "no *ars erotica*" is immediately qualified. | concept | epistemology, ethics, history
- [[Concepts/Deployment of Sexuality (Foucault)]] — *Dispositif de sexualité*: four strategies; family as sexualizer not prohibitor; alliance vs sexuality; "sex" as imaginary point. | concept | political-philosophy, sociology, history
- [[Concepts/Ethics of the Self and Care of the Self (Foucault)]] — Greco-Roman ethics as aesthetics of existence: care of the self (epimeleia heautou), use of pleasures (chresis aphrodision), parrhesia (truth-telling courage). Ethics as self-constitution, not law-following. | concept | ethics, ancient-philosophy
- [[Concepts/Specific Intellectual (Foucault)]] — Against the universal intellectual (Sartre): the specific intellectual speaks from concrete, expert knowledge of a particular institution rather than in the name of universal humanity. | concept | political-philosophy, ethics

**Poststructuralism & Difference (Gilles Deleuze, 2026-06-29 Ingest)**:
- [[Concepts/Rhizome vs Trees (Deleuze-Guattari)]] — Anti-hierarchical model of thought: the tree enforces binary branching; the rhizome connects any point to any other across heterogeneous registers. Six principles: connection, heterogeneity, multiplicity (n−1), asignifying rupture, cartography, decalcomania. | concept | metaphysics, philosophy-of-language, political-philosophy
- [[Concepts/Becoming (Deleuze)]] — Pure transformation simultaneously pulling in both directions; perpetually eludes the present (Aeon vs. Chronos). Affirms the simulacrum against Platonic Forms; dissolves personal identity. | concept | metaphysics, ontology
- [[Concepts/Incorporeal Event (Deleuze-Stoics)]] — Stoic: events are incorporeal (subsisting at the surface of bodies, expressed by infinitives). Ethics = counteractualization, being worthy of what happens; the "fourth person singular." | concept | metaphysics, ontology, ethics
- [[Concepts/Haecceity and Multiplicity (Deleuze)]] — Individuation irreducible to subjects/substances: longitude (speeds/slownesses), latitude (capacities to affect/be affected). Plane of consistency vs. plane of forms. | concept | metaphysics, ontology
- [[Concepts/Ethics Without Morality (Deleuze-Spinoza)]] — Body as model; consciousness as triple illusion. Ethics (immanent typology) vs. morality (transcendent judgment). Good/bad replaces Good/Evil; sad passions as political instruments; joy as criterion. | concept | ethics, metaphysics
- [[Concepts/Eternal Return as Selective Ontology (Deleuze-Nietzsche)]] — Two-stage test: (1) practical elimination of half-willing; (2) expulsion of reactive forces — only becoming-active has being. Transmutation = negation → affirmation. Overman as superfold. | concept | metaphysics, ethics, ontology
- [[Concepts/Desire and the Body without Organs (Deleuze-Guattari)]] — Desire is productive (not lack); unrolls a plane of immanence (BwO). Against Oedipal overcoding; desire is collective and assembled; pleasure interrupts. | concept | ethics, political-philosophy, philosophy-of-mind
- [[Concepts/Schizoanalysis and Anti-Oedipus (Deleuze-Guattari)]] — Pragmatic alternative to interpretation: asks what lines one is on. Capitalism as axiomatic of decoded flows; machinic enslavement vs. social subjection. | concept | political-philosophy, ethics
- [[Concepts/Minor Literature and Language (Deleuze-Guattari)]] — Minor literature (Kafka): high deterritorialization, immediate political charge, collective enunciation. Major/minor = constants vs. continuous variation. | concept | philosophy-of-language, aesthetics, political-philosophy
- [[Concepts/Molar and Molecular Lines (Deleuze-Guattari)]] — Three lines: rigid segmentary, supple molecular, line of flight. Society defined by its lines of flight. Politics as active experimentation. | concept | political-philosophy, metaphysics
- [[Concepts/Smooth and Striated Space (Deleuze-Guattari)]] — Smooth: intensive, haptic, nomadic. Striated: metric, optical, State-organized. Nomad art positively expresses smooth space. | concept | aesthetics, political-philosophy, metaphysics
- [[Concepts/Cinema - Time-Image and Movement-Image (Deleuze)]] — Movement-image: sensory-motor schemata. Time-image (post-war): pure optical-sound situations; chronosigns/lectosigns/noosigns; time shown directly. | concept | aesthetics, philosophy-of-time
- [[Concepts/Diagram (Deleuze on Francis Bacon)]] — Asignifying, involuntary marks that break through figurative givens and open possibilities of fact. Bacon's third path: localized diagram produces the Figure. | concept | aesthetics, philosophy-of-art

**Public Choice & Political Economy (Mancur Olson, Buchanan & Tullock, 2026-06-29 Ingest)**:
- [[Concepts/Collective Action and Free Riding (Olson)]] — The logic of group organization; why rational self-interested individuals free-ride on public goods, and why large groups fail to organize without selective incentives. | concept | political-philosophy, economics
- [[Concepts/Distributional Coalitions (Olson)]] — Lobbies, cartels, and interest groups focused on redistributing existing income to themselves, indifferent to the social efficiency losses they cause. | concept | political-philosophy, economics
- [[Concepts/Institutional Sclerosis (Olson)]] — The accumulation of distributional coalitions in stable societies over time, which complicates regulation and retards economic growth and technological adoption. | concept | political-philosophy, economics, sociology
- [[Concepts/Encompassing Organizations (Olson)]] — Large-scale interest groups that represent a substantial share of a nation's resources and therefore internalize the social costs of their redistributive actions. | concept | political-philosophy, economics
- [[Concepts/Jurisdictional Integration and Sclerosis (Olson)]] — The dismantling of domestic cartels and guilds by expanding market boundaries (e.g., EEC or customs unions) and shifting policy decisions to a larger scale. | concept | political-philosophy, economics
- [[Concepts/Stagflation and Evolutionary Macroeconomics (Olson)]] — Macroeconomic rigidities and sticky prices explained by the slow collective decision-making of coalitions, providing micro-foundations for involuntary unemployment and stagflation. | concept | political-philosophy, economics
- [[Concepts/Constitutional Political Economy (Buchanan-Tullock)]] — The choice of rules (constitutional stage, veil of uncertainty) vs. choices made within those rules (operational stage). | concept | political-philosophy, economics
- [[Concepts/Decision-Making Costs Model (Buchanan-Tullock)]] — The expected costs function (External Costs + Decision Costs) used to model the optimal consensus threshold. | concept | political-philosophy, economics
- [[Concepts/Unanimity Rule (Buchanan-Tullock)]] — Unanimity as the unique rule that guarantees Pareto-efficient outcomes, limited by holdout and transaction costs. | concept | political-philosophy, economics
- [[Concepts/Logrolling and Vote-Trading (Buchanan-Tullock)]] — Exchange of votes across a sequence of issues, allowing expression of preference intensity; Pareto efficiency vs. pork-barrel spending. | concept | political-philosophy, economics
- [[Concepts/Pressure Groups and Special Interests (Buchanan-Tullock)]] — Lobbies and special interests analyzed as rational responses to expected differential advantages from the public budget. | concept | political-philosophy, economics
- [[Concepts/Rational Irrationality (Caplan)]] — When the price of holding a false belief approaches zero (as in voting), rational agents consume irrationality to satiation; the mechanism behind systematic democratic failure. | concept | political-philosophy, economics, epistemology
- [[Concepts/Miracle of Aggregation]] — The statistical claim that random voter errors cancel out in large electorates; valid only if errors are unsystematic — refuted by Caplan's SAEE data. | concept | political-philosophy, economics
- [[Concepts/Systematic Economic Biases (Caplan)]] — Four directional biases in lay economic belief (anti-market, anti-foreign, make-work, pessimistic) documented by the 1996 SAEE, driving persistent democratic policy failure. | concept | political-philosophy, economics
- [[Concepts/Rational Ignorance (Downs-Tullock)]] — The rational choice not to acquire political information because one vote almost never determines outcomes; precursor to Caplan's rational irrationality. | concept | political-philosophy, economics
- [[Concepts/Paradox of Democracy (Caplan)]] — Why democracies persistently adopt policies harmful to most citizens; resolved by voter irrationality rather than agency failure or mere ignorance. | concept | political-philosophy, economics
- [[Concepts/Democratic Fundamentalism (Caplan)]] — The quasi-religious commitment to democracy as immune to external critique; the mirror image of the market fundamentalism charge routinely leveled at economists. | concept | political-philosophy
- [[Concepts/The Idea Trap (Caplan)]] — A self-reinforcing cycle: economic shocks reduce voter literacy, intensify bad-policy demand, worsen outcomes, repeat. | concept | political-philosophy, economics

**Wittgenstein's Philosophical Investigations - A Critical Guide (Arif Ahmed, 2026-06-29)**:
- [[Concepts/The Standard Metre Bar (Wittgenstein)]] — PI §50: the bar is a means of representation, not an object of one. Kripke's side now primary: the definition fixes the reference of a rigid designator; "Stick S is one meter long at t₀" is contingent a priori. Jacquette polarity reply remains. WRPL is a different collision. | concept | philosophy-of-language, epistemology
- [[Concepts/Concept Possession as Ability (Wittgenstein)]] — The view that possessing a concept or understanding a word is a practice-embedded technique or two-way power, rather than an occurrent mental or physical state. | concept | philosophy-of-mind, philosophy-of-language
- [[Concepts/Semantic Contextualism (Wittgenstein)]] — Bridges's critique of the contextualist reading of Wittgenstein's proper names (PI §79), defending the anti-theoretical character of meaning against Truth-Conditional Semantics. | concept | philosophy-of-language
- [[Concepts/Linguistic Turn (Rorty)]] — Rorty's historicist account of the linguistic turn, contrasting quietist therapeutic metaphilosophy with pragmatic social-practice theory. | concept | metaphilosophy, pragmatism
- [[Concepts/Deductive Inference as Aspect Perception (Wittgenstein)]] — Ahmed's thesis that logical rules are autonomous and logical insight into novel rules is a perceptual achievement of aspect-dawning. | concept | philosophy-of-logic, epistemology
- [[Concepts/Remembering Intentions (Wittgenstein)]] — Child's exegesis of PI §§633–693, analyzing past semantic intentions as dispositional realities rather than conscious experiences. | concept | philosophy-of-mind, epistemology
- [[Concepts/The Small Things - Nutrition Place Climate Recreation (Nietzsche)]] — Diet, locality, climate and choice of recreation as the questions on which "the salvation of humanity" depends; "the scholar is a decadent"; the casuistry of selfishness. | concept | ethics, philosophy-of-life
- [[Concepts/Becoming What One Is (Nietzsche)]] — *Ecce Homo*'s subtitle and its paradox: becoming what one is presupposes not knowing what one is; self-forgetting as reason. Against Aristotle's telos and Sartre's lucid project. | concept | ethics, philosophy-of-mind
- [[Concepts/Style as Communication of a State (Nietzsche)]] — Style as the transmission of a pathos through signs *and their tempo*; no "good style in itself"; the acoustic delusion by which readers conclude there is nothing to hear. Contrasted with Skinner/Austin on uptake. | concept | aesthetics, philosophy-of-language, rhetoric
- [[Concepts/Inspiration as Revelation (Nietzsche)]] — The *Zarathustra* chapter's phenomenology of inspiration: passivity, involuntariness, rhythm as the measure of force, "the body is inspired." The vault's case of a physiological explanation used to authorize rather than deflate. | concept | aesthetics, philosophy-of-mind
- [[Concepts/The Immoralist (Nietzsche)]] — The title's two denials (the good man as highest type; Christian morality); "the condition of the existence of the good is the lie"; morality's self-overcoming through truthfulness. 1881 *Dawn* §103 is a different pair (hypocrisy / false hypotheses). | concept | ethics, philosophy-of-religion
- [[Concepts/Morality of Custom (Nietzsche)]] — *Dawn* §9: morality *is* obedience to tradition as command; the free man is *immoral*. 1887 sovereign individual is the fruit, not this aphorism. | concept | ethics, moral-psychology
- [[Concepts/Faith in Morals (Nietzsche)]] — 1886 Preface to *Dawn*: the philosophers' unexamined foundation; Circe; *autosuppression of morals*. 1881 does the mining. | concept | ethics, metaphilosophy
- [[Concepts/Feeling of Power (Nietzsche)]] — *Dawn*'s psychology (feeling / desire / love / demon of power). **Not** will to power. | concept | ethics, moral-psychology
- [[Concepts/Revaluation of All Values (Nietzsche)]] — Not a new table of values but a formula for an operation, with the September 1888 chronology. Records what the text does *not* say: no four-part plan, no identification with *The Antichrist*, no *Will to Power* project. | concept | ethics, history-of-ideas

### Schools

Movements and traditions (`type: school`, in `Schools/`).

- [[Schools/Confucianism]] — *Analects*-shaped (Chin 2014), not Mencius/Xunzi/Zhu Xi. Humaneness, rites, gentleman, filiality, rectifying names, *shu*. Virtue-rule and trust; stay in office knowing the Way cannot be realized. Youzi vs Zengzi and Book 19 already split the school. | school | ethics, political-philosophy, philosophy-of-language
- [[Schools/Daoism]] — Philosophical position of the *Daodejing* (Ziporyn 2023), not later religious Daoism. Course, ironic virtuosity, nondoing, unhewn, water/low place. Quietist (chs. 17, 57, 80) and proto-Legalist (chs. 3, 65) readings both live in the text. Confucian counterpart now in; *Zhuangzi* still outstanding. | school | metaphysics, ethics, political-philosophy, philosophy-of-language
- [[Schools/Neoplatonism]] — The Platonic tradition Plotinus inaugurated (204–70) through Porphyry, Iamblichus, and the Athenian School to the Academy's closure (529): three aims (systematic metaphysics of the One, the soul's return, defense of Hellenism against Christianity); later development as footnotes to the *Enneads*' open problems; vast influence via Augustine, Pseudo-Dionysius (now primary: *Divine Names* / *Mystical Theology*), Islamic falsafa, and the Renaissance. | school | metaphysics, philosophy-of-religion, history-of-philosophy
- [[Schools/Neopythagoreanism]] — Not the ancient school but its *reconstruction*: "a branch of Platonism that emphasizes the role of number" (One/Indefinite Dyad), from Speusippus and Xenocrates through Eudorus, Moderatus, Nicomachus, and Numenius, plus the Roman wonder-workers (Nigidius Figulus, Apollonius of Tyana). The image of Pythagoras the Middle Ages inherited. | school | metaphysics, religion, reception-history
- [[Schools/Pythagoreanism]] — The brotherhood founded by Pythagoras at Croton (c. 530 BC): on Burkert, metempsychosis + ritual *acusmata* at its core with a later, contested *mathematici* strand; on Zhmud (2012) a political *hetairia* (not a sect), with the acusmatici/mathematici split a late fiction and Aristoxenus' catalogue as its prosopographical backbone (Croton → Thebes/Phlius → Archytas of Tarentum). | school | metaphysics, mathematics, religion
- [[Schools/Existentialism]] — Sartre's 1945/46 definition: existence precedes essence / begin from the subjective. Two columns: Christian (Jaspers, Marcel) and atheistic (Heidegger, the French, Sartre). Heidegger's *Letter on Humanism* refuses the enrolment. Kierkegaard now a primary (*Fear and Trembling*); not in Sartre's two columns. Overlaps [[Schools/Phenomenology]]; not identical. | school | existentialism, ontology, ethics
- [[Schools/Phenomenology]] — Founded by Edmund Husserl (early 20th c.): systematic enquiry into conscious experience ("to the things themselves"). Husserl now primary (*Cartesian Meditations*): neo-Cartesianism, *ego—cogito—cogitatum*, pairing, monadological idealism. Heidegger radicalised it: from consciousness to Dasein, from epistemology to ontology, from timeless essences to historicality, added *Destruktion*. Sartre: negative phenomenology. Merleau-Ponty now primary (*Phenomenology of Perception*): incomplete reduction, one's own body, *I can*, situated freedom. Also Levinas, Gadamer, Derrida. | school | epistemology, ontology, philosophy-of-mind
- [[Schools/Enactivism]] — Varela, Thompson, and Rosch 1991: cognition as embodied action that enacts a world. Constructive sequel to Dreyfus; neighbouring family to Clark's extended mind, not the same thesis. | school | philosophy-of-mind, cognitive-science, phenomenology

*(Most schools are still captured only in the `schools:` frontmatter of thinker and concept pages — e.g. Stoicism, Epicureanism, Empiricism, Rationalism, Neoplatonism, Pyrrhonism, Scholasticism, Pragmatism, Aristotelianism, Platonism. Promote to dedicated pages on a future pass / leg transition.)*

### Arguments

Standalone lines of reasoning that recur across sources (`type: argument`, in `Arguments/`).

- [[Arguments/Ontological Argument]] — Traces the a priori proof of God's existence from Anselm to Descartes, along with key historical critiques (Gaunilo, Kant, Hume). Kant now primary: [[Concepts/Existence is Not a Real Predicate (Kant)]]. | argument | metaphysics, epistemology
- [[Arguments/Antinomies of Pure Reason (Kant)]] — Four cosmological conflicts; transcendental idealism as the key; 1–2 both false, 3 both may be true (freedom + nature), 4 necessary being not in the series. | argument | metaphysics, epistemology
- [[Arguments/Simulation Argument (Bostrom)]] — Bostrom 2003 (now primary): at least one of *f_P* ≈ 0 / *f_I* ≈ 0 / *f_sim* ≈ 1; even split in current ignorance; official corollary is about *our* descendants, not a proof we are sims. Bland indifference; attenuated substrate-independence. Virk reception (NPC reading, probability garble); Chalmers refinement (humanlike sims, sim blockers, ≥25%). Tension with *Superintelligence* horn 1. | argument | metaphysics, probability, ai
- [[Arguments/Knowledge Argument (Jackson)]] — Complete physical information is not complete information. 1982 origin (Fred, then Mary); distinguished from the modal/zombie argument and from Nagel's point of view. 1986 display: she knows everything *physical* about other people, not everything about them. Not imagination, not intensionality, not her own experiences. Objections: Churchland ×3; Lewis–Nemirow ability analysis. | argument | philosophy-of-mind, epistemology, metaphysics
- [[Arguments/Inconsistent Triad in Sense-Datum Theories (Sellars)]] — Sensing a sense content entails non-inferentially knowing it is red (A); the ability to sense is unacquired (B); the ability to know facts of the form *x is φ* is acquired (C). Any two entail the denial of the third. The classical sense datum is "a mongrel" of an explanatory posit and an epistemic foundation. Includes the "another language" (code vs. theory) rebuttal to Ayer. | argument | epistemology, philosophy-of-mind
- [[Arguments/Zombie Argument (Chalmers)]] — Five converging arguments against logical supervenience of consciousness (zombie conceivability, inverted spectrum, epistemic asymmetry, Mary, absence of analysis) plus the survey of failed reductive programs; Levine's explanatory gap; direct clash with Dennett. Jackson 1982 ranks the knowledge argument *above* this modal argument. Ancestor: [[Arguments/Identity Thesis and Pain (Kripke)]] (no "zombie," no full-world duplicate). | argument | philosophy-of-mind, metaphysics
- [[Arguments/Identity Thesis and Pain (Kripke)]] — If pain = C-fiber stimulation the identity is necessary; the heat analogy fails because pain is picked out by being pain; God must still make the stimulation felt as pain. Dualism not thereby entailed. | argument | philosophy-of-mind, metaphysics
- [[Arguments/Cartesian Skepticism and the Simulation Riposte (Chalmers)]] — The master argument for external-world skepticism, the Simulation Riposte ("That's what someone in a simulation would say") defeating every classic refutation, and Chalmers's dissolution: concede the Knowledge Question, win the Reality Question. Putnam item now reconstructed from Pritchard and Ranalli 2016. | argument | epistemology, metaphysics
- [[Arguments/Putnam Brain in a Vat Argument]] — From causal constraints on reference: a lifelong computer-envatted brain cannot truly think "I am a BIV." Semantic, not a knowledge claim. Brueckner (question-begging / circular), Wright (third-person / self-knowledge), Stroud (transcendental dilemma), Nagel (reductio). Reception from 2016. | argument | epistemology, philosophy-of-language, metaphysics
- [[Arguments/Otto and Inga (Clark and Chalmers)]] — If Inga's standing memory is a belief, Otto's notebook entry is a belief; Twin Otto shows a belief is not in the head. Four coupling features; experiences not claimed to extend. | argument | philosophy-of-mind, epistemology
- [[Arguments/Wilt Chamberlain Argument (Nozick)]] — Start from any patterned just *D*1; people pay Wilt a quarter; *D*2 is just if *D*1 was. Liberty upsets patterns. | argument | political-philosophy, ethics
- [[Arguments/Three Problemata (Kierkegaard)]] — If ethical = universal, Abraham is condemned. Hegelians praise Abraham. Therefore ethical ≠ universal — or stop talking of faith. *Modus tollens* against *Sittlichkeit*. | argument | ethics, philosophy-of-religion

### Contradictions

Major unresolved tensions between pages (`type: contradiction`, in `Contradictions/`). Each states both positions, names the pages/sources holding them, and is linked from both conflicting pages via `> [!warning]` callouts. Created in the 2026-06-04 schema migration.

- [[Contradictions/Confucius vs Laozi - Humaneness and Rites vs Course Lost]] — Are *ren* / *li* / *xiao* the work of a moral way (Analects) or symptoms that the course has already been lost (*Daodejing* chs. 18–19, 38)? Both now primary. Shared *wuwei* word (15.5 vs chs. 37–48) is not the same thesis. Anchors: [[Thinkers/Confucius]] ↔ [[Thinkers/Laozi]].
- [[Contradictions/Dehaene vs Chalmers - Access Consciousness vs the Hard Problem]] — Dehaene: explain conscious access and leftover qualia go the way of vitalism; Chalmers swapped the easy/hard labels. Chalmers: GNW/signatures explain awareness, not experience; zombies remain conceivable. Anchors: [[Thinkers/Stanislas Dehaene]] ↔ [[Thinkers/David Chalmers]]. Localizes the Type-A side of Dennett vs Nagel onto a specific reductive program.
- [[Contradictions/Merleau-Ponty vs Sartre - Situated Freedom vs Radical Freedom]] — Sartre: wholly and forever free, or not at all; not to choose is to choose. Merleau-Ponty: never determinism and never an absolute choice; not-refusing is not a choice. Sartre named once (the dream). Anchors: [[Thinkers/Maurice Merleau-Ponty]] ↔ [[Thinkers/Jean-Paul Sartre]].
- [[Contradictions/Sartre vs Freud - Lucidity vs the Unconscious]] - Both understand the human from subjectivity and self-formation, but split over whether the mind harbours an *unconscious* (Freud) or is wholly *lucid* (Sartre). Foucault's third pole (HS1): Freud is a relay of the deployment of sexuality, not the breaker of silence. Anchors: [[Thinkers/Jean-Paul Sartre]] ↔ Freud (no dedicated page) ↔ [[Thinkers/Michel Foucault]].
- [[Contradictions/Heraclitean Flux vs Parmenidean Stasis]] - Is change/becoming the fundamental nature of reality (all is flux), or is change logically impossible (stasis)? Anchors: [[Thinkers/Heraclitus]] ↔ [[Thinkers/Parmenides]].
- [[Contradictions/Pythagoras the Scientist vs the Religious Charismatic]] - Was Pythagoras the founder of mathematical natural science or a religious wonder-worker onto whom later ages projected a science he never had? A clash over historical *method* between two rigorous moderns: **Burkert (1972)** (deflation — religious charismatic; science is late/Academic) ↔ **Zhmud (2012)** (rehabilitation — both teacher and scientist; the *legend* grew, not the science). They converge on two negatives (smithy experiment fiction; tetractys late). Anchors: [[Sources/Lore and Science in Ancient Pythagoreanism - Walter Burkert (1972)]] ↔ [[Sources/Pythagoras and the Early Pythagoreans - Leonid Zhmud (2012)]] / [[Thinkers/Hippasus]].
- [[Contradictions/Atomist Materialism vs Platonic-Aristotelian Teleology]] - Is the order of the universe the result of blind, mechanical necessity (atoms/void) or intelligent design (teleology)? Anchors: [[Thinkers/Democritus]] ↔ [[Thinkers/Plato]] / [[Thinkers/Aristotle]].
- [[Contradictions/Dialectical Idealism vs. Atomist Materialism]] - Is the universe ultimately an organic, teleological self-unfolding of mind (Hegelian Idealism) or a blind, mechanical assembly of material particles (Atomist Materialism)? Anchors: [[Thinkers/Hegel]] ↔ [[Thinkers/Democritus]] / [[Thinkers/Lucretius]].
- [[Contradictions/Plato vs Aristotle on the Forms]] - Are universals separately existing, transcendent realities (Plato) or immanent forms of individual substances (Aristotle)? The foundational metaphysical split. Anchors: [[Thinkers/Plato]] ↔ [[Thinkers/Aristotle]].
- [[Contradictions/Plato vs Aristotle on the Ideal State]] - Should the ideal city maximize unity via guardian communism (Plato) or is the state essentially a self-sufficient plurality that maximal unity destroys, favoring private property with common use (Aristotle)? Anchors: [[Thinkers/Plato]] ↔ [[Thinkers/Aristotle]].
- [[Contradictions/Stoic Determinism vs Aristotelian Contingency]] - Is every event fixed by fate/*logos* (Stoics) or are some futures genuinely open (Aristotelian modality)? Anchors: [[Concepts/Dichotomy of Control (Epictetus)]] ↔ [[Concepts/Modal Copula (Aristotelian Modality)]]; the Epicurean swerve as a third option.
- [[Contradictions/Rationalism vs Empiricism on Innate Ideas]] - Does the mind hold innate ideas (Descartes) or is it a blank slate (Locke, Hume)? Anchors: [[Concepts/Clear and Distinct Perception (Descartes)]] ↔ [[Concepts/No Innate Principles or Ideas (Locke)]].
- [[Contradictions/Occasionalism vs Natural Causation]] - Is God the sole real cause (al-Ghazali) or do created things have real causal powers (Ibn Rushd)? With Hume's naturalist twist. Anchors: [[Concepts/Occasionalism vs Natural Causation (Ghazali and Ibn Rushd)]] ↔ [[Thinkers/Ibn Rushd]].
- [[Contradictions/Plotinus vs Iamblichus - Contemplation vs Theurgy]] - Can the soul return to the divine by philosophy alone (Plotinus, Porphyry — the undescended soul awakens through contemplation) or does salvation require ritual action and divine grace (Iamblichus, the Athenian School)? Prefigures Christian nature-vs-grace debates. Anchors: [[Thinkers/Plotinus]] ↔ [[Thinkers/Iamblichus]]. | contradiction | philosophy-of-religion, metaphysics
- [[Contradictions/Temporal Creation vs Eternal World]] - Did the world begin in time by God's free choice (al-Ghazali's eternal will) or is the cosmos beginningless (Avicenna/Aristotle, defended by Ibn Rushd)? Turns on whether "will" can select between indiscernibles without a reason. Aquinas & Kant agree the eternity side is undemonstrable. | contradiction | metaphysics, theology, cosmology
- [[Contradictions/The Highest Good - Pleasure vs Virtue]] - Is the highest good pleasure/ataraxia (Epicurus) or virtue alone (Stoics), with Plato's *Philebus* as a third position? Anchors: [[Thinkers/Epicurus]] ↔ [[Thinkers/Seneca]].
- [[Contradictions/Free Will - Spinozan Necessity vs Kantian Autonomy]] - Is the will an illusion of ignorance (Spinoza's necessitarianism) or autonomous self-legislation (Kant)? Anchors: [[Concepts/Deus sive Natura - Substance Monism (Spinoza)]] ↔ [[Concepts/Freedom as the Key to Autonomy (Kant)]].
- [[Contradictions/Hegelian Monism vs. Kantian Dualism]] - Does human reason possess the power to achieve absolute knowledge of ultimate reality (Hegelian Monism) or is it forever limited to sensory appearances (Kantian Dualism)? Anchors: [[Thinkers/Hegel]] ↔ [[Thinkers/Kant]].
- [[Contradictions/Popper vs Kuhn - Falsification vs Paradigms]] - The rationalism↔anarchism spectrum on method and progress: permanent rational **falsification** toward truth (Popper) ↔ paradigm-bound **normal science** + goal-less **revolutions** (Kuhn) ↔ Lakatos's research-programmes mediation ↔ **no method at all, "anything goes"** (Feyerabend) + Hacking's experimental pivot (entity realism via intervention/use grounds realism even when representation is troubled). The central tension of the Philosophy of Science leg. Anchors: [[Thinkers/Karl Popper]] ↔ [[Thinkers/Thomas Kuhn]] ↔ [[Thinkers/Paul Feyerabend]] ↔ [[Thinkers/Ian Hacking]].
- [[Contradictions/Seth vs Chalmers - Real Problem vs Hard Problem]] — Same Nagel datum; opposite agendas. **Seth**: explain/predict/control properties until the residual *why* evaporates (vitalism). **Chalmers**: after the functions, experience is still left over; materialism fails. Third dissolve route beside Clark and Dehaene. Anchors: [[Thinkers/Anil Seth]] ↔ [[Thinkers/David Chalmers]].
- [[Contradictions/Nozick vs Rawls - Historical Entitlement vs Patterned Justice]] — Is justice in holdings a history of entitled moves, or a patterned / end-state profile (difference principle) institutions must continuously realize? Rawls is reported from Nozick's Ch. 7, not a vault primary. Third voice: Buchanan veil of uncertainty. Anchors: [[Thinkers/Robert Nozick]] ↔ Rawls (uningested).
- [[Contradictions/Kripke vs Quine - De Re Necessity vs Holism]] — Quine: necessity lived on the analytic line and de re modality is unintelligible. Kripke: necessity is metaphysical; Hesperus/gold identities are necessary a posteriori. Not a synthesis; 2-D is a third position. Anchors: [[Thinkers/Saul Kripke]] ↔ [[Thinkers/W.V.O. Quine]].
- [[Contradictions/Putnam vs Nagel - Semantic Anti-Skepticism vs Intelligible Skepticism]] — Does the intelligibility of the BIV refute causal theories of reference, or do those theories show the scenario cannot be truly thought? Both sides reported (Putnam 1981, Nagel 1986). Third pole: Chalmers grants we cannot know we are not simulated. Anchors: [[Thinkers/Hilary Putnam]] ↔ [[Thinkers/Thomas Nagel]] / [[Thinkers/David Chalmers]].
- [[Contradictions/Hohwy vs Clark - Secluded Inference vs Extended Mind]] — Same Helmholtz/Friston mechanism; opposite geography. **Hohwy**: PEM relative to the nervous system, behind a veil of sensory input. **Clark 2016**: action-oriented engagement machine; evidentiary boundary is Hohwy's term; demon is a red herring; PP is extension-friendly, not a proof. 2023 then popularizes "perfect internal organs." Also split on psychosis (2016 more careful than 2023) and the hard problem (declined vs incremental vs later deflated). Anchors: [[Thinkers/Jakob Hohwy]] ↔ [[Thinkers/Andy Clark]].
- [[Contradictions/Dreyfus vs Computationalism - Skilled Coping vs Formal Rules]] - Can intelligence be reproduced by formal operations on discrete facts? **Dreyfus**: embodied situational know-how; GOFAI is a degenerating program; not a proof of impossibility. **Computationalism** (Turing, Minsky, Hofstadter, Chalmers): formal structure is what intelligence is. Third voices: Searle (syntax/semantics, different diagnosis), Clark (mechanizes Dreyfus, drops the pessimism), Hohwy (keeps the mechanism, rebuilds internalism), Mitchell (empirical ally), Varela/Thompson/Rosch (enaction: different fight — pregiven vs enacted world). Anchors: [[Thinkers/Hubert Dreyfus]] ↔ [[Thinkers/Alan Turing]] / [[Thinkers/Marvin Minsky]].
- [[Contradictions/Representational Cognitivism vs Enaction]] — Is cognition recovery of a pregiven world, or enactment of a world through embodied action? **Cognitivism**: strong representation. **Enaction**: operational closure + structural coupling + natural drift. Neighbouring: Dreyfus vs computationalism; Hohwy vs Clark. Anchors: [[Thinkers/Francisco J. Varela]] ↔ cognitivist pole (Minsky / Fodor / Jackendoff).
- [[Contradictions/Hadot vs Foucault - Care of the Self vs Cosmic Universalization]] — They agree antiquity is an art of living modernity forgot. **Foucault**: techniques of the self, aesthetics of existence, *voluptas*, writing *of* the self. **Hadot**: spiritual exercises aimed at the Whole; *gaudium* ≠ pleasure; interiorization is universalization; Foucault's model risks “late twentieth-century Dandyism.” Anchors: [[Thinkers/Pierre Hadot]] ↔ [[Thinkers/Michel Foucault]].
- [[Contradictions/Hofstadter vs Searle - Strange Loops vs Chinese Room]] - Can formal symbol manipulation produce genuine meaning and consciousness? **Hofstadter**: yes — via isomorphism and strange loops (sufficient complexity + self-reference → semantics emerges). **Searle**: no — syntax is never sufficient for semantics; biological causation is required. The vault's central AI & Cognitive Science contradiction. Anchors: [[Thinkers/Douglas Hofstadter]] ↔ [[Thinkers/John Searle]], with [[Thinkers/Alan Turing]]'s Imitation Game at the origin.
- [[Contradictions/Wiener vs Shannon - Information and Meaning]] - Two founders share the same entropy mathematics (H = −∑ p log p) but reach opposite conclusions: **Shannon** brackets semantics — "the semantic aspects of communication are irrelevant to the engineering problem"; **Wiener** insists information is fundamentally social and semantic — "society can only be understood through a study of the messages." Secondary clash: Shannon treats information as a measurable engineering resource; Wiener argues information is not a commodity (decays, anti-additive, cannot be owned). Anchors: [[Thinkers/Norbert Wiener]] ↔ [[Thinkers/Claude Shannon]].
- [[Contradictions/Pearl vs. Statistics Orthodoxy - Causation Purged and Recovered]] - The deepest methodological rift in the AI & Cognitive Science leg: **Galton, Pearson, and Fisher** deliberately excluded causal language from statistics (correlation-only science; RCTs as the only legitimate causal method). **Pearl**: this was a century-long mistake that blocked science and crippled AI at rung 1. Causal Revolution (1990s–2010s) provides the formal tools (do-calculus, DAGs, SCMs) to answer causal and counterfactual questions from observational data. Modern deep learning repeats the statistics orthodoxy's error at scale. Relation to Hume's induction problem, Popper's conjecturalism, and Turing's imitation game. Anchors: [[Thinkers/Judea Pearl]] ↔ the Galton/Pearson/Fisher tradition.
- [[Contradictions/Dennett vs Nagel - Multiple Drafts vs Subjective Experience]] - Is consciousness irreducibly subjective? **Nagel** (1974, now primary): something it is like for the organism; point of view is a type; physicalism unintelligible, not refuted; 2023 hopes for monism, not property dualism. **Dennett** (1991): the residue is a Theater illusion; heterophenomenology studies reports without remainder. Third voice: **Chalmers** takes Nagel's conceivability argument all the way to property dualism — a step Nagel refuses. Fourth voice: **Jackson** (1982, now primary) — a missing *property* that refutes Physicalism and may be physically idle. Anchors: [[Thinkers/Daniel Dennett]] ↔ [[Thinkers/Thomas Nagel]] / [[Thinkers/Frank Jackson]].
- [[Contradictions/Hobbesian Absolutism vs. Lockean Constitutionalism]] - Is sovereign political authority absolute, undivided, and irrevocable (Hobbes), or is it a limited, divisible fiduciary trust subject to a right of revolution (Locke)? Anchors: [[Thinkers/Thomas Hobbes]] ↔ [[Thinkers/Locke]].
- [[Contradictions/Utilitarian Consequentialism vs. Kantian Deontology]] - Is the morality of an action determined solely by its consequences (utilitarianism) or by its conformity to duty and the categorical imperative (deontology)? Anchors: [[Thinkers/John Stuart Mill]] ↔ [[Thinkers/Kant]].
- [[Contradictions/Dialectical Idealism vs. Historical Materialism]] - Is human history driven by the developmental unfolding of ideas and consciousness (Hegel) or by the concrete development of material, economic forces of production (Marx)? Anchors: [[Thinkers/Hegel]] ↔ [[Thinkers/Karl Marx]].
- [[Contradictions/Subjective Idealism vs. Physical Realism]] - Is reality fundamentally mind-dependent ideas (Berkeley's subjective idealism) or mind-independent material substances (Cartesian/Lockean physical realism)? Anchors: [[Thinkers/George Berkeley]] ↔ [[Thinkers/Descartes]] / [[Thinkers/Locke]].
- [[Contradictions/Rousseau vs. Hobbes on the State of Nature]] - Is the state of nature a state of peace, pity, and freedom corrupted by society (Rousseau) or a war of all against all (Hobbes)? Anchors: [[Thinkers/Jean-Jacques Rousseau]] ↔ [[Thinkers/Thomas Hobbes]].
- [[Contradictions/Berlin vs Rousseau - Negative Liberty vs Forced to Be Free]] — Can obedience to a collective will *be* freedom (Rousseau I.7–8), or is that domination renamed (Berlin)? Still lecture-shaped; *Crooked Timber* adds a second front (Maistre / “born free”). Liberal-despotism horn left open. Anchors: [[Thinkers/Isaiah Berlin]] ↔ [[Thinkers/Jean-Jacques Rousseau]].
- [[Contradictions/Kierkegaard vs Hegel - Faith vs Sittlichkeit]] — Can the single individual stand higher than the ethical whole, or is that moral evil to be annulled in *Sittlichkeit*? Hegel's *Philosophy of Right* still reported, not primary. Anchors: [[Thinkers/Søren Kierkegaard]] ↔ [[Thinkers/Hegel]].
- [[Contradictions/Kierkegaard vs Kant - Absolute Duty vs Universal Law]] — Can there be a duty to God that gives love of neighbour the opposite expression to universal law? Kant never answers this book. Third corner vs Mill. Anchors: [[Thinkers/Søren Kierkegaard]] ↔ [[Thinkers/Kant]].
- [[Contradictions/Schopenhauer's Pessimism vs. Leibnizian Optimism]] - Is the universe the worst of all possible worlds driven by an insatiable, suffering Will (Schopenhauer) or the best of all possible worlds structured by sufficient reason (Leibniz)? Anchors: [[Thinkers/Arthur Schopenhauer]] ↔ [[Thinkers/Gottfried Wilhelm Leibniz]].
- [[Contradictions/Early vs Later Wittgenstein]] - A philosopher refuting himself: does language have one hidden **logical form** with meaning as picturing/reference (*Tractatus*, 1922), or is it an open plurality of **language-games** with meaning as **use** and no shared essence (*Philosophical Investigations*, 1953)? Feeds two opposed movements — Vienna Circle/[[Thinkers/A.J. Ayer|Ayer]] (early) vs. ordinary-language philosophy/Austin/[[Thinkers/John Searle|Searle]] (late). Anchors: [[Concepts/Picture Theory of Language (Wittgenstein)]] ↔ [[Concepts/Meaning as Use (Wittgenstein)]] / [[Concepts/Language-Games (Wittgenstein)]].

## How to Use This Index

Grok always consults this index before deep dives. It lists every significant page with a short description so relevant material can be located quickly without full-text search at small-to-medium scale.

When new pages are created or existing pages change substantially:

1. Add or update the entry in the appropriate category.
2. Keep the one-line description accurate.
3. Increment relevant counters in the Statistics section.
4. Add `[[wikilinks]]` from here where helpful.

Users: Browse this page, follow links, or use Obsidian's graph view + quick switcher in parallel. For powerful dynamic views, enable the Dataview plugin and write queries against page frontmatter.

*This index is a living artifact. It will be updated on every significant change to the wiki.*
